## Story & Purpose Auditor

### efigure1.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Four superficially different spectral-estimation schemes (Welch/Hann segments, cosine-taper-then-parabola, sliding sinc, Thomson multitaper) are all quadratic estimators whose eigen-taper bases, eigen-weight ladders, degrees of freedom (ν ≈ 13–17) and leakage kernels are closely comparable — differing mainly in how sharply the weight ladder decays and how much power escapes the band |f| ≤ R.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No —
- The four rows are the backbone of the argument but are only implicitly labelled, via the y-axis text of the leftmost panel. A reader scanning the grid cannot name row 1 ("bank as constructed") vs row 2 ("eigen-tapers of Q") without the caption.
- ν appears as a bare annotation in (i)–(l) with no definition or units; it is arguably the single most decision-relevant number in the figure and is undefined on the graphic.
- The dotted vertical lines in (m)–(p) are unlabelled; R is never shown on the figure.
- Q (the quadratic form whose eigen-tapers row 2 shows) appears nowhere in the figure.
- The half-power widths and out-of-band power fractions — the quantitative punchline — live only in the caption prose, not in the panels.
- Panel (c) is normalised (h/max h) while (a), (b), (d) are not, so row 1 cannot be read as a like-for-like comparison without noticing the axis label change.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — this is a comparison across methods × diagnostic, and a 4×4 small-multiple matrix is the correct choice. Line plots for tapers, a bar ladder for the discrete eigen-weights, and a dB log-magnitude plot for the kernel each match their quantity. Shared x-ranges within rows (0–256; i up to 12; ±25) support column comparison. No mismatch.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel (h) is stated to be identical to panel (d) — a duplicated panel occupying 1/16 of the figure.
- Three separate legends ((a) shifts, (d) i=1–4, (e) i=1–4) encoding at most two distinct mappings; the i=1–4 legend appears twice.
- "k" axis label repeated 8 times, "i" 4 times, "fNΔ" 4 times; y-tick labels repeated across columns where scales are identical.
- Empty x-axis real estate in (i) and (l), where the ladder ends at i = 7 but the axis runs past 10 (defensible for comparability, but the emptiness reads as missing data rather than "no further eigen-weights").
- Horizontal gridlines in (m)–(p) only; inconsistent with the rest of the grid.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the caption opens "Fig. 5 in full," which concedes that some subset of these panels already appears in the main text. That is a legitimate supplement role, but as drafted the reader cannot tell which panels are new. Separately, the caption's numeric list (half-power widths 5.84/8.03/8.03/7.41; leakage 0.05/0.13/1.39/0.22%) plus the four ν values is an eight-number, four-row table hiding in prose — either promote it to a small table or annotate it on the panels, not both.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The caption is an inventory of panel contents with no claim. Nothing tells the reader what to conclude from the comparison. A supplement figure of 16 panels needs a one-sentence verdict in the first line (e.g., "All four banks span nearly the same subspace; they differ only in weight decay and out-of-band leakage").
- [BLOCKING] Row identity is not on the figure. Add left-margin row titles ("Taper bank", "Eigen-tapers of Q", "Eigen-weights", "Kernel").
- [BLOCKING] ν is undefined on the graphic and in the caption; the dotted lines and R are likewise undefined in-figure.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (h) duplicates (d); the annotation "the bank itself, as in (d)" is an admission rather than a fix.
- [ADVISORY] Row 1 mixes normalised (c) and unnormalised (a, b, d) vertical scales, and the four y-ranges all differ, undermining the column comparison the layout invites.
- [ADVISORY] The key quantitative results (half-power width, % out-of-band power) are not visible anywhere in the plot area.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Rewrite line 1 of the caption as the claim, then give construction detail. Keep "Fig. 5 in full" but state explicitly which panels are new relative to Fig. 5.
- Add rotated row titles down the left margin; drop the redundant per-panel y-axis text where the row title carries it.
- Annotate each of (m)–(p) in-panel with its half-power width and out-of-band fraction; label one dotted line "|f| = R/2" in panel (m) only.
- Print ν with a one-line in-figure gloss ("equivalent degrees of freedom") or define it in the caption's first sentence.
- Replace panel (h) with something that earns the slot — e.g. the eigen-taper set overlaid on the ideal Slepian sequences, or the cumulative weight fraction — or grey it out and shrink it, making the "identical by construction" point visually rather than in text.
- Consolidate to a single legend for i = 1–4 (figure-level, top right) and keep the "shifts n = 0, 32, …, 192" annotation in (a).
- Either normalise all of row 1 to max h, or none of it; state the choice in the row title.
- Remove x-axis labels and tick labels from all but the bottom row of each shared-scale block; remove gridlines from (m)–(p) or add them throughout.
- Truncate the i-axis in (i) and (l) to the number of non-zero weights, or add a light "0" baseline marker past i = 7 so the absence reads as measured zero.

### efigure2.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Discarding the leakiest (7th) Slepian taper — or equivalently using adaptive weights or a tapered-then-smoothed periodogram — removes most of the broadband leakage bias (≈10 → ≈4 dB, vs. 18 dB untapered) in the low-power part of an AR(4) spectrum at essentially the same smoothing bandwidth/variance.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the residual panel (b) with direct end-labels is close to self-reading, but three critical things live only in the caption: (i) the degrees of freedom ν for each estimator, which is the entire basis for claiming the comparison is variance-matched and therefore fair; (ii) the shorthand "Untapered, then box" / "Cosine taper, then parabola", which a reader cannot decode without the text; (iii) the quantitative headline numbers (18, 10, 4 dB for f∆ ≥ 0.4), which are stated in prose but not marked on the plot. Additionally, the three blues (L = 7, L = 6, L = 7 adaptive) are not reliably distinguishable at print size, especially where the light-blue band is overdrawn by the navy adaptive trace in (b).

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — overlaid spectral line plots plus a difference (residual) panel is the correct idiom for "estimator vs. truth across frequency," and panel (b) is exactly the right move: it converts a hard visual comparison of near-overlapping curves into a direct read of bias against a zero line. The only structural question is whether panel (a) still earns its space given that (b) carries the claim.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Grey ν = 2 periodogram in (a): consumes the most ink of any element, is not part of the claim (it is not smoothed and does not appear in (b)), and is the main source of visual clutter in the peak region.
- Legend box in (a): duplicates the direct labelling scheme already used successfully in (b); occupies the high-value upper-right area.
- Horizontal gridlines: low value once curves are dense; y-ticks suffice.
- Panel (a)'s peak region (f∆ < 0.2): all estimators coincide there, so ~40% of the panel's width carries no discriminating information.
- Very high-frequency spiky excursions of the adaptive curve in (b) below −5 dB: visually loud, not part of the argument.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panel (a) is largely subsumed by panel (b): every comparison the reader is asked to make (who sits above the truth, and by how much) is easier in (b). Panel (a) survives only as a "here is what the spectrum actually looks like" orientation view. There is also a high chance this extended-data figure repeats the layout of a main-text figure with a different process or N; if so, only the residual panel needs repeating.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The variance-matching that legitimises the comparison (ν = 17.2, 14, 12, 16.7, and frequency-varying for adaptive) is invisible in the graphic. Without it, a sceptical reader can dismiss the whole figure as "of course less leakage, you smoothed more/less."
- [BLOCKING] Three shades of blue for three conceptually different estimators (L = 7, L = 6, adaptive) are not separable at reproduction size; in (b) the navy adaptive trace overplots the light-blue L = 6 band, so the L = 6 result — the figure's central claim — is partially hidden.
- [ADVISORY] The quantitative claims (18 / 10 / 4 dB median over f∆ ≥ 0.4) are asserted in the caption but not extractable from the figure; the reader sees jagged bands, not medians.
- [ADVISORY] Legend terminology ("then box", "then parabola") is unexplained in-figure.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) y-range spans 90 dB to accommodate periodogram spikes, compressing the region where the estimators actually differ.
- [ADVISORY] No indication of which region is "low power" vs "high power", i.e. where leakage is expected to matter.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Annotate ν next to each curve's direct label (e.g. "Multitaper, L = 7 (ν = 14)"), and for the adaptive curve write "ν = 14 → 6.5". This single change converts the figure from an assertion into an argument.
- Re-colour: keep one hue family only for the multitaper family if you must, but differentiate by line style as well (solid / dashed / dash-dot), and draw in order light→dark→adaptive-on-top-thin so nothing is occluded; or give the adaptive estimator its own hue.
- In (b), overlay a thick median-bias line per estimator on top of the thin raw residual, and mark the f∆ ≥ 0.4 band with light shading plus the three numbers (18, 10, 4 dB) as in-plot annotations at the right edge.
- Delete the legend in (a) and direct-label there too, matching (b); this frees the upper-right region.
- Consider demoting (a) to a smaller inset or removing the grey periodogram from it; if (a) stays, clip its y-range (e.g. −30 to 50) and note "periodogram clipped".
- Drop the horizontal gridlines in both panels.
- If a main-text figure already uses this layout, keep only panel (b) here.

### efigure3.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** At a matched design resolution, all five taper-then-smoother recipes produce essentially the same main lobe (panel a), but their out-of-band skirts differ by roughly 50 dB, with Bohman-then-box leaking least and untapered-then-box leaking most (panel b).

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No (close, but not quite) — The two-panel linear/dB pairing is immediately legible and the direct end-labels in (b) are excellent. Three things stop it from standing alone:
- The figure's headline number (the ~51 dB spread at |f| = 12/(NΔ)) exists only in the caption; nothing on the plot marks that abscissa or the two bracketing levels.
- The equivalent degrees of freedom (ν = 7.3 … 17.2) are the variance side of the bias–variance trade-off being illustrated, and they appear nowhere on the figure. Without them the reader sees "Bohman wins on every axis," which is not the intended message.
- "Ideal box of width R" is plotted but R is never given a value on the axes; the reader must infer R = 8/(NΔ) from the dotted edges at fNΔ = ±4.
- In (b) the dotted "ideal box" renders as two isolated vertical lines with a short top segment; a reader may mistake these for reference markers rather than the box transfer function (which is −∞ dB outside). This reads as an artifact.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a comparison of functional shapes, so overlaid line curves are correct, and splitting into linear (main-lobe agreement) and dB (skirt decay) is the standard and right choice: each panel does a job the other cannot. Axis ranges are sensibly different per panel (±10 vs ±24).

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- The legend box in (a) duplicates the direct labels in (b) for the same five curves — one labelling scheme should serve both panels.
- All curves are even in f, so the left half of both panels is pure mirror duplication. Roughly half the ink carries no information.
- Faint horizontal gridlines in (a) and (b) add little given that no precise value read-off is being requested.
- The top horizontal dotted segment of the box in (b), which sits on the 0 dB line and is largely hidden under the other curves.
- The flat near-zero tails in (a) beyond |fNΔ| ≈ 7, which convey nothing the dB panel does not convey better.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Low — Table 2 is explicitly cross-referenced and presumably carries N, R, ν and half-power widths, so the numeric caption content is duplicated there; but the shape information (ripple, shoulder rounding, skirt slope) is genuinely visual and cannot be tabulated. Panel (a) is the weaker half: its entire content is "the main lobes agree to within a few percent, except the multitaper ripples," which could be compressed into one sentence plus the half-power-width column of Table 2. If space is tight, (a) is the supplement candidate, not the whole figure.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The quantitative claim in the caption (51 dB spread at |f| = 12/(NΔ)) is not visible in the figure. A reader must measure it by eye against an unmarked abscissa.
- [BLOCKING] The variance cost (ν) is absent from the figure, so the figure as drawn supports a conclusion ("use Bohman") stronger than the data warrant. The trade-off is the point; show both sides.
- [BLOCKING] The multitaper curve is not at matched resolution (7.4 vs 8.0 /(NΔ)) and visibly exceeds the others in peak height in (a). Nothing on the figure flags that this one curve is on slightly different footing, which undermines the "same design resolution" premise stated in the caption.
- [ADVISORY] "recipe (b′)" is an internal cross-reference that will be opaque to anyone reading the figure out of order; the caption should name the recipe rather than index it.
- [ADVISORY] Legend ordering in (a) (Multitaper, Untapered, Cosine, Hann, Bohman) matches neither the visual top-to-bottom order in (b) nor any performance ranking.
- [ADVISORY] The dotted box in (b) is visually ambiguous (see above).
- [ADVISORY] Bohman is the only dashed curve; if the distinction is needed because light blue and blue are confusable, consider changing the hue instead so line style does not imply a different kind of object.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a light vertical rule at fNΔ = 12 in (b) and annotate the two extreme intercepts (−24 dB, −75 dB) with a brace or a "51 dB" label. This converts the caption's key number into a visual fact.
- Append ν to each direct label in (b), e.g. "Bohman, then box (ν = 7.3)". This puts the trade-off on the page at zero extra ink.
- Delete the legend in (a) and rely on the direct labels in (b), with a one-line caption note that colours are shared across panels. If (a) needs its own cue, label the two or three curves that actually differ there (multitaper ripple, cosine shoulder) in place.
- Plot only f ≥ 0 in both panels and state the symmetry in the caption; reinvest the freed width in vertical extent so the dB decay slopes separate more clearly.
- In (b), draw the ideal box as a single short horizontal segment at 0 dB spanning |fNΔ| ≤ 4 with no verticals, or drop it entirely from (b) since it is uninformative on a log scale.
- Either (i) re-tune the multitaper design so its half-power width is 8.0/(NΔ) like the rest, or (ii) mark it explicitly on the figure (e.g. an asterisk in its label) and say in one clause why exact matching is not achievable for the L = 7 case.
- Trim (a)'s x-range to about ±7 (or 0–7 after folding) so the main-lobe comparison fills the panel.
- Reorder labels and legend by ν or by skirt level so the ranking is readable without decoding.
- Replace "recipe (b′)" with the descriptive name, keeping the cross-reference in parentheses.

### efigure4.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** *Probably*: "At matched spectral resolution, the nine estimators trade variance against leakage/side-lobe suppression — Welch buys the most degrees of freedom but leaks the most, the Hann/Bohman-then-box smoothers leak least but gain the fewest degrees of freedom, and multitapers sit in between." The figure supports this, but it does not *assert* it: the reader must mentally join three panels by line colour to see the trade-off, so as drawn the claim reads more like "here are three diagnostics for nine methods versus resolution."

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — close, but three gaps. (1) The ordinate of (b) is a ratio whose normalisation is never stated on the figure ("fraction of total kernel power"?), and (c)'s "dB" has no stated reference (the caption says "relative to its maximum"). (2) $N=256$, the key experimental condition, appears only in the caption. (3) The curves terminate at very different $x$ values (Hann-then-Gaussian ends at ~9.5, multitapers at ~16, Welch at ~24.5) with no on-figure explanation — a reader will wonder whether the method failed, was not computed, or hit a parameter limit. Positives worth keeping: fully spelled-out axis labels, the in-panel annotations for the dotted line and the filled markers, and the legend with descriptive method names — these do a lot of the caption's work already.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate (with one addition recommended) — the purpose is establishing relationships under a swept parameter, and small-multiple line plots against a shared abscissa is the right choice; plotting everything against *measured* half-power width rather than the nominal bandwidth parameter is the correct design decision and is the figure's main methodological virtue. However, if the intended claim is the *trade-off*, the most direct chart is missing: a Pareto-style scatter of panel (a) versus panel (b) (EDOF on one axis, leakage on the other, resolution encoded by marker size or annotated along each method's trajectory) would show the frontier in one view instead of requiring cross-panel colour matching.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Four series rendered in near-identical blues (multitaper $2\alpha-1$, $2\alpha-2$, sine tapers, and Bohman-then-box), separated only by dash pattern and marker; they cross repeatedly in (a) and (c).
- Horizontal gridlines in all three panels contribute little at this data density.
- Markers at every swept point (some curves have 8–10) — in (a) several curves collapse onto one another and the marker clutter obscures the fact that they *are* coincident.
- Empty lower-right quadrant used only for the legend (defensible, but it costs ~25% of the figure area).
- In (a), the curves for untapered/cosine/multitaper-$2\alpha-1$/Hann-then-Gaussian are essentially the same line; plotting all four at full weight spends ink on a redundancy that could be stated once.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the dotted line explicitly marks the single width ($8/N\Delta$) tabulated in Table 2, so the figure's vertical slice at $x=8$ duplicates that table. The figure earns its place by showing that the ranking is *stable* (or not) across resolutions, but that message is never stated, so a sceptical reader could ask why the table isn't sufficient. Also check against any companion figure showing the kernels themselves; panels (b) and (c) are summary statistics of those kernels.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (b) and (c) ordinates are not interpretable without the caption: (b) lacks its normalisation, (c) lacks its dB reference.
- [BLOCKING] Colour/style encoding is not separable at print size — four blue series that overlap and cross cannot be traced reliably, which breaks the cross-panel comparison the figure depends on.
- [BLOCKING] Unequal and unexplained $x$-extents of the curves; the reader cannot tell whether a short curve reflects a method limitation or an arbitrary sweep choice.
- [ADVISORY] The trade-off — the actual scientific point — is left for the reader to assemble; no panel states it.
- [ADVISORY] $N=256$ and "exact computation, no sampling variability" live only in the caption; both are load-bearing.
- [ADVISORY] The "$8/(N\Delta)$, the width in Table 2" annotation appears only in (a) although the dotted line appears in all three panels.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) has no reference for what is achievable: the reader cannot judge whether $\nu \approx 30$ at width 16 is good.
- [ADVISORY] Legend order does not follow any visible ranking (e.g. by leakage), making lookup slower than necessary.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Rewrite the ordinates to be self-contained: "Fraction of kernel power beyond twice the half-power half-width" and "Peak side-lobe level beyond $1.5\times$ the half-power half-width (dB below kernel maximum)".
- Recolour: give the three multitaper variants one hue with three line styles, and move Bohman-then-box to a clearly distinct hue (it currently reads as a fourth multitaper). Consider direct end-of-line labels for the 3–4 curves that matter most and de-emphasise the rest to thin grey.
- Extend all sweeps to a common $x$ range, or add a one-clause on-figure note explaining why some methods stop early (e.g. "Gaussian smoother not defined beyond ...").
- Add the claim to the figure: either annotate the extremes in (a)/(b) ("most degrees of freedom, most leakage" / "least leakage, fewest degrees of freedom"), or replace the empty quadrant with a fourth panel plotting EDOF against leakage at fixed resolution to show the frontier directly, with the legend moved into a panel margin.
- Put $N=256$ in a corner of panel (a) and repeat the "$8/(N\Delta)$, Table 2" tick label (or just a small "Table 2" tick) in (b) and (c).
- In (a), add a light reference line for the ideal $\nu = 2\,\times$(width$\times N\Delta$) so the reader can see how far each method falls short, and state once in the caption that four methods are coincident rather than drawing four full-weight overlapping curves.
- Drop the horizontal gridlines; thin the markers where curves have many points.
- Since this is already an extended-data figure, keep it there and make Table 2 the in-text artefact — but add one caption sentence asserting what the sweep adds beyond the table (e.g. whether the ranking is resolution-independent).

### figure1.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: Three apparently different PSD estimators — eigenvalue-weighted multitaper with all N Slepians, box-smoothed untapered periodogram, and the average of sliding sinc-window periodograms — give the same spectrum, the first two to machine precision and the third to O(1/N_s).

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — Panel (a)'s legend header ("Three estimates that coincide") and panel (b)'s in-plot annotations do most of the work, which is good practice. But three gaps force the reader to the caption: (i) panel (c) never states *what* the difference is taken against (multitaper) or *which* estimator it refers to (sliding window); (ii) the symbols R, λ_i, N, N_s, α, ν are undefined in the graphic; (iii) panel (a) cannot actually be read as evidence of coincidence — at this line weight the dashed and dotted greens are indistinguishable from a single plotted trace, so a skeptical reader cannot verify overlap versus omission.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — (a) overlay to show identity, (b) log-scale difference traces to quantify agreement, (c) log-log convergence with a power-law guide. All three match their purposes. One caveat: (a) is an "identity" claim, and overlays are the weakest way to show identity (perfect agreement looks the same as a plotting bug). A zoom inset over a 2–3 Hz window, or offsetting the three traces slightly with explicit markers at subsampled frequencies, would make the overlay verifiable.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- The full 0–40 Hz spectral detail in (a): the many peaks are irrelevant to the claim (the data are arbitrary de-identified EEG), yet they occupy ~55% of the figure's area. A shorter panel or a narrower frequency span would suffice.
- Horizontal gridlines in (a) and (c) — no value is being read off these axes.
- The five decades of empty space in (b) between the two difference traces; the y-range is necessary but the whitespace invites compression (e.g., a broken axis) or is itself part of the message and should be labelled as such.
- (a)'s legend label "Sliding sinc window, N_s = 16N" duplicates information that (c) already parameterises.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — panel (b)'s dark-green trace is essentially the single rightmost point of panel (c) shown frequency-by-frequency; and panel (a) contributes no quantitative information that (b) does not state more precisely. If space is tight, (a) is the supplement candidate, with (b) and (c) carrying the argument. Conversely, the two numbers quoted in the caption (3×10⁻¹³ dB, 0.06 dB) are the entire content of (b) — consider whether (b) could be a one-line statement in text plus (c).

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (c) lacks a stated reference: "Max. |difference|" between what and what? Add "vs. multitaper" to the axis label or an in-plot annotation, as done in (b).
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (a) does not visually support its own legend header. As drawn, coincidence is asserted, not shown. Add a zoom inset or sparse markers so the reader can see three traces lying on top of one another.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption/axis mismatch: the caption says (c) plots against "window length N_s" but the axis is N_s/N.
  - [ADVISORY] Symbols R, α, ν, λ_i appear in the legend/caption but are defined only elsewhere in the text; ν = 17 is mentioned in the caption but is not shown anywhere in the figure and does not support the claim.
  - [ADVISORY] Colour encoding is doing double duty: "the two greens mark one estimator reached by two routes" is a subtle and important point that is explained only in the caption. It should be visible in the legend.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) consumes the most area but carries the least information; the panel hierarchy inverts the argument's weight.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Relabel (c)'s y-axis "Max. |difference| from multitaper (dB)" and annotate the trace "Sliding sinc window".
  - Add a small zoom inset to (a) over a ~2 Hz band around a peak, with markers, so the three-way overlap is demonstrable.
  - Restructure the legend in (a) as two grouped entries: "Multitaper (all N tapers, eigenvalue-weighted)" and "Same estimator, two routes: box-smoothed periodogram / sliding sinc window", making the identity claim explicit in the graphic.
  - Shrink (a) to roughly one third of the figure height (or restrict to 0–20 Hz) and give the reclaimed space to (b) and (c), which carry the quantitative claim.
  - Annotate (b) with the two agreement levels directly on the plot ("≈3×10⁻¹³ dB: machine rounding", "≈0.06 dB") so the caption's numbers live in the figure.
  - Remove gridlines; define R (and N_s) with a short inline gloss in the legend, e.g. "box of width R = 1 Hz".
  - Consider merging (b) and (c) into a single panel, or moving (a) to the supplement, to reduce the three-panel footprint to the two panels that carry the evidence.

### figure2.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Every spectral estimator is the true PSD convolved with a smoothing kernel (plus noise), so the choice of taper and smoothing width trades variance for bias — and the best choice is a kernel about as wide as the spectral feature you want to resolve.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visual architecture (4 methods × [estimate, kernel]) reads immediately, and the in-panel annotations (method name, ν, "True PSD") carry a lot of the load. But three things that the claim depends on are caption-only: (i) the half-power widths and broadband-leakage percentages, (ii) the fact that the (h) inset spans 0.08/Δ while (e–g) span 0.02/Δ, and (iii) the verdict per column ("too narrow / just right / too wide"). A reader scanning the figure will likely conclude that (h)'s main lobe is comparable to (g)'s, which is the opposite of the point. The convention that unresolvable side lobes are drawn as an envelope is also invisible.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — paired small multiples of dB-scale line plots are the right vehicle for "same data, four processing choices," and the two-row layout enforces the estimate↔kernel correspondence that is the figure's conceptual core. One caveat: the figure asks the reader to compare skirt decay and main-lobe width *across* panels, which small multiples do poorly. A fifth panel (or an inset) overlaying all four kernels on common axes would make the 22 dB / 39 dB skirt improvement and the 0.8→47.8/(NΔ) width progression directly visible instead of inferred from four separate frames.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Repeated y-axis tick labels on panels (b), (c), (d) and (f), (g), (h) — axes are shared; ticks alone suffice.
- Horizontal gridlines in all eight panels; at dB scale they add little and compete with the fine structure of the periodogram and side lobes.
- Panel (a) and (b) are visually near-identical at this vertical scale; the taper's benefit is essentially invisible in the top row, so (b)'s top panel is doing less work than its footprint suggests.
- Inset frame + leader lines are justified, but the double-boxed appearance in (e–h) is heavier than needed.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the caption's numeric series (half-power widths 0.8/1.2/8.2/47.8, leakage 1.3%/0.02%/0.12%/65%, ν values) is a four-column table masquerading as prose. It is neither readable from the figure nor efficiently readable from the caption. Low risk of duplicating another figure, but (a) vs (b) top panels are internally redundant.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The (h) inset uses a 4× wider x-span than (e–g) with no visual flag, inviting the reader to misjudge the main-lobe width — precisely the quantity the "wider kernel merges the peaks" claim rests on.
- [BLOCKING] The figure's quantitative argument (kernel width vs. peak width vs. peak separation; fraction of leaked power) is not encodable anywhere in the graphic. Without the caption, the reader cannot tell *why* (g) is preferred over (c)'s neighbour or over (h).
- [ADVISORY] "True PSD" floats in the lower left of (a) with no leader line to the black curve, and the black/orange pairing in (c)–(d) is never re-identified.
- [ADVISORY] The |f| ≤ 8/(NΔ) band that defines "broadband leakage" is a stated measure with no visual referent in (e–h).
- [ADVISORY] The envelope-vs-side-lobe drawing convention is a silent data transformation.
- [ADVISORY] Caption mixes specification, results, and interpretation; the last three sentences are discussion text.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Either put all four insets on the same 0.08/Δ span, or add an explicit "note wider span" tag plus a scale bar inside (h)'s inset; a shaded rectangle in (h)'s inset marking the 0.02/Δ span used in (e–g) would fix this in one stroke.
- Annotate each kernel panel with its half-power width as a horizontal bar at −3 dB, and shade the |f| ≤ 8/(NΔ) region; the leakage percentage can then sit as a small number outside the shading. This moves the argument from caption into ink.
- Add a one-line verdict above each column — "leaks", "leaks less", "matched to peak", "over-smoothed" — so the narrative is legible in a single scan.
- Draw a horizontal bar in one top panel showing the peak width 7/(NΔ) and the separation 30/(NΔ), so R = 8 and R = 48 acquire meaning without arithmetic.
- Add leader lines from "True PSD" to the black curve in (a); repeat a minimal legend or a short black tick-label in (c)/(d).
- Drop duplicated y-tick labels and gridlines; consider a small overlay panel of all four kernels for direct cross-method comparison.
- Move the numeric series (ν, half-power width, leakage %) into a compact three-row table or into annotations, and cut the interpretive final sentences from the caption to the body text.

### figure3.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: There is a broad optimum in smoothing bandwidth where the falling noise and rising bias of the smoothed periodogram trade off to minimize RMS error, and that optimum shifts to smaller bandwidth when only the spectral peak region is scored.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — Panel (b) is close to self-reading: the labeled Bias/Noise/Total curves, the dual $R$–$\nu$ axes, and the "too noisy / just right / too smooth" annotations carry the argument without text. Three things break it:
  1. The circled points are only meaningful by cross-reference to another figure's panels; as drawn they look like distinguished data points with no on-figure explanation.
  2. Panel (a) has no stated purpose. A reader cannot tell from the visualization why a taper illustration precedes a bias–variance curve; the two panels do not share an axis, a quantity, or an obvious logical link.
  3. In panel (a), the dashed taper is plotted against the $x_k$ amplitude axis after being rescaled to the record's peak. The taper is a dimensionless 0–1 weighting; putting it on a data-amplitude axis implies it has units of $x_k$ and makes its plateau height (~68) look like a meaningful number.

CHART TYPE: Mismatch (partial) — Panel (b) is a relationship/tradeoff plot and the line plot with decomposed components is exactly right; keep it. Panel (a) is a process-explanation panel bolted onto a quantitative-relationship figure. Either (i) move it to the figure where the record and the tapering step are first introduced (the "motif" figure), or (ii) if tapering must be shown here, reduce it to a small inset showing only the taper shape on a 0–1 axis, not the rescaled overlay on the record.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
  - Horizontal gridlines in panel (b): faint but unused — no value is read off the $y$-axis precisely; the annotations do that work.
  - The rescaled dashed taper envelope in panel (a) (both upper and lower mirrored copies), which adds ink without adding information beyond "a taper tapers."
  - Leader line for "just right, $\nu=16.7$" crosses the dashed peak-region curve, creating a false intersection.
  - Redundant encoding in panel (b): solid+filled-circle vs dashed+open-square vs direct labels. Direct labels alone suffice; the marker distinction could go.
  - The full black record in panel (a) is largely hidden behind the orange tapered record except at the ends; most of the black ink is not visible information.

REDUNDANCY RISK: High — Panel (a) appears to replot the same record already shown in Fig.~\ref{fig:motif}(a), and the three circled points in panel (b) are explicitly stated to be panels (b)–(d) of that same figure. This figure therefore re-presents two elements of an earlier figure. Panel (a) is a strong candidate for deletion or for merging into the motif figure; panel (b) is the novel content and should stand alone as the figure.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (a) has no demonstrated relation to the figure's claim and duplicates an earlier figure; as composed, the figure has two claims and therefore no single claim.
  - [BLOCKING] The taper in panel (a) is plotted on the $x_k$ amplitude axis after arbitrary rescaling, which misrepresents a dimensionless weighting function as a data-valued curve.
  - [ADVISORY] The circled points are uninterpretable without the caption's cross-reference.
  - [ADVISORY] "Noise" is defined in the caption as the standard deviation of the estimate while the $y$-axis is "RMS error (dB)"; the reader cannot tell whether the three curves are commensurable (do Bias and Noise add in quadrature to Total in these units?).
  - [ADVISORY] 300 realizations, no uncertainty band. The minimum is explicitly described as "broad," which is the claim — but the reader has no way to judge how broad relative to sampling scatter.
  - [ADVISORY] The two dotted vertical lines ("peak width," "peak separation") are asserted as reference scales but their derivation is invisible; they read as decoration unless the claim is that the optimum sits near the peak width.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Promote panel (b) to be the whole figure. Move panel (a) into the motif figure where the record and taper are introduced, or drop it.
  - If panel (a) is retained anywhere, give the taper its own right-hand axis from 0 to 1, drop the mirrored lower envelope, and show only the top half.
  - Annotate the circled points in place with their roles rather than by cross-reference, e.g. "(b) $\nu=2$", "(c) $\nu=16.7$", "(d) $\nu=98.4$" beside each circle, so the tie to the motif figure is readable on the page.
  - Add a light shaded band (e.g. $\pm$ one standard error over the 300 realizations) to the solid Total curve so "broad minimum" is visually earned rather than asserted.
  - Remove the horizontal gridlines; reroute the "just right" leader so it does not cross the dashed curve.
  - State in the axis label or a short in-plot note how Bias and Noise combine into Total, so the decomposition is self-evident.
  - Consider marking the optimum of each Total curve with a small tick on the axis, which would make the "optimum shifts left for the peak region" claim immediate rather than inferred from curve shapes.

### figure4.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** Summing the eigenvalue-weighted Slepian kernels over tapers builds up a spectral window that is (essentially exactly) a box of width $R$, so the multitaper estimator smooths the spectrum with a box — and tapers beyond the $2\alpha$th contribute only leakage.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visual logic of (a)→(b)→(c) ("individual tapers → individual kernels fill in gaps → sum is a box") is readable without text, and the $\pm R/2$ dotted lines and the dashed "Box" reference are well annotated. But four things break without the caption: (i) nothing on the figure says these are *Slepian/DPSS* tapers or gives $N$, $R$, $\alpha$; (ii) panel (d)'s $1-\lambda_i$ is not identified as "energy outside the band", so the reader cannot tell that a *low* value is good; (iii) the y-axis in (c) is labelled $\mathcal{K}(f)/N\Delta$ but the caption defines the plotted quantity as $\sum_{i\le L}\lambda_i|H^{(i)}|^2/2\alpha\Delta$ — the reader must reconcile two different normalizations; (iv) in (b) the axis reads $|H^{(i)}(f)|^2/N\Delta^2$ while the caption says "$|H^{(i)}(f)|^2/\Delta$ in units of $N\Delta$". The normalization story must be identical on-axis and in-caption or panel (c)'s "top lowered by 3%" claim is uncheckable.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — (a)–(c) are functions of a continuous variable, so overlaid line plots are correct; (c)'s family-of-curves-converging-to-a-reference is exactly the right idiom for "adding tapers builds the box"; (d)'s log-scale line-with-markers correctly shows the ~10 orders of magnitude fall/plateau in concentration. One refinement: the key quantitative claims in (c) (rounded edges over $1/(N\Delta)$, 3% top reduction, ripple at $L=7$) are sub-pixel at this scale — an inset zoom on one shoulder and on the plateau would make them visible rather than asserted.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel (a) y-range $\pm0.20$ when data span $\approx\pm0.12$: ~40% dead ink.
- Panels (b) and (c): the $|fN\Delta|>5$ regions in (b) are entirely flat zero — the band could be truncated to $\pm5$ (in (c) the out-of-band floor is informative, so keep it).
- Horizontal gridlines in all four panels add no readability for smooth curves; the $\pm R/2$ dotted lines and the box are the only rules that carry meaning.
- Redundant legends: (a) and (b) both encode "taper index, dark→light" in separate legends; one shared colour legend would do.
- **Colour-direction conflict:** in (a)/(b) dark = low index; in (c) dark = *large* $L$. Same palette, reversed meaning, three panels apart — this is noise that actively misleads.
- Panel (d) markers on every integer $i$ out to 17 where the curve is flat; the plateau is made by ~8 of them.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panels (a) and (d) are standard DPSS textbook content (taper shapes; eigenvalue ladder) and carry no new result; (d) in particular is a 17-number monotone sequence that a table or a single sentence ("$1-\lambda_i<10^{-4}$ for $i\le5$, $O(1)$ for $i\ge9$") would convey. The paper's actual novelty lives in (b)+(c). If space is tight, (a) and (d) are the supplement candidates; (b)+(c) alone tell the claim.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Normalization mismatch between axis labels and caption in both (b) and (c). The reader cannot verify the "3% lower top" or "exactly a box" claims without knowing which scaling is plotted.
- [BLOCKING] Colour semantics reverse between (a)/(b) (dark = first taper) and (c) (dark = most tapers) with no on-figure cue.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (d) y-axis gives no indication that smaller is better, or that the plateau means "pure leakage"; the two vertical lines ($L=2\alpha-1$, $2\alpha$) are labelled but their significance (cutoff choice) is not.
- [ADVISORY] Why the *seventh* taper in (a) is unmotivated on-figure; it is presumably $2\alpha-1$, the conventional last usable taper, which is worth stating in the annotation.
- [ADVISORY] The quantitative claims about (c) (edge rounding scale, 3% droop, ripple amplitude) are not resolvable at printed size.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) is in sample index $k$ while (b)–(d) are in dimensionless frequency $fN\Delta$ / index $i$; the time↔frequency transition is unlabelled.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Make the plotted quantity and its normalization identical in the axis label and the caption for (b) and (c); if (c) is the box-height-normalized running sum, state that the flat level should be $\mathcal{K}=1/R$ and mark that level with a thin reference line so "exactly a box" is visually falsifiable.
- Fix the colour direction: use dark→light for increasing taper index everywhere, and in (c) distinguish $L$ by a single-hue *sequential* ramp in the same direction, keeping black solid only for $L=N$ and black dashed only for the ideal box.
- Add in-panel text annotations instead of relying on the caption: "Slepian tapers, $N=256$, $\alpha=4$" in (a); "each kernel fills the gaps of the previous" arrow in (b); "$L=2\alpha-1=7$: rounded shoulders + ripple" pointing at that curve in (c); "energy outside $|f|\le R/2$ — smaller is better" and "leakage-only tapers" over the plateau in (d).
- Add an inset to (c) zooming the plateau ($|fN\Delta|<3$, y-range 0.118–0.126) and one shoulder, so the 3% droop, the ripple, and the $1/(N\Delta)$ edge width are visible.
- Tighten panel (a) to the data range, truncate (b) to $|fN\Delta|\le5$, drop horizontal gridlines, merge the (a)/(b) legends into one shared taper-index key.
- Consider demoting (a) and (d) to the supplement and enlarging (b)+(c) to a two-panel main figure; the claim in your one-sentence summary is carried entirely by those two.
---

### figure5.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Four superficially different spectral estimators (Welch, cosine-taper + parabolic smoothing, sliding sinc window, and Thomson multitaper) are all equivalent to a bank of tapers built from a single window, and the flatness of each method's eigen-weight ladder sets its equivalent degrees of freedom ν (13.4–17.2).

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the column structure and clean titles get you most of the way, but three things break without the caption: (i) nothing in the figure states that the bottom row belongs to the same method as the panel directly above it (colour matching implies it, but it is never declared); (ii) ν is annotated but never defined, so the reader cannot tell whether larger is better or what quantity it measures; (iii) the top-row y-axes are four *different* normalizations (h_{k−n}, h_k·cos(...), h/max h, h^{(i)}_k), so the apparent amplitude differences across panels are artefacts the reader will over-interpret. Panel (c) also shows four curves with no legend, and (d) shows four curves labelled i = 1…4 while the title says L = 7, which reads as a contradiction until the caption explains "the first four shown."

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — line plots for continuous taper shapes and bar charts for the discrete weight ladder are both correct choices, and the small-multiple grid (method × property) is the right structure for a "these are all the same object" argument. The consistent x-range (0–256) in the top row and the consistent i-range (to ~12) and y-range (0–0.20) in the bottom row are exactly what makes the comparison readable; keep them.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Repeated y-tick labels on (f), (g), (h) — the axes are shared with (e); one labelled axis suffices.
- Repeated "k" and "i" x-axis labels on every panel; one per row (or a single centred label) is enough.
- The 0.10 tick and extra headroom in (b) inflate the panel relative to its data range.
- Empty x-range beyond i = 7 in (e) and (h) is *not* noise (it enables comparison) but should be visually acknowledged, e.g. a faint baseline, so it does not read as missing data.
- Inconsistent in-panel annotation style: (a) and (c) use a text note, (b) and (d) use a legend. Pick one per row.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Low — the four ν values could be a one-line table, but the *shape* of the ladder (fast decay vs. flat plateau) is the visual argument and cannot be tabulated compactly. Panels (a–d) are not duplicated elsewhere given that the eigen-tapers and kernels are correctly deferred to Fig. S1. The one candidate for trimming is panel (c)'s top plot: its point ("a sinc window slid along the record") is largely carried by the caption's limit statement and by (g), not by the curve shapes.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The figure's organizing logic — three *routes* to a taper bank plus one *reference* bank — is stated only in the caption. Nothing on the canvas distinguishes (d)/(h) as the reference against which the other three are being judged, which is the whole point of the comparison.
- [BLOCKING] ν is the punchline quantity and is undefined in-figure; a reader scanning panels (e–h) cannot know that flatter ladder → larger ν → lower variance.
- [BLOCKING] Four different y-axis normalizations in the top row silently prevent the cross-panel comparison the layout invites.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (c) lacks a legend identifying which curve sits at which centre (k = −128, 0, 128, 256); two of the four are clipped at the panel edges and look like different functions.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (d): "Multitaper, L = 7" over four plotted curves invites a miscount.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b)'s y-axis label h_k cos(2πf′kΔ) is a formula, not a label; it forces the reader into the methods section mid-scan.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add two row labels on the left margin ("Taper bank" / "Eigen-weights c_i") and a single method name spanning each column, with the fourth column visually set apart (e.g. a light background band or a "reference" tag) so the three-routes-plus-reference structure is visible at a glance.
- Print the definition ν = 2(Σc_i)²/Σc_i² once, as a small annotation over the bottom row, next to the ν values; consider ordering or annotating the bottom row so the flat-ladder → high-ν trend is explicit (e.g. a short arrow or "flatter ⇒ larger ν" note).
- Normalize all four top-row tapers the same way (unit energy is the natural choice) and use a single shared y-axis label such as "taper amplitude"; move the exact expressions to the caption.
- Add a four-entry legend to (c) matching the shade ramp to the four centres, mirroring (b) and (d); or drop (c)'s top panel to the supplement and let (g) carry the sliding-sinc case.
- Change (d)'s title to "Multitaper, L = 7 (first 4 shown)".
- Strip duplicate tick labels and axis titles in the bottom row; keep one shared y-label and one x-label per row.
- Consider annotating each bottom panel with the effective number of contributing weights (or drawing a horizontal line at 1/ν-equivalent height) so the "ladder flatness" claim is measurable from the plot rather than inferred.

### figure6.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: At matched spectral resolution, the choice of estimator determines leakage: the untapered smoothed periodogram (and, less severely, the L=7 multitaper) sits tens of dB above the true PSD in the low-power band because its smoothing kernel has heavy tails/side lobes, whereas a cosine-tapered periodogram smoothed with a parabola and Welch's method track the true spectrum closely.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — The visual logic (bad estimate above → bad kernel below) is legible, and the 22 dB / 14 dB arrows are effective, but several load-bearing elements are caption-only: (i) ν is printed in every panel and never defined or explained in the figure; (ii) "True PSD" is labelled only in (a), so in (b)–(d) the black curve is unidentified; (iii) 𝒦(f) is never identified as the smoothing/spectral-window kernel, and the relationship between rows is not signposted; (iv) the half-power-width matching — the entire basis for the comparison being fair — is invisible: the insets show the kernel tops but nothing marks the common width or the band |f| ≤ R; (v) the quantitative punchline (median absolute errors 11.6 / 4.6 / 0.9 / 0.8 dB) exists only in prose; (vi) panels (c) and (d) have no annotation at all, so the reader must infer "no offset" from absence.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — This is a comparison of four methods plus a mechanistic explanation, and the 4-column small-multiple with shared axes and a paired diagnostic row is the right form. The insets are the right tool for resolving the near-peak kernel structure. One caveat: the top row shows a single realization, which is a weak vehicle for a *comparison* claim; a faint ensemble envelope or median behind each colored curve would let the same panels carry both the anecdote and the statistic.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Repeated y-axis tick labels in all four columns of both rows; only the leftmost panel needs them (this is the largest single ink saving).
- Repeated inset axis tick labels (0.39/0.4/0.41 and 0, −25, −50) in all four insets.
- Horizontal gridlines in the top row compete with the data and are not used for any readout.
- The −150 dB region of (e)–(h) is mostly empty for (e) and (f); the shared range is defensible but the lower quarter carries almost no information.
- Inset connector boxes/leader lines are drawn heavier than needed.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — The figure itself is not duplicated, but (a) the caption's per-estimator error numbers explicitly duplicate Table~\ref{tab:ensemble}, and (b) the kernel row is described as "as in Fig.~\ref{fig:motif}", so some kernel material is already established earlier. If Fig. motif already makes the kernel-tails-cause-leakage point, panels (e)–(h) could shrink (or two of them move to the supplement) and this figure could concentrate on the four-way estimate comparison.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] The fairness premise of the comparison — equal half-power width — is stated only in the caption and cannot be verified or even seen in the figure. Without a visual marker the reader cannot rule out that (c) and (d) simply smooth harder.
  - [BLOCKING] The caption concedes "This realization has more leakage than is typical," and then gives ensemble numbers (5.3 / 2.3 / 1.0 / 1.0 dB) that differ substantially from this realization's (11.6 / 4.6 / 0.9 / 0.8 dB). The figure therefore overstates the (a)/(b) failure relative to the paper's own evidence. A figure should not need its caption to walk back its visual impression.
  - [ADVISORY] ν appears eight times and is never defined in the figure; readers who skim will not know whether higher or lower is better.
  - [ADVISORY] "True PSD" identified only in panel (a); black curve unlabelled in (b)–(d).
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (c),(d) lack any annotation parallel to the 22 dB / 14 dB arrows, so the good cases read as "nothing to see" rather than as a measured result.
  - [ADVISORY] The band |f| ≤ R, which defines the "power outside" percentages quoted in the caption, is not drawn on (e)–(h).
  - [ADVISORY] Color is the only thing distinguishing the four estimates and is not reused elsewhere in the figure (e.g., kernels are all black), so the color encoding does nothing the panel letters don't already do.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Draw the half-power width on each kernel inset (e.g., a horizontal bar at −3 dB with its width annotated, or a shared dashed reference width repeated in all four insets) so the matched-resolution premise is visible.
  - Add a faint ensemble band (e.g., 10th–90th percentile over the 300 realizations) or the ensemble median behind each colored curve in (a)–(d); keep the single realization on top. This removes the need for the caption's disclaimer and makes the claim ensemble-level.
  - Annotate (c) and (d) with their own leakage figures (e.g., "<1 dB" with the same arrow style) so all four panels are read on one scale.
  - Print the median absolute error (this realization and ensemble) as a small annotation in each of (a)–(d), and cut those numbers from the caption; keep the table for the full breakdown.
  - Label the black curve "true PSD" in every top panel, or place a compact shared legend once at the figure level.
  - Define ν in the axis/legend region (e.g., "ν = equivalent degrees of freedom") or drop it from the panels and report it only in the table.
  - Remove y-tick labels from columns 2–4 in both rows and from insets 2–4; delete the top-row gridlines. Consider truncating the kernel y-range to −140 dB.
  - Shade or bracket |f| ≤ R in (e)–(h) so the "power outside the band" percentages are anchored to something drawn.
  - If Fig. motif already establishes the kernel–leakage mechanism, consider reducing (e)–(h) to two representative kernels in the main text and moving the other two to the supplement.

### figure7.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Three practically-used spectral estimators (7-taper Thomson, cosine-tapered periodogram smoothed by a parabola, and 9-segment Welch) give essentially the same PSD for one EEG epoch — typically agreeing to within each estimator's own ±1.5–1.6 dB sampling noise — with the residual disagreement concentrated in the inter-harmonic valleys.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — mostly readable, but with one important gap. Both panels carry in-plot legends, the grey band is annotated in-plot, and the difference panel makes the comparison explicit, which is good. However, the figure's own visual evidence *appears to contradict* the claim: panel (b) shows dozens of excursions to +5 to +12 dB, i.e. far outside the grey band, so a reader looking only at the graphic would conclude "these estimators disagree at many frequencies." The statistic that actually carries the claim — the *median* absolute difference of 0.6/0.9 dB — exists only in the caption. Secondary gaps: the degrees of freedom (ν = 14, 16.7, 17.6) and half-power widths (0.91/1.03/0.97 Hz), which are the fairness conditions of the comparison, are not on the figure; "cosine taper, then parabola" cannot be interpreted without the text's recipe (b′).

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — overlaid line spectra for the "do they agree?" question plus a residual/difference panel with a tolerance band is the right construction (essentially a Bland–Altman layout in the frequency domain). Log-frequency-free linear axis is fine for 0–40 Hz harmonic structure. The only structural addition worth considering is a third small panel (or inset in (b)): an ECDF or histogram of |difference| with the median and 95th percentile marked, which would let the central claim be read off the graphic rather than the caption.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel (b) y-axis spans ±15 dB while the data occupy roughly −5 to +12.5 dB; ~40% of the panel is empty, shrinking the features that matter.
- Dashed zero line is largely redundant with the grey band centred on zero.
- Horizontal gridlines in both panels add ink without supporting any quantitative read-off the claim requires.
- Panel (a)'s fine peak structure is visually dominated by the blue multitaper trace's noise wiggle; since (a) exists only to establish "the three curves lie on top of each other," much of its detail is decorative relative to the claim.
- Two-line right-aligned text block for the grey band could be one short line ("grey: ±1.6 dB, multitaper s.d.").

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panel (a) re-plots the same EEG epoch (and presumably the same multitaper estimate) already shown in Fig. `fig:abstract`, so it is partly a repeat; and the numerical content of the caption (ν, half-power widths, medians, 95th percentiles for three estimators) is a small table masquerading as prose. Panel (b) is the novel, load-bearing content. Consider: keep (b) in the main text, move (a) to the supplement or compress it to a narrow strip, and promote the summary numbers to a 3-row table.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The graphic's dominant visual message (many large spikes outside the tolerance band) conflicts with the caption's headline claim ("agree to within their own noise"). As drawn, the figure argues against its own title sentence. Either show the distribution of differences so the reader can see that the *typical* difference is sub-band, or restate the claim as "agree to within their own noise except in the inter-harmonic valleys."
- [BLOCKING] The claim's key evidence (median |Δ| = 0.6 and 0.9 dB; 95th percentiles 4.2 and 5.9 dB) is caption-only. Put the medians on panel (b) as horizontal reference lines or in a small inset ECDF.
- [ADVISORY] The fairness of the comparison rests on matched resolution (0.91/1.03/0.97 Hz half-power width) and on ν; neither is visible. Annotate ν in the legend of (a) (e.g. "Multitaper, L = 7 (ν = 14)") and mark a resolution bar (~1 Hz) in (a).
- [ADVISORY] Legend entry "Cosine taper, then parabola" is uninterpretable standalone; add "(recipe b′)" or a one-clause descriptor.
- [ADVISORY] The caption's mechanistic explanation (seventh Slepian side lobes filling the valleys) is a distinct assertion that the figure does not demonstrate; it needs either a supporting inset or explicit hedging.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) has no x tick labels; acceptable for a shared axis, but the reader must trace down to (b) to locate peaks — ensure the panels are exactly x-aligned (they appear to be) and consider light shared vertical guides at 10/20/30 Hz instead of horizontal gridlines.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Rewrite the caption's first sentence to match what is visible, e.g. "Three practical estimators agree to within their own noise at most frequencies; the exceptions are the valleys between harmonics."
- Add an inset or third panel: ECDF (or histogram) of |difference| for the two comparisons, with median, 95th percentile, and the 1.6 dB noise level marked. This converts the claim from caption-asserted to figure-demonstrated.
- Draw horizontal lines (or short tick marks at the right edge) at the median |Δ| for each comparison in panel (b).
- Clip panel (b) to about −6 to +13 dB to recover the wasted vertical space; drop the dashed zero line and the horizontal gridlines.
- Shade or bracket the inter-harmonic valley frequencies in (b) so the "where the disagreement lives" point is visible, not just stated.
- Annotate ν (and optionally half-power width) directly in the panel (a) legend; add a 1 Hz resolution bar.
- Move the three-estimator numerical summary (ν, half-power width, median |Δ|, 95th percentile) out of the caption into a compact table, and shorten the caption to the claim plus the reading instructions.
- If Fig. `fig:abstract` already shows this epoch's multitaper spectrum, reduce panel (a) to a half-height strip or relocate it to the supplement and lead with the difference panel.

### figure8.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Multitaper estimation and taper-plus-parabola smoothing of the periodogram deliver nearly interchangeable (median |Δ| = 0.9 dB), far less variable time–frequency pictures of a seizure than the raw periodogram, whose variance obscures the same spectral structure.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the figure is unusually close to self-reading (each spectrogram carries an in-panel title with its estimator, resolution and degrees of freedom; the shared colour bar is labelled in physical units; the panel (h) title states the comparison and the median is annotated in-panel), but four things cannot be recovered without the caption:
- The grey band in (a) is never labelled; a reader does not know it marks the seizure, nor that (b) is drawn from inside it.
- The triangles in (a) and the white dashed lines in (c,e,g) are unexplained pointers; the "(d)" and "(f)" text next to the triangles helps, but the link to the dashed lines must be inferred.
- Panel (d) has no legend, so the reader must find the legend in (f) two panels below and assume identical colour coding.
- The abrupt low-power block after ~370 s and the vertical striping near 400 s (electrode/suppression artefact?) are visually dominant but unannotated, and are easily misread as an estimator effect.
- Minor: "ν" appears in three panel titles with no in-figure gloss (degrees of freedom); the sign convention of (h) is only decipherable from its title.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — the figure mixes three purposes and matches each: spectrograms for change over time and frequency, overlaid line spectra for estimator-to-estimator comparison at fixed instants, and a difference histogram for the agreement claim. Two refinements worth considering: (i) the three-panel stacked spectrogram invites eyeballing rather than measurement, so the histogram in (h) is doing the real comparative work — consider adding a companion histogram for (c) minus (e) so the periodogram's excess variance is quantified, not merely visible; (ii) in (d,f) a semi-transparent grey band or thin line for the periodogram already works, but the four-curve overlay is near the limit of legibility where the smoothed estimates coincide — a small residual sub-panel (each smooth estimate minus the multitaper) would make "they agree" measurable rather than asserted.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel (b): six seconds of raw ictal EEG. It supports no part of the main claim; the ictal rhythm's frequency content is already shown in (f) and in the spectrograms. Strong candidate for removal or supplement.
- Panel (a) is context rather than evidence; it can shrink substantially (it currently occupies a full row of width) or lose its ±500 µV clipping, which throws away the seizure amplitude it is meant to convey.
- Redundant tick labels on the three spectrogram x-axes; only the bottom axis needs numbers.
- The colour bar is very tall and its axis label sits far from the bar, straddling panels (c) and (e); a shorter bar with the label adjacent would read faster.
- Frequency axis extends to 30 Hz in all spectrograms while the informative energy is below ~20 Hz; the upper third is mostly uniform blue in (g).

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — (d) and (f) make the same methodological point at two time points; one of them (most likely (d), the pre-ictal case) could move to the supplement without weakening the claim. Panel (h) reduces to a single number ("median |difference| 0.9 dB") that is already printed on the panel and stated in the caption, so the histogram is defensible only if the *shape* (symmetry, tail width) matters to the argument — if it does, say so on the panel. Panels (a) and (b) are likely to duplicate recording-overview material from an earlier figure in a paper of this kind.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The shaded interval in (a) is unlabelled; the central object of the figure (the seizure) is identified only in the caption.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (d) lacks a legend and cannot be read independently of (f).
- [BLOCKING] The post-370 s low-power block and ~400 s vertical stripes are prominent, unexplained, and confoundable with estimator behaviour; annotate them (e.g. "amplifier saturation/artefact") or exclude the interval.
- [ADVISORY] The claim that the two smoothed estimators agree is quantified (h) but the claim that both beat the periodogram is left to visual impression; no matching statistic is given.
- [ADVISORY] EEG in (a) is clipped at ±500 µV, hiding the ictal amplitude that (b) is included to show — the two panels partly work against each other.
- [ADVISORY) "ν" and "L" are used in panel titles without in-figure definition.
- [ADVISORY] Welch's method appears only in (d,f) and has no spectrogram, so the four-estimator comparison is asymmetric; either add a fourth spectrogram or state in-panel that Welch is shown at the two instants only.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add the word "Seizure" (and onset/offset times) inside or above the grey band in (a), and repeat thin onset/offset markers on the spectrograms so the ictal window is locatable without the caption.
- Move the legend to panel (d), or place one shared legend once and reference it, so both spectrum panels read alone.
- Annotate the artefact/suppression region after ~370 s directly on one spectrogram, or crop the time axis to the interval the figure is arguing about.
- Delete panel (b) (or move to supplement) and reclaim the space to enlarge the three spectrograms; drop duplicate x-tick labels on (c) and (e).
- Add a second, lighter histogram (or a second in-panel median) for "periodogram minus multitaper" in (h) so both halves of the claim — periodogram is noisy, the two smoothers agree — are quantified in the same place.
- Consider trimming the frequency axis to 0–20 or 0–25 Hz and un-clipping (a), so amplitude and the informative band are both preserved.
- Retitle (h) in claim form, e.g. "Multitaper and smoothed periodogram agree within ~1 dB", rather than the mechanical "Panel (e) minus panel (g)"; the same applies to the whole figure if a single overall title is permitted.

### figure9.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** UNCLEAR — the figure is making two claims at once: (i) window length trades time localization against frequency localization for sleep spindles (panels a–g), and (ii) multitaper and cosine-taper+parabola smoothing reach nearly the same achieved resolution/variance except in the small-α regime, where the integer-taper constraint penalizes multitaper (panel h, and the left-vs-right pairing). Either claim alone is defensible; together they compete, and the caption's final sentence ("Short windows keep the spindles brief…") states only claim (i), so the reader is left unsure which panel is the argument and which is the illustration.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No —
- The white resolution box is labeled "resolution of the panel" in (b) only, and its second, load-bearing meaning ("twice its area ≈ ν") exists nowhere on the figure.
- ν, α, L and R appear as bare symbols in panel titles, axis labels and the (h) annotation with no on-figure definition; ν in particular is the quantity the whole right-hand comparison rests on.
- The grey shading in (a) is unexplained on-figure; a reader cannot know it marks scored spindles.
- Critically, the spindles themselves are not locatable in (b)–(g). There are no vertical guides carrying the five shaded epochs down into the spectrograms and no horizontal marking of the 11–16 Hz band. The claim is about spindles, but in the spectrograms the spindle band is visually indistinguishable from the alpha/theta activity dominating 5–15 Hz.
- The three-row × two-column logic (window length × method) must be reconstructed by reading six panel titles rather than being read off row/column headers.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — spectrograms are the right choice for showing time-frequency concentration, and the dumbbell/lollipop design in (h) is a good match for paired within-setting comparisons (it encodes the gap between methods directly, which a bar chart or table would not). Two reservations: the rainbow colormap is perceptually non-uniform and not colorblind-safe, and it creates spurious banding that will be read as structure; and the deep-red saturation across the whole 0–4 Hz band consumes roughly a third of each panel's area while conveying no gradient at all.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Method names repeated in all six panel titles; belong as two column headers.
- x-axis tick labels repeated on rows (b–c) and (d–e); only the bottom row needs them.
- Numeric values (1.1 Hz, ν=2.0, etc.) printed twice — once in panel titles (b–g), once as labels in (h).
- Saturated dark-red 0–4 Hz region in all six spectrograms.
- Frequency axis extended to ~24 Hz when the argument concerns 11–16 Hz; the top third is largely uniform blue.
- Vertical gridlines in (h) add little given the direct value labels.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium-to-High — Panel (h) re-states in graphical form exactly the six (width, ν) pairs already written in the titles of (b)–(g); as drawn it is a six-number table. Its value is the *gap* encoding and the "small-α regime" annotation, which is worth keeping only if claim (ii) is the figure's point. Separately, three window lengths × two methods is more cases than a single claim needs; the 2 s row is intermediate and could move to the supplement without weakening either claim. If the manuscript contains an earlier figure on achieved half-power width versus α or L, (h) likely duplicates it.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Two competing main claims in one figure; decide whether this is a resolution-tradeoff figure or a method-comparison figure and demote the other.
- [BLOCKING] The spindles — the object of the stated claim — cannot be located in panels (b)–(g). Without time and frequency guides the reader cannot verify "short windows keep the spindles brief and blur them in frequency."
- [BLOCKING] ν is undefined on-figure yet is the axis of comparison in (h) and the interpretation of the white box; the box's area-to-ν relation is caption-only.
- [ADVISORY] Rainbow colormap: non-uniform and not colorblind-safe; introduces false contours in a figure whose argument is about smoothness versus structure.
- [ADVISORY] Low-frequency clipping wastes dynamic range and hides whether the two methods differ in the delta band.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (h) duplicates numbers already in the panel titles.
- [ADVISORY] Row/column structure is implicit rather than labeled.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Pick one claim for the title/caption. If the claim is the resolution tradeoff, keep one method (three panels) and move the method comparison plus (h) to a companion figure or supplement. If the claim is the method comparison, keep (h) prominent and reduce the spectrograms to a single window length pair plus one contrasting pair.
- Carry the five shaded spindle epochs from (a) down through (b)–(g) as faint vertical bands or tick marks under each panel, and mark 11–16 Hz with thin horizontal lines. This single change is what lets the figure be read without the caption.
- Add two column headers ("Multitaper" / "Cosine taper, then parabola") and three row labels ("1 s", "2 s", "4 s"); strip the method names and window lengths from the six titles, leaving only the achieved width and ν.
- Move the white-box legend and its "area ≈ ν/2" meaning into a small annotated inset next to the colorbar, and gloss ν, α, L, R there in one line.
- Replace the rainbow map with a perceptually uniform sequential map (viridis, magma) and either raise the upper colorbar limit or plot a whitened/1-f-corrected PSD so the 0–4 Hz band is no longer flat red.
- Truncate the frequency axis at ~20 Hz.
- If (h) is retained, drop the duplicated numeric labels from the panel titles and add a light vertical reference line at the target half-power width R so the reader sees which method meets the specification.

---

## Composition & Layout Inspector

### efigure1.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — The core design is admirably lean (two-spine axes, no frames, no fills, no 3D, no shadows, uniform line weights). Remaining excess is redundancy rather than ornament: (i) y-tick labels are repeated on all four panels of rows 2, 3 and 4 even though the scale is identical across each row — three of four sets are non-data ink; (ii) the x-axis label is repeated on all four (and in rows 1–2, all eight) panels that share the same abscissa variable ($k$ appears eight times, $i$ four times, $fN\Delta$ four times); (iii) row 4 carries light horizontal gridlines that no other row has — they are lighter than the curves (good) but their presence in only one row is an inconsistency, and the dotted $|f|=R/2$ references already supply the needed reading aid.

READING FLOW:     Natural — The matrix reads correctly: methods across (columns, consistent colour per method), processing stage down (rows). Column titles sit only on row 1 and colour identity carries them downward, which works. One minor backtrack: the annotation in (h), "the bank itself, as in (d)", forces an upward jump three rows; and row 1 mixes annotation idioms (text in a and c, legend keys in b and d) so the eye must re-learn how each panel is indexed.

CHARTJUNK:
  - None identified in the decorative sense — no borders, fills, shadows, or 3D.
  - Borderline: gridlines present only in row 4.
  - Borderline: legend keys in (b), (d) and (e) duplicate information (shade → index) that is constant across the whole figure and could be stated once.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate — Panel spacing is generous and readable. The empty right-hand thirds of (i) and (l) are *informative* white space (the ladder terminates at $i=7$) and should be preserved for cross-column comparison, not cropped. No crowding anywhere.

AXES:             OK — Bars in row 3 are anchored at zero; rows 1–2 include zero and the sign of the tapers is meaningful; row 4 is a dB (log-ratio) scale referenced to its own peak, a documented and field-standard exception. No inverted axes. Two small consistency points: row 1 y-limits legitimately differ per column (different quantities, with (c) explicitly normalised by $\max h$), but the reader is not warned — worth a clause in the caption. Tick-label precision switches between rows (0.0/0.1/0.2 vs 0.00/0.05/0.10), which is defensible but slightly jarring in a shared column.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — Appropriate; these are deterministic taper and kernel constructions, not estimates. No action needed.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(p) all present, all parenthesised lowercase, all set outside the axes at upper-left, uniform size and weight. Exemplary.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — Panels are near-square, which suits the compact-support tapers and the symmetric kernels equally. Row 4 could arguably be wider than tall (it is a spectrum), but uniformity across a 4×4 matrix outweighs per-row optimisation here.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — All annotations ("shifts $n=0,32,\ldots,192$", "centred at $k=-128,0,128,256$", "$\nu=13.4$" etc., "the bank itself, as in (d)") are unboxed, unfilled inline text and none overlaps a curve or bar. The $\nu$ values in row 3 are a model use of direct annotation. The only weakness is idiomatic inconsistency within row 1 (text vs legend) and the cross-panel pointer in (h).

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — Three frameless but detached two-column keys, in (b) ($f'N\Delta=0\ldots3$), (d) ($i=1\ldots4$) and (e) ($i=1\ldots4$). None overlaps data (peaks in (b) reach ~0.068 and in (d)/(e) ~0.12, comfortably below the key), so nothing is blocking. However, (d) and (e) carry the *same* key (shade → taper index) in two different hues, and rows 2's remaining panels (f, g, h) rely on the reader importing that mapping from (e) — a lookup cost repeated across the figure.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant y-tick labels on columns 2–4 of rows 2, 3 and 4, where the scale is identical to column 1.
  - [ADVISORY] $x$-axis label repeated eight times ($k$), four times ($i$), four times ($fN\Delta$).
  - [ADVISORY] Gridlines appear only in row 4, inconsistent with the other twelve panels.
  - [ADVISORY] Duplicate shade→index legends in (d) and (e); (f)–(h) depend on an off-panel mapping.
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes on line panels (b), (d), (e) where inline labelling at each curve's peak would remove the lookup entirely.
  - [ADVISORY] Row 1 mixes annotation idioms (free text in a, c; legend keys in b, d), so the indexing convention changes panel to panel.
  - [ADVISORY] Cross-reference "as in (d)" in (h) forces an upward jump across three rows.
  - [ADVISORY] Row 1 y-limits differ per column, including a normalised axis in (c); the caption does not flag this.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Share y-axes within each row (`sharey=True`): keep tick labels and the y-label on column 1 only. This alone removes ~24 redundant label strings and reinforces that the rows are directly comparable.
  - Print each x-axis label once per row, under the leftmost panel (or once under the bottom row for $fN\Delta$), rather than under every panel.
  - Either drop the row-4 gridlines (the dotted $|f|=R/2$ lines plus the dB ticks suffice) or add the same faint lines to rows 1–2 for consistency; do not leave a single row treated differently.
  - Replace the keys in (d) and (e) with a single shade ramp legend — e.g. four short colour swatches labelled $i=1\ldots4$ placed once beside the row-2 y-label, or inline labels "$i=1$", "$i=2$", "$i=3$", "$i=4$" set in each curve's own colour at its peak in panel (e) only. Curves in (e)–(h) cross repeatedly, so if inline labels are used, place them at each curve's global maximum (a flat, uncrowded segment) and stagger them vertically; add a thin same-colour leader line where a label would otherwise sit within a line width of a neighbouring curve.
  - In (b), label the four cosine-modulated curves inline at their first extremum ("$f'N\Delta=0$" on the flat plateau, the others at their first trough), matching curve colour, and delete the key.
  - Harmonise row 1's annotation idiom: use the same short inline text form in all four panels (e.g. "shifts $n=0,32,\ldots,192$", "modulations $f'N\Delta=0\ldots3$", "centres $k=-128,0,128,256$", "tapers $i=1\ldots7$"), so the reader learns one convention.
  - Replace "the bank itself, as in (d)" in (h) with a self-contained phrase such as "eigen-tapers $=$ the bank (Slepians)", removing the need to look back up the column.
  - Add one caption clause noting that row 1 y-scales are panel-specific and that (c) is normalised by $\max h$, so absolute heights are not comparable across that row.
  - Preserve the blank right-hand portion of (i) and (l) and state in the caption that the abscissa is common to row 3, so the truncation of the ladder at $i=7$ reads as data rather than as a plotting artefact.
---

### efigure2.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Good — open (two-spine) axes, no bounding boxes, no 3D, shadows, or fills. Horizontal gridlines are light and subordinate to the data. Only minor redundancy: the legend key lines in (a) duplicate information that is already carried by the curve colours, and panel (a)'s y-axis extends to +60 dB although the data top out near +48 dB.

READING FLOW:     Natural — (a) → (b) left to right; within (b) the inline labels are stacked in the same vertical order as the curves they name, so no backtracking. Only friction is the mode switch between panels: the reader learns the colour code from a legend in (a), then must re-learn it from inline text in (b).

CHARTJUNK:
  - None substantive. (Legend sample-line swatches in (a) are the only non-essential marks; they become unnecessary if the series are labelled directly.)

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate — panel (b)'s right-hand margin is well used by the labels; some unused headroom at the top of (a) above +48 dB (partly occupied by the legend, so acceptable).

AXES:             OK — both x axes begin at 0 and end at the Nyquist limit 0.5, the field convention. Neither y axis begins at zero, but both are in dB (a log scale), which is a documented and appropriate exception; (b) is a difference scale centred on 0 with a zero reference line, which is exactly right. No inverted axes. Tick density and labelling are clean.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — uncertainty is conveyed indirectly via the degrees-of-freedom values ν quoted in the caption rather than graphically.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — "(a)" and "(b)" both top-left, same size and style.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — wide panels suit frequency-domain traces; the horizontal extent resolves the dense oscillations without vertical exaggeration.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — no callout bubbles, borders, or fills. The zero reference line in (b) is a good replacement for an annotation.
