LEGEND STYLE:     Mixed — Box in (a) (does not overlap data; it sits in an empty region above the roll-off), Inline in (b). The inline labels in (b) are colour-matched and placed outside the data region, which is the preferred Tufte solution. Two concerns in (b): (i) "Cosine taper, then parabola" (orange) and "Multitaper, L = 7, adaptive" (dark blue) sit at y ≈ 0 and y ≈ −2 with very little vertical separation, and both name curves whose right-hand ends oscillate through the same ±3 dB band — at reduced publication width these two labels will be hard to associate with the correct trace; (ii) the longest labels run flush to the right edge of the canvas with essentially zero margin.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Inline labels in (b) ("Cosine taper, then parabola", "Multitaper, L = 7, adaptive") reach the right-hand edge of the figure canvas; at journal trim or on scaling these will be clipped. Increase the right margin or shorten the labels.
  - [ADVISORY] Two-standard labelling scheme: detached legend box in (a) versus direct labels in (b). Prefer direct labelling in both panels for consistency and to eliminate the lookup cost; at f∆ = 0.5 the five smoothed estimates in (a) are separated (≈ −3, −8, −14, −20 dB), so right-end inline labels are feasible there too.
  - [ADVISORY] The orange and dark-blue labels in (b) are vertically crowded and both terminate in the same oscillating band, risking mis-attribution.
  - [ADVISORY] No graphical indication of estimator uncertainty; ν appears only in prose.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) y-axis headroom to +60 dB is ~12 dB above the maximum plotted value.
  - [ADVISORY] Gridlines in (a) run through the dense grey periodogram; although lighter than the data, they add visual noise in the most crowded region.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add ~8–12 % right-hand padding to panel (b) (or place the label block inside a reserved axes-adjacent margin) so no label glyph touches the canvas edge; verify at final column width.
  - Stagger the orange and dark-blue labels in (b) vertically (e.g. orange at +1.5 dB, adaptive at −4 dB) and attach a short, thin leader line in the series colour from each label to its curve endpoint.
  - Replace the legend in (a) with colour-matched end-of-line labels at f∆ = 0.5 (green, blue, light blue, adaptive/orange) plus an inline "True PSD" label on the black curve near f∆ ≈ 0.35; keep "Periodogram" as a grey inline label in the low-level region. This removes the legend box and the swatch ink entirely.
  - Trim panel (a)'s y-axis to about +50 dB (or −35 to +50) to recover the unused headroom once the legend is removed.
  - Consider dropping or further lightening gridlines in (a), or restricting them to the axis ticks; alternatively keep them only in (b), where they support reading the quoted 18 dB / 10 dB / 4 dB offsets.
  - Optionally add a small χ²-based confidence-interval bar (one per estimator, or a single representative bar with ν annotated) in a quiet corner of (a) to make the degrees-of-freedom trade-off visible rather than caption-only.

### efigure3.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The curves themselves are drawn cleanly at appropriate weight, and the horizontal gridlines are appropriately subordinate (light grey, thinner than the data). Two problems reduce the ratio: (i) panel (a)'s y-axis extends to ~0.26 while the maximum plotted value is ~0.135, so roughly half the panel is non-data area existing only to house the legend; (ii) that legend is itself redundant with the inline labels in panel (b), which name all five series.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — (a) → (b) left to right; within (b) the right-hand labels are encountered after the curves, which is the correct order. One friction point: a reader in panel (a) must jump up-left to the legend to decode five colors, then return to the curves, whereas panel (b) requires no lookup at all. The two panels therefore teach the reader two different decoding habits.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Legend block in (a) — redundant given the inline labels in (b); the line-sample swatches duplicate information already carried by curve color.
- Nothing else: no borders, fills, shadows, or 3D effects. Spines appear to be left/bottom only, which is correct.

**WHITE SPACE:** Excessive in panel (a) — the upper ~45% of the axes is empty. Elsewhere adequate; the right-hand label gutter in (b) is well proportioned.

**AXES:** OK — (a) begins at zero on both axes. (b) is dB relative to peak, so the 0 → −100 downward scale is field-standard and not an inverted-axis violation. Minor notes: the x-ranges differ between panels (±10 vs ±25) with no visual cue linking them; and the (b) y-limit of −100 dB clips the Bohman curve at |fNΔ| ≈ 20, which may be intentional but reads as a truncation.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate, these are exact deterministic kernels.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a) and (b) both upper-left, outside the axes, same size and style.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — wide panels suit the frequency-domain curves. However, panel (a)'s effective data aspect is worse than it appears because of the unused vertical headroom; tightening the y-limit would improve the visual resolution of the main-lobe ripple without changing the figure footprint.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — no callout boxes, brackets, or filled text blocks. The dotted ideal-box outline is data, not annotation.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Mixed — Box in (a), Inline — clean in (b). Panel (b) is exemplary: labels sit in the right margin, color-matched, outside the data region, no overlap with any curve. Panel (a) retains a detached legend that is now unnecessary. In (b), "Untapered, then box" (≈ −30 dB) and "Multitaper, L = 7" (≈ −38 dB) sit close together vertically and are the one pair a reader might have to disambiguate; the remaining three are well separated.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Detached legend box on a line chart in panel (a); it duplicates the inline labels already present in panel (b) and imposes a lookup cost that (b) has eliminated.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) y-axis extends to ~0.26 against a data maximum of ~0.135; the empty upper half exists only to accommodate the legend.
- [ADVISORY] In (b), the "Untapered, then box" and "Multitaper, L = 7" labels are crowded in the same vertical neighbourhood (~8 dB apart) where the two curves also converge; at reduced publication width they may be hard to associate with the correct series.
- [ADVISORY] The change of x-range between (a) and (b) is unmarked, so the reader must infer that (a) is a zoom of (b)'s central region.
- [ADVISORY] Light blue (Bohman) on white is the lowest-contrast series; the dashing helps, but at print scale the thin dashes near −95 dB will be faint.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete the legend from (a) and rely on (b)'s inline labels, adding one clause to the caption ("curves are named at their right-hand ends in (b)"). Alternatively, label the five curves directly in (a) as well — but since they overlap heavily there, deferring to (b) is cleaner.
- With the legend gone, set (a)'s y-limit to ~0.15 (ticks at 0.00–0.15 by 0.05). The main-lobe ripple of the multitaper kernel and the 0.125 plateau shared by the box-based kernels will roughly double in visual amplitude, which is the substantive comparison the panel exists to make.
- Separate the two crowded labels in (b): nudge "Multitaper, L = 7" down 3–4 dB, or attach a short thin leader line in the matching blue from each label to its curve endpoint.
- Add a light cue tying the panels together — e.g. faint vertical reference lines at fNΔ = ±10 in (b), or a note in the caption that (a) spans the shaded central region of (b).
- Consider extending (b)'s y-limit to −105 or −110 dB so the Bohman skirt is not cut off at the axis boundary, and thicken the dashed light-blue line by ~0.2 pt for print contrast.
- Confirm the caption defines ν once where it first appears; as drafted the symbol is introduced parenthetically without a gloss, which forces a jump to Table 2.

### efigure4.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Good (minor work) — No 3D, no shadows, no panel bounding boxes; axes are open (left + bottom spines only), which is Tufte-compliant. Faint horizontal gridlines are lighter than the data lines, so they do not compete. Small remaining redundancies: the x-axis title "Half-power width ×*N*Δ" is repeated three times (unavoidable given the side-by-side layout, but panel (a)/(b) share an identical scale and could share one title if stacked); the legend box's nine marker+line samples are the largest single block of non-data ink in the figure, though justified by series count.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic (mild) — Panels read (a)→(b) left-to-right, then (c) below: natural. However, the shared legend occupies the fourth quadrant at lower right, so a reader decoding panel (a) must jump diagonally to the opposite corner and back. This is the standard compromise for a shared legend, but the lookup cost is real with nine entries and four near-identical blue/grey line styles.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- None substantive. No decorative elements, no frames, no fills.
- Marker *fill* is used as an encoding channel ("filled = no flat top"), but it is only defined in panel (c); filled markers also appear in (a) and (b) where the convention carries no meaning, which turns an encoding into visual noise there.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate — Panels are not crowded; curves are distinguishable. The empty fourth quadrant is productively used by the legend, though the legend floats low and leaves a large unused gap between it and panel (b) above.

**AXES:** OK — (a) *y* begins at zero, correct. (b) log *y* is appropriate for six decades of leakage and is an accepted exception. (c) dB is a log/ratio scale, so a non-zero origin is legitimate. *x* begins near 1–2 rather than 0; acceptable since half-power width is a ratio quantity that cannot be zero, but it is not documented in the caption. All three panels share the same *x* range (~0–27), which correctly enables cross-panel comparison — good.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — and correctly so: the caption states these are exact computations with no sampling variability. No action needed.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a), (b), (c) all present, all top-left, same size and style (lowercase parenthesized, not bold). Consider bolding for journal house style, but placement and consistency are correct. The legend quadrant is unlabeled, which is appropriate (it is not a panel).

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — Panels are near-square, suitable for these monotone trade-off curves where both axes carry comparable analytic weight; no time-series stretching needed.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — Both annotations ("8/(*N*Δ), the width in Table 2" in (a); "filled markers: no flat top…" in (c)) are unboxed, unshadowed grey inline text placed in empty regions. Verified no overlap with data: the (a) annotation ends near *x*≈17 while the pink curve reaches *y*=60 only at *x*≈24.5; the (c) annotation sits in vacant upper-right space. Labeling the dotted reference line only once, in (a), rather than in all three panels, is exemplary economy.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — justified, since nine series exceed the 2–5 range where inline labeling is practical. The legend is borderless (good) and does not overlap any data (it occupies its own quadrant). Weakness: entry order does not track the vertical order of curve endpoints in any panel, and three of nine entries are the same dark blue distinguished only by solid/dashed/dotted plus small circle/square/triangle markers, with a fourth light-blue series nearby — at final publication width these will be hard to separate.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Four blue-family series (Multitaper ×3, Bohman) are separable only by line dash and small marker glyph; at reduced print size the dotted vs. dashed distinction and the open-circle vs. open-square distinction will likely collapse.
- [ADVISORY] Legend entry order is unrelated to curve order at the right-hand edge of any panel, maximizing lookup cost for a nine-entry legend placed diagonally opposite panel (a).
- [ADVISORY] Marker fill is a meaningful encoding in (c) only, but filled markers also appear in (a) and (b), where they encode nothing — readers may infer a convention that does not apply.
- [ADVISORY] Legend sits low in its quadrant, leaving a conspicuous vertical gap below panel (b) and an uneven visual balance across the 2×2 grid.
- [ADVISORY] Non-zero *x* origin is scientifically fine but undocumented; a half-sentence in the caption would pre-empt reviewer queries.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) and (c) carry the dotted 8/(*N*Δ) line with no label; correct economy, but the caption should state explicitly that the same line appears in all three panels (it currently says only "the dotted line marks…").

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Reorder legend entries to match the top-to-bottom order of curve endpoints in panel (a) (Welch, Untapered, Cosine, Multitaper 2α−1, Multitaper sine, Multitaper 2α−2, Hann-box, Bohman-box, Hann-Gaussian), so the legend becomes a spatial index rather than an arbitrary list.
- Add sparse inline endpoint labels in panel (a) for the three or four best-separated series (Welch at ν≈60, Untapered/Cosine at ≈33, Hann-then-box at ≈17, Bohman at ≈14), color-matched and placed to the right of the final marker in the wide empty region beyond *x*=17. This lets most readers avoid the legend entirely while the legend still resolves the crowded middle group.
- Differentiate the blue family further: give Bohman a clearly distinct hue (e.g., teal or purple) and increase marker size slightly, or use filled-vs-open consistently only where it is not already reserved for the flat-top encoding.
- Standardize marker fill: in (a) and (b) use open markers throughout, reserving filled markers exclusively for the flat-top convention in (c); note this explicitly in the caption.
- Raise the legend to sit flush with the top of the panel (c) axes (or move it into the upper-right of the empty quadrant) to tighten the grid and shorten the eye's travel from panels (a)/(b).
- Add to the caption: "The *x* axis begins near the smallest achievable half-power width; zero is unattainable for all methods," and "The dotted vertical line at 8/(*N*Δ) appears in all three panels."
- Consider stacking (a) above (b) with a single shared *x* axis title if the journal permits a taller figure; this would remove two redundant axis titles and free space to widen all panels.

### figure1.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Good — thin curves carry most of the ink; horizontal gridlines are light gray and recede behind the data; no borders, fills, 3D effects, or shadows; open-circle markers in (c) are minimal. Minor redundancy: the "Frequency (Hz)" label and full 0–40 tick set appear twice (panels a and b) for the same variable and range, and the legend key in (a) duplicates information already spelled out in the caption.

READING FLOW:     Natural — (a) full width on top, then (b) → (c) left to right; panel labels read in order without backtracking. One friction point: panels (a) and (b) plot the same 0–40 Hz domain at different physical widths, so vertical comparison of a spectral feature in (a) with its difference in (b) requires re-scaling by eye.

CHARTJUNK:
  - None substantive. The legend header line "Three estimates that coincide" is non-data text, but it carries the figure's main claim efficiently; acceptable, though it duplicates the caption.
  - Legend line swatches in (a) are necessary (the three curves superimpose exactly and cannot be labeled inline), so they are not junk.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate — the empty upper-right of (a) is productively used by the key; the ~12-decade gap between the two traces in (b) is data-driven (it *is* the result) and should not be compressed. Slight surplus between the right edge of (b) and the y-label of (c).

AXES:             OK — no zero-baseline violation: (a) is in dB (a logarithmic ratio scale), (b) and (c) are log axes; all defensible. No inverted axes. Tick labeling is clean (decade-only labels on the log axes). Two small inconsistencies: y-label capitalization differs between (b) "|Difference|" and (c) "Max. |difference|"; and (c)'s abscissa is N_s/N while the caption describes it as "the window length N_s."

ERROR BARS:       Not present — for a spectral estimate with a stated ν = 17 equivalent degrees of freedom, no uncertainty is shown in (a). Not blocking (the figure's claim is about agreement between estimators, not about spectral uncertainty), but a reader will ask whether the 0.06 dB discrepancy in (b) is negligible relative to estimator variance.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a), (b), (c) all present, same parenthetical style, same size, each at the top-left. Note that (a) sits at the top-left of the *figure* canvas while (b) and (c) sit at the top-left of their axes; the horizontal offset from the y-label differs slightly between (a) and (b).

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — (a) is wide and short, correct for a 0–40 Hz spectrum with many narrow peaks; (b) is wide enough to resolve the noise-like traces; (c) is near-square for a five-point power law. Only quibble: (c) is tall and narrow enough that the slope of the solid line versus the 1/N_s guide is exaggerated relative to a square panel.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — the two labels in (b) and the "1/N_s" label in (c) are unboxed, unfilled inline text, color-matched to their series. This is exactly the Tufte-preferred form. "1/N_s" sits clear of both the dotted guide and the solid curve.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box (justified in a) / Inline — clean (b, c) — the detached key in (a) would normally be flagged as replaceable by direct labels, but the three curves coincide to within 0.06 dB and cannot be separated at the point of labeling, so a key is the correct choice here. It does not overlap data. In (b), inline labels are used correctly: the "Sliding sinc window − multitaper" label sits above its trace and the "Untapered, then box − multitaper: rounding error" label above the lower trace, each in the series color.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (a) and (b) share the 0–40 Hz domain but not the same physical width, defeating vertical registration between the spectrum and its residuals.
  - [ADVISORY] The dark-green label in (b) is placed close to the 10⁰ gridline and only ~1 decade above a trace whose spikes reach ~10⁻¹; at reduced publication width the descenders may touch the tallest excursions.
  - [ADVISORY] No uncertainty indication in (a) despite ν = 17 being quoted in the caption; the reader cannot judge the 0.06 dB agreement against estimator variance.
  - [ADVISORY] Axis-label capitalization mismatch: "|Difference| (dB)" vs "Max. |difference| (dB)".
  - [ADVISORY] Caption/axis mismatch in (c): caption says "against the window length N_s", axis reads N_s/N.
  - [ADVISORY] "Frequency (Hz)" label and tick set duplicated between (a) and (b).

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Set panel (b) to the same width and x-limits as (a) and stack them on a shared abscissa; drop (a)'s tick labels and its "Frequency (Hz)" title, keeping one axis label for both. Move (c) to its own row or to the right of (a)'s legend space.
  - Raise the "Sliding sinc window − multitaper" label by half a decade, or nudge it left/right to a quieter stretch of the trace; if separation is still tight at final width, add a thin dark-green leader line.
  - In (a), add a short vertical bar or a shaded band in the legend area showing the 95% CI half-width implied by ν = 17, so the 0.06 dB agreement can be read as negligible by inspection.
  - Harmonize y-label capitalization across (b) and (c); change (c)'s caption wording to "against the window length ratio N_s/N" or relabel the axis N_s (in units of N).
  - Consider moving "Three estimates that coincide" out of the legend into the caption's first sentence, leaving three bare keyed entries; alternatively keep it, as it is the cheapest possible statement of the result.
  - Optional: widen (c) slightly toward square so the departure of the measured points from the 1/N_s guide is read at its true slope.

### figure2.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Good — Spines are reduced to left/bottom, gridlines are faint horizontal rules well below the weight of the data, no fills, shadows, 3-D effects, or panel bounding boxes. Y-axis labels appear only on the leftmost column and x-axis labels only on the bottom row, which correctly eliminates redundant axis ink. The only non-data ink of consequence is the light grey inset frames and their connector lines, which are functionally necessary to define the zoom region and are drawn at appropriately subordinate weight.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — Estimator complexity increases left→right (untapered → tapered → lightly smoothed → heavily smoothed), and the vertical pairing (estimate above, its kernel below) lets the reader read each column as a unit without backtracking. Excellent structural design: the 2×4 grid is doing real analytical work (small multiples in Tufte's sense).

**CHARTJUNK:**
- None identified in the strict sense. Inset frames are justified; connector lines are thin and grey.
- Minor redundancy: the orange series-identifier text is repeated four times across (b)–(d) ("Periodogram, cosine taper" / "Cosine taper, then parabola" ×2). Only the *R* and ν values actually differ between (c) and (d); the repeated method string could be compressed.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate — The empty lower half of panels (c) and (d) and the empty upper band of (e)–(h) are the unavoidable cost of a common scale across small multiples, which is the right trade. The inter-row gutter is slightly generous and could be tightened ~10–15% to give the panels more area at publication width.

**AXES:** OK — Y axes are in dB relative to σ²Δ (a logarithmic/ratio scale), so a zero origin is neither required nor meaningful; the zero-reference convention is documented in the caption. No inverted axes. Shared limits across each row are correctly enforced, which is essential for the intended comparison. Two small issues: (1) the top row carries x tick labels but no *f*Δ axis title, relying on the bottom row for the variable name — acceptable given the shared columns, but a reader excerpting panel (a) alone loses the abscissa identity; (2) the noise excursions in (a) and (b) are clipped at the lower axis limit, so the reader cannot see the true dynamic range of the periodogram's downward spikes.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — Appropriate here; uncertainty is conveyed instead by the equivalent degrees of freedom ν annotated in each top-row panel, which is the correct spectral-analysis convention. Consider one sentence in the caption stating explicitly that no confidence intervals are drawn and that ν quantifies the estimator variance, so a reviewer does not read the absence as an omission.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, uniformly outside the top-left corner of each axes, same size and parenthetical style. They do not collide with data or with the insets.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — Individual panels are approximately square, suitable for these spectral curves where both the frequency axis and the ~200 dB vertical dynamic range carry information. The overall 2077×1097 canvas (≈1.9:1) is sensible for a two-row, four-column layout.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean, with two caveats — The in-panel method/ν annotations are plain colored text with no border or fill, exactly as Tufte would prescribe; they sit in genuinely empty regions and do not overlap the data. The insets are the main annotation risk: in panel (e) the inset's lower boundary sits near −50 dB while the main kernel curve rises through roughly −55 to −50 dB over *f*Δ ≈ 0.25–0.35, so the frame and the curve are within a few pixels of touching and will likely collide when the figure is scaled to single-column width. The "True PSD" label in (a) is placed at the bottom-left, far from the black curve it names, so its referent must be inferred rather than read.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Inline — clean (one weak placement) — No detached legend boxes anywhere; every series is labeled inline in its own color (grey for the untapered periodogram, orange for the tapered/smoothed estimates, black for truth). This is the ideal treatment and eliminates all lookup cost. The single weak case is "True PSD" in (a): it is black and therefore correctly color-matched, but it is remote from the black curve and sits in the panel's empty lower-left corner, so it functions as a caption rather than a direct label.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Panel (e): the inset frame's bottom edge and the main kernel curve approach within a few pixels around *f*Δ ≈ 0.25–0.35; at final publication width the frame will clip or touch the curve.
- [ADVISORY] "True PSD" in (a) is spatially divorced from the black curve it labels; the reader must infer the association.
- [ADVISORY] The noise spikes in (a) and (b) are clipped at the bottom y-limit, concealing the estimator's true downward excursions and making the two panels' variance look artificially bounded.
- [ADVISORY] Inset x-spans differ between (e)–(g) (0.02/Δ) and (h) (0.08/Δ). This is stated in the caption, but nothing in the graphic itself signals the change of scale, so a reader comparing main-lobe widths across the row may be misled.
- [ADVISORY] Top-row panels have x tick labels but no *f*Δ axis title, leaving the abscissa unnamed if a panel is viewed in isolation.
- [ADVISORY] The method string in the orange annotations is repeated across (b)–(d) where only *R* and ν differ.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Move panel (e)'s inset up and/or right by a few percent of the axes height, or raise the main panel's upper y-limit slightly, to open clear white space between the inset frame and the kernel curve. Verify the clearance at the final single-column reproduction size, not at full resolution.
- Reposition "True PSD" to sit immediately beside the black curve — e.g., just right of the peak near *f*Δ ≈ 0.2 on the descending skirt, or at the curve's left endpoint near 10 dB — or attach a short thin black leader line from the existing text to the curve. Keep it black to match the series.
- Extend the top-row lower y-limit (e.g., to −60 dB) or explicitly state in the caption that the periodogram excursions are clipped at −45 dB, so the clipping is not mistaken for the data's actual floor.
- Add a small in-panel marker of the zoom span — e.g., "×25" / "×6" or the span "0.02/Δ" / "0.08/Δ" printed in grey inside each inset — so the change of inset scale in (h) is visible in the graphic itself and not only in the caption.
- Add the *f*Δ axis title beneath the top row, or alternatively suppress the top row's x tick labels and rely on the shared column geometry; either is internally consistent, whereas ticks-without-a-name is not.
- Compress the repeated orange annotations: print the method once (e.g., as a column heading or only in (b)) and reduce (c) and (d) to the distinguishing quantities *R* and ν.
- Tighten the inter-row gutter by roughly 10–15% and redistribute the reclaimed height to the panels, increasing the area devoted to data without altering any scale.

### figure3.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — Panel (b) is enclosed by a full rectangular frame (the right-hand spine carries no ticks or information and the top spine does double duty as the ν axis), while panel (a) uses only left and bottom spines. The inconsistency is visible and the right spine is pure non-data ink. Horizontal gridlines at 2/4/6/8 dB are appropriately faint (lighter than the curves) and are acceptable, though they are partly redundant with the left-hand ticks and could be dropped without loss. Otherwise the figure is commendably lean: no fills, no shadows, no 3-D effects, no legend boxes.

READING FLOW:     Natural — (a) above (b), left→right within each panel. One minor cost: the ν scale in (b) sits on the top spine, so the reader must jump up and back down to convert R to degrees of freedom; this is a standard convention for secondary scales and is acceptable. The three callout texts in (b) are read in a slightly jumbled order (upper-left, centre, lower-right), but they follow the curve's own left→right progression, so the flow is defensible.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Right-hand spine of panel (b) (no ticks, no data).
  - Marker glyphs on the solid "whole band" curve are dense enough (≈18 points) that they add little beyond the line itself; thinning them would recover ink.
  - The large open annotation circles are justified (they key panel (b) to Fig. motif), but the one at R=0 is clipped by the left spine and reads as a drawing artifact rather than a deliberate mark.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, with two local problems — the vertical gap between the *k* axis label of (a) and the ν axis of (b) is larger than needed (roughly 8% of figure height doing no work), while the region of (b) between x≈8 and x≈20 is crowded, with the "just right" text, its leader, the circled point, and both curves competing in a small area.

AXES:             OK — Both axes of (b) begin at zero; the symmetric y range in (a) is correct for a zero-mean oscillatory record. No inverted axes. Tick labels in (a) (0, 255, 511, 767, 1023) are sensibly chosen for a power-of-two record.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — and, unusually and commendably, the caption states this explicitly ("the curves are ensemble averages and carry no error bars"). No action required.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent in style and both present, but not consistently positioned: (a) sits above the top-left corner of its axes, whereas (b) sits to the *left* of its axes at mid-height of the ν-axis band. They do not align on a common left margin.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — (a) is wide and short, correct for a 1024-sample time series; (b) is close to square, correct for two crossing error curves.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — no boxes, fills, or borders anywhere; all callouts are bare text with thin grey leader lines, which is the right choice. Two placement issues: the leader for "just right, ν = 16.7" runs from the text down-left to the circled point and crosses the dashed peak-region curve on the way; and the circled point at R = 0 is bisected by the left spine. The grey "peak width" / "peak separation" labels on the dotted verticals are well handled — subordinate colour, no overlap with data.

LEGEND STYLE:     Inline — clean (with one crowding problem) — All four series are labelled directly ("Total, peak region", "Total, whole band", "Bias", "Noise"), with no legend box and no lookup cost. Excellent. However "Total, whole band" is wrapped onto three lines and jammed against the right frame beside the solid curve's endpoint at y ≈ 5.0; at reduced publication width the text will very likely touch both the curve terminus and the spine. "Bias" and "Noise" are correctly placed on flatter segments and coloured to match their grey curves.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Right-hand spine of panel (b) is non-data ink and makes the two panels stylistically inconsistent (two spines in (a), four in (b)).
  - [ADVISORY] Inline label "Total, whole band" is wrapped over three lines and pressed against the right frame and the endpoint of the solid curve; risk of clipping/overlap when the figure is scaled to column width.
  - [ADVISORY] Leader line for "just right, ν = 16.7" crosses the dashed peak-region curve; the text also sits in the most crowded part of the plot.
  - [ADVISORY] The large annotation circle at R = 0 is clipped by the left spine.
  - [ADVISORY] At small R the solid and dashed curves coincide, so filled circles and open squares interleave and the two series are momentarily indistinguishable; the reader cannot tell which markers belong to which curve there.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel labels (a) and (b) are not aligned to a common left margin or vertical reference.
  - [ADVISORY] Excess vertical white space between the two panels; panel (b) is correspondingly compressed.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the right-hand spine of panel (b) (keep the top spine, since it carries the ν scale), matching panel (a)'s open-frame style.
  - Move "Total, whole band" inside the axes, set on one or two lines, placed just above the solid curve near R ≈ 55–60 where the curve is straight; or widen the right margin by ~6 mm and keep it as a two-line label with a short grey leader.
  - Reposition "just right, ν = 16.7" below and left of the minimum (e.g. near R ≈ 14, y ≈ 1.0, in the empty region between the solid curve and the Noise curve) with a short upward leader, so the leader no longer crosses the dashed curve.
  - Nudge the R = 0 annotation circle inboard, or start the x axis at a small negative value, so the circle is drawn complete.
  - Thin the markers on the solid curve (plot every second or third point) and, in the R ≲ 8 region where the curves coincide, either offset the dashed curve by a hair or drop its markers, so the two series remain separable.
  - Align both panel labels flush to the same left margin at the top of their respective axes.
  - Reclaim ~half the inter-panel gap and give the height to panel (b), which will also relieve the crowding around the minimum.
  - Consider removing the four horizontal gridlines; the left-hand ticks already carry the vertical scale, and the curves are read as shapes rather than as precise dB values.

### figure4.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The overall ink economy is high (thin curves, faint horizontal gridlines that sit well below the data in weight, no 3D, no shadows, no fills). Two recoverable items: (1) all four panels carry a complete four-sided bounding box; the top and right spines encode nothing and can be erased (or reduced to Tufte range-frames). (2) Panels (c) and (d) carry multi-entry legend boxes that duplicate information available by direct labeling — in (d) the two vertical reference lines are already self-explanatory if labeled in place, exactly as panel (b) labels its ±R/2 lines.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — 2×2 grid reads left→right, top→bottom, and the logic (tapers → individual kernels → running sum → concentration ladder) follows that order. Panels (b) and (c) share the same abscissa (fNΔ, −8 to 8), which supports vertical comparison; (a)→(b) transition from index k to frequency is signposted by the axis labels.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Top and right spines on all four panels (non-data ink).
- Seven-entry, three-column legend in (c) occupying a large block of the plot interior.
- Two-entry legend in (d) for what are simply two annotated vertical reference lines.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate overall, locally excessive — In (b) roughly half the plotting area (|fNΔ| > 4) and the upper third (above 0.5) contain no data. The shared x-range with (c) is a legitimate reason to keep it, but the range could be trimmed to ±6 in both panels without losing the out-of-band comparison. (c) likewise has empty upper region, which the legend usefully occupies.

**AXES:** OK — (a) is symmetric about zero and includes it; (b) and (c) begin at zero on y; (d) uses a log ordinate for a quantity spanning 10⁻¹⁰–10⁰, which is scientifically justified, and begins the abscissa at i = 1 because i is a 1-indexed taper number. No inverted axes. Minor: in (d) the curve and the final marker appear to run into the right spine — extend the x-limit to ~17.5 so the last point is not clipped by the frame. Also consider whether (a)'s y-limits of ±0.20 are needed when the data span only ±0.13; ±0.15 would remove dead band.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate and explicitly justified ("Every curve is computed exactly, and nothing is simulated"). No action needed; the caption already forestalls the reviewer question.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(d) all present, all upper-left, same parenthesized lowercase style and size. They are not bold; that is acceptable if consistent, but bolding would improve scanability at print width. Note that (a) and (c) labels sit outboard of the y-axis label while (b) and (d) sit just inside their panels' left margin — align all four to the same offset from the panel's top-left corner.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — each subpanel is modestly wider than tall (~1.35:1), suitable for these spectral/frequency traces; (d), a monotone ladder in an index, also reads well at this ratio.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — The −R/2 and R/2 labels in (b) are exemplary: unboxed, unfilled, set directly atop their dotted reference lines, outside the data. No overlap, no competing borders. This is the pattern the other panels should imitate.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box (three panels) — (a) three-entry legend, lower-left interior, no border/fill (good) but placed in a region traversed by the descending limb of the light-blue h⁽⁷⁾ trace; at publication scale the first swatch/label row (≈ y = −0.08, k ≈ 30–60) risks near-collision with that curve. (b) three-entry legend, upper-right, in genuinely empty space — no overlap. (c) seven-entry, three-column legend spanning much of the panel's upper half; it does not overlap data (the curves are near zero there) but it is a large lookup table for what is a simple monotone darkening sequence. (d) two-entry legend, lower-right, in empty space — no overlap, but redundant with in-place labeling.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Legend in (a) is positioned where the h⁽⁷⁾ curve descends through y ≈ −0.05 to −0.07 around k ≈ 30–60; the topmost legend row may clip or crowd that trace when the figure is reduced to single-column width.
- [ADVISORY] Detached legends on line charts in (a), (b), (c), (d) impose a lookup cost that inline labeling would eliminate.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (d)'s legend describes two vertical reference lines; these are better labeled in place, matching the ±R/2 treatment in (b). Current inconsistency between panels for the same annotation task.
- [ADVISORY] Non-data top and right spines on all four panels.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b): ~50% of the plotting area and the top third are empty; panel (a) has ~35% dead vertical band from over-wide y-limits.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (d): terminal data marker at i = 17 abuts/overruns the right spine.
- [ADVISORY] Panel labels (a)/(c) and (b)/(d) are not on a common left offset.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace (a)'s legend with inline labels colored to match each trace: h⁽¹⁾ just above its peak at k ≈ 128, h⁽²⁾ beside its positive lobe near k ≈ 95, h⁽⁷⁾ beside its left-hand peak near k ≈ 20. This clears the lower-left region entirely and removes the near-collision risk.
- Replace (b)'s legend with i = 1, i = 2, i = 3 set directly above each series' tallest lobe (fNΔ ≈ 0, ±1.2, ±1.9), in the matching blue. Stagger vertically so the three labels do not align at one height.
- In (c), keep a compact legend only for the two qualitatively different curves — "Box" (dashed) and "L = N" (heavy black) — and label the blue family with a single inline annotation such as "L = 1, 3, 5, 7, 9 (light→dark)" placed beside the L = 1 curve, or label just L = 1 and L = 9 at their peaks with an arrow-free "increasing L" note. This collapses seven legend rows to two or three.
- In (d), delete the legend and set "L = 2α − 1 = 7" and "2α = 8" vertically along or atop their respective reference lines, mirroring panel (b). Use short thin leader lines in the matching gray/black if the two labels crowd each other.
- Erase the top and right spines on all panels (or adopt range-frames terminating at the data extrema). Keep the faint horizontal gridlines as they are — they are correctly subordinate to the curves.
- Trim (b) and (c) x-limits to ±6 to recover empty margin while preserving the shared abscissa and the visible out-of-band decay; reduce (a)'s y-limits to ±0.15.
- Extend (d)'s x-limit to ~17.5 so the final marker is not clipped by the frame.
- Bold the (a)–(d) labels and align all four to an identical offset from the top-left corner of the axes.
- Caption: it is unusually informative and quantitative (3% top depression, 1/(NΔ) edge rounding) — retain. Add one clause stating that curve darkness encodes increasing L in (c) and increasing i in (a)/(b), so the color ordering is documented in text even where inline labels are abbreviated.

### figure5.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Good — open (two-spine) axes, no frames, no gridlines, no fills or shadows, flat 2-D rendering of 2-D data. Only minor redundancy: the identical 0.00–0.20 y-tick labels are repeated on all four bottom panels (e–h) although the scale is shared, and "k"/"i" axis labels are repeated four times per row.

READING FLOW:     Natural — top row = taper banks, bottom row = corresponding weight ladders, with column-wise correspondence reinforced by consistent per-method colour. One local exception: the two-column legends in (b) and (d) are filled column-major (i=1, i=2 | i=3, i=4), so a reader scanning left→right encounters 1, 3, 2, 4 and must backtrack.

CHARTJUNK:
  - None identified in the strict sense (no ornament, no 3-D, no shadows).
  - Borderline: four repeated y-tick-label sets in the bottom row carry no new information.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, with locally excessive void — panels (e) and (h) have bars only to i=7, leaving roughly the right 40% of each panel empty. This is defensible (common x-range aids cross-panel comparison of ladder length/flatness) but should be stated in the caption so it reads as information rather than waste. The top strip of (b) is the opposite problem: title + 4-entry legend + plateau of the dark curve are compressed into a narrow band.

AXES:             OK — bottom row all begin at zero on a common 0–0.20 scale (correct for normalized weights); top-row y-limits differ because the quantities differ, and each includes zero where the curve is signed. No inverted axes. Two small issues: (i) bottom-row x-axes are ticked only at 5 and 10, so the reader cannot immediately confirm the first bar is i=1; (ii) top-row x-ticks (0, 128, 256) are very sparse for reading the shift positions quoted in the (a) and (c) annotations.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate, the caption states every curve and weight is computed exactly.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, parenthesized, same size/weight, top-left and outside the axes in every panel.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — each panel is close to square, suitable for these symmetric window shapes and short bar ladders; the 4×2 grid uses the page width efficiently.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — all annotations ("shifts n=0,32,…,192", "centred at k=−128,0,128,256", "ν=13.4" etc.) are unboxed, unfilled inline text placed in empty regions; none overlaps data. The ν labels in (e–h) sit in genuinely empty upper-right space and are a good example of annotation replacing a table.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box (frameless) — (b) and (d) use detached 4-entry keys; (a) and (c) use inline prose annotations instead. Two concerns: (1) the strategy is inconsistent across a row of otherwise parallel panels; (2) in (b) the legend block sits immediately above the dark-brown plateau at h≈0.068 — at reduced publication width the descenders of the top legend row and the plateau will nearly touch, and the lightest entry (f′NΔ=3, pale cream) is hard to distinguish from the page. No current overlap of data, so this is advisory rather than blocking.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Legends in (b) and (d) are column-major, producing a 1, 3, 2, 4 left-to-right reading order.
  - [ADVISORY] In (b), the legend's lower edge is very close to the plateau of the f′NΔ=0 curve; clearance will shrink on scaling.
  - [ADVISORY] Lightest shades in (b) and (c) (pale cream / pale green) have low contrast against white and may vanish in print or on projection.
  - [ADVISORY] Inconsistent labelling strategy within the top row: (a) and (c) annotate parameters as text, (b) and (d) use legends.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant y-tick labels on (f), (g), (h); redundant axis labels repeated four times per row.
  - [ADVISORY] Bottom-row x-axes lack a tick at i=1, leaving the index origin implicit.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (e) and (h) carry ~40% empty plot area; the reason (shared x-range for comparability) is not stated.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Replace the (b) and (d) legends with direct inline labels coloured to match each curve: in (b) put "f′NΔ = 0, 1, 2, 3" at each curve's first peak or at the right edge; in (d) label i=1…4 at each taper's principal lobe, staggered vertically, with a 1–2 pt leader line where lobes crowd. This removes the lookup cost and frees the crowded top strip.
  - If the legends are retained, set them row-major (matplotlib: pass the handles/labels reordered, or use `ncol=2` with pre-transposed lists) and nudge the (b) legend up or move it to the upper-left gap outside the plateau.
  - Darken the lightest tints by ~15–20% (or switch to a perceptually uniform 4-step sequential ramp such as the last four steps of a ColorBrewer/viridis-style ramp) so all four bank members remain distinguishable in greyscale and at small size.
  - Harmonize the top row: give (a) and (c) the same labelling device as (b) and (d) — inline per-curve labels for the shift/centre values — so the four panels read as one family.
  - In the bottom row, drop y-tick labels on (f), (g), (h) (keep the ticks) and keep a single "Weight cᵢ/Σcᵢ" label on (e), as is already done; keep only one "i" label per row if the panels are tightly abutted, or retain all four if they are visually separated.
  - Add a tick (or minor tick plus label) at i=1 on the bottom-row x-axes.
  - Add one clause to the caption: "(e–h) share a common axis range i = 1…12 so that ladder length is directly comparable," converting the empty area in (e) and (h) into an explicit datum.
  - Optionally thin the top-row x-ticks to 0, 64, 128, 192, 256 in (a) and (c) so the quoted shift positions can be read off the axis.

### figure6.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — The plotting frames appear to be open (left/bottom spines only), gridlines are light and subordinate to the data, and there are no fills, shadows, or 3D effects. Excellent baseline. Two redundancies remain: (i) y-axis tick labels are repeated on all four panels of each row even though all panels in a row share an identical scale (60/40/20/0/−20/−40 top; 0/−50/−100/−150 bottom) — Tufte would keep them only on the leftmost panel and keep bare ticks elsewhere; (ii) the three inset y labels (0, −25, −50) and the three inset x labels (0.39, 0.4, 0.41) are repeated four times in the bottom row. Removing the repeats in panels (f)–(h) and relying on the (e) inset as the reference would recover meaningful white space around the kernel skirts.

READING FLOW:     Natural — Left→right across methods, top→bottom from estimate to kernel, with each column forming a coherent method-wise unit. The vertical pairing (a)↔(e), (b)↔(f), etc., is immediately legible. Minor friction: the top row carries x tick numbers but no axis title, so the reader must glance down two rows to find "fΔ"; this is acceptable in a shared-axis layout but could be signalled by dropping the top-row tick labels entirely.

CHARTJUNK:
  - None substantive. Arrows with "22 dB" / "14 dB" are data-bearing annotations, not ornament.
  - Inset connector lines are thin gray and functionally necessary (they define the magnification region) — acceptable.
  - Inset bounding rectangles are the only enclosing boxes in the figure; they are justified as frames for a different coordinate system, but they could be reduced to two spines each for consistency with the main panels.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, with locally excessive dead area — In (a)–(d) the y-range extends to −40 dB although no curve descends below about −22 dB; roughly the bottom fifth of each top panel is empty (used in (a) only for the "True PSD" text). In (e) and (f) the kernels never fall below about −60 dB, leaving the lower half of those panels blank; the shared −150 dB limit is defensible for cross-panel comparison but costs substantial real estate in half the row.

AXES:             OK — Zero origin does not apply: both ordinates are decibel (log-ratio) scales, and the abscissa fΔ does begin at 0 and ends at the Nyquist 0.5, which is field-standard. No inverted axes. Shared limits within each row make the four methods directly comparable — correct choice. One inconsistency: inset x tick labels mix significant figures (0.39, 0.4, 0.41); use 0.390/0.400/0.410 or 0.39/0.40/0.41.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — No error bars are drawn, and none are expected for single-realization spectral estimates. Uncertainty is instead conveyed by the equivalent degrees of freedom ν printed in each top panel, but ν is never defined as such in the caption. Because ν is the figure's only uncertainty statement, it must be explicitly defined (e.g., "ν, equivalent degrees of freedom of the estimate") — otherwise the figure carries an undefined uncertainty quantity.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(h) present on every panel, uniformly parenthesized, same weight and size, all at upper-left outside the plotting area. No action needed.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — Panels are close to square, which suits the frequency-vs-level curves (the spectral peaks near fΔ ≈ 0.11–0.14 and the high-frequency plateaus are both resolvable). The 2×4 arrangement at ~2077×1097 px gives a sensible overall landscape shape. No change needed.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean, with two placement issues — The "22 dB" and "14 dB" double-headed arrows are exemplary: unboxed, unfilled, color-neutral, and placed in empty regions; they directly measure the vertical gap between estimate and true PSD and could not be replaced by anything simpler. Two concerns: (1) "True PSD" in (a) sits at about −40 dB in the lower-left corner, well separated from the black curve it names (the black curve is near −20 dB at that abscissa and near +10 dB at f=0); the reader must guess the referent. (2) In (e) and (f) the inset rectangle's lower edge and its x tick labels come close to the main kernel trace, which runs at roughly −50 dB across that region; at final publication width the inset frame or its "0.39/0.4/0.41" labels may visually collide with the curve.

LEGEND STYLE:     Inline — clean — No detached legend box anywhere. Each top panel is labeled in situ with a color-matched two-line annotation ("Untapered, then box / ν = 17.2", etc.), which eliminates the lookup cost entirely; this is exactly the Tufte-preferred solution. The labels sit in the empty upper-right of each panel and do not touch the traces. The only series lacking an inline label at its own location is the black true PSD (see ANNOTATION BOXES).

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] ν appears in all four top panels as the figure's sole uncertainty measure but is nowhere defined in the caption (SD/SEM/CI analogue). State explicitly that ν is the equivalent number of degrees of freedom and how it was computed.
  - [ADVISORY] "True PSD" label in (a) is spatially detached from the black curve; referent is ambiguous.
  - [ADVISORY] Inset frame and inset x tick labels in (e) and (f) risk clipping/overlapping the main kernel trace near −50 dB at reduced publication width.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant y-axis tick labels on panels (b)–(d) and (f)–(h); redundant inset tick labels on (f)–(h).
  - [ADVISORY] Unused vertical range: bottom ~20% of (a)–(d) and bottom ~50% of (e)–(f) contain no data.
  - [ADVISORY] Top row has x tick labels but no axis title, duplicating information already carried by the bottom row.
  - [ADVISORY] Inconsistent decimal formatting on inset x ticks (0.39, 0.4, 0.41).

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add to the caption: "ν is the equivalent number of degrees of freedom of each estimate." Also state the smoothing bandwidth convention used for the half-power widths so ν and R are self-contained.
  - Move "True PSD" to sit immediately beside the black curve — e.g., just above it near fΔ ≈ 0.30–0.35 in panel (a), where the black trace is isolated from the green estimate — or attach a short thin black leader line from the existing text to the curve.
  - Nudge the insets in (e) and (f) upward (or shrink them ~10%) so at least one label-height of clear space separates the inset's bottom edge from the −50 dB kernel trace; verify at final single-column or full-width scale.
  - Delete y tick labels from panels (b)–(d) and (f)–(h), retaining bare tick marks; delete inset tick labels from (f)–(h) and note in the caption that all insets share the axes of (e).
  - Delete x tick labels from the top row and let the bottom-row "fΔ" axis serve both rows in each column; this also visually binds each (a)–(e) pair.
  - Consider raising the top-row lower limit from −40 to −30 dB and, if cross-row comparison permits, clipping the bottom-row range to −140 dB; this enlarges the curves without changing any comparison.
  - Reduce inset frames to left and bottom spines only, matching the open-frame style of the main panels.
  - Harmonize inset x tick labels to two decimals (0.39, 0.40, 0.41).

### figure7.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work (minor) — The plotting is admirably lean: light horizontal gridlines, no fills, no 3D, no shadows, shared x-axis with labels only on the lower panel. Two small issues: (i) the legend in (b) restates two series already color-coded and named in (a), duplicating data ink; (ii) the grey-band annotation duplicates text already in the caption ("the grey band is ±1.6 dB"). Confirm that panels carry only left/bottom spines — any top/right box rule should be erased.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic (mildly) — Flow within each panel is left→right and (a)→(b) top→bottom, which is correct. However, the legend in (a) is at top-right while the legend in (b) is at bottom-left: the eye must zig-zag diagonally across the figure to reconcile the same three color codes. Additionally, the reader must jump from the grey-band annotation (top of (b)) down to the band itself (centre of (b)).

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Second legend in (b) is largely redundant with the legend in (a) (same two colors, same two names, plus "− multitaper").
- Explanatory sentence inside (b) repeats caption text; a two-word inline label on the band would suffice.
- No other ornamentation identified.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate overall, but unevenly distributed — panel (a) is crowded between 0–15 Hz where the three curves overplot at ~20 harmonics, while panel (b) has a large empty region below −5 dB that is filled only by the legend. Panel (a)'s upper-right region is the only genuine white space and it is consumed by the legend.

**AXES:** OK — (a) y-axis not starting at zero is fully justified (dB, i.e., log-ratio scale). (b) is a difference axis centred on zero with an explicit zero reference line: correct. x-axis begins at 0 Hz. No inverted axes. Minor: (b)'s ±15 dB range is ~40 % wider than the data (−5.2 to +12.4 dB); tightening to ±13 dB (or asymmetric −7/+13) would enlarge the plotted signal, provided the legend is relocated.

**ERROR BARS:** Defined — uncertainty is shown as a ±1.6 dB grey band, explicitly identified as the standard deviation of the multitaper estimate in both annotation and caption. This satisfies the "define your uncertainty" requirement. State in the caption whether ±1.6 dB is 1 SD of a *single* frequency ordinate (it appears to be) so readers do not read it as a confidence band on the difference curves.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a) and (b) both present, both top-left outside the axes, same size, same parenthetical style.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Needs adjustment — the overall figure is portrait (986 × 1226). Each individual panel is landscape, which is right for a spectrum, but panel (a) must resolve ~20 harmonic peaks and three near-coincident traces across 40 Hz in ~830 px; at single-column publication width the three curves will be visually inseparable below 15 Hz. Consider a wider (1.5-column or full-width) layout, giving panel (a) a ~2.2:1 aspect and panel (b) ~2.8:1, or reduce (b)'s height since it carries less structure.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — the two-line note "Grey band: ±1.6 dB, the standard deviation of the multitaper estimate" is unboxed (good, no competing border or fill) but it is a floating block of prose inside the data region. Its lower edge sits close to the 12.4 dB pink spike near 9 Hz; at reduced print size this will read as a near-collision, and any small change in data or font metrics will make it a true overlap. It is also redundant with the caption.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box (two of them) — (a): three-entry legend at top-right, sitting over empty space, no frame, no data overlap: acceptable. Because the three traces are nearly coincident over most of the band, direct inline labeling of (a) is genuinely impractical, so the legend is defensible here — but it should be the *only* legend in the figure. (b): two-entry legend at bottom-left duplicating colors already established in (a); with only two curves this is a prime candidate for inline labeling or outright deletion.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Annotation text in (b) approaches the 12.4 dB pink peak at ~9 Hz; risk of clipping/overlap at final publication width.
- [ADVISORY] Annotation text duplicates caption content and occupies prime data real estate.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant second legend in (b); color key is already defined in (a).
- [ADVISORY] Legend positions alternate corners across panels (top-right, then bottom-left), forcing a diagonal eye path.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) y-range is ~40 % larger than the data range, wasting vertical space and compressing the difference structure.
- [ADVISORY] Portrait aspect compresses panel (a); three overlapping traces across 20 harmonics will merge at single-column width.
- [ADVISORY] Verify the zero reference line in (b) is visually lighter than the data curves — a black dashed rule can compete with the two colored traces where they cross zero repeatedly.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete the legend in (b). Label the two difference curves inline at their right-hand endpoints (x ≈ 38–40 Hz), where both run within ±3 dB and are separable: "cosine + parabola" in orange, "Welch" in pink, each color-matched, staggered vertically (e.g., +3 dB and −3 dB) with a short thin leader line in the series color if needed. Note in the caption that both are differences from the multitaper estimate.
- Replace the two-line grey-band note with a compact inline label placed immediately to the right of the band at the plot's right margin, outside the data envelope: "±1.6 dB (1 SD, multitaper)". This removes the near-collision at 9 Hz and the caption duplication in one move.
- Keep the legend in (a) but move it to a fixed, consistent corner and confirm ≥1 line-width clearance from the 20–40 Hz traces; alternatively, since the three curves separate visibly in the valleys around 27–30 Hz, consider three short color-matched inline labels there with leader lines and remove the legend entirely.
- Tighten panel (b) to ±13 dB (or −7/+13 dB) once the legend is removed, and reduce its height relative to (a) — (b) has far less fine structure to resolve.
- Re-render at 1.5-column or full-page width so panel (a)'s harmonic peaks and the blue/orange/pink separation survive reduction; target ~2:1 or wider per panel.
- Reduce the zero line in (b) to a thin grey (e.g., 0.5 pt, 40 % black) so it recedes behind the data, and drop the 0 dB gridline in (b) to avoid doubling it.
- Confirm only left and bottom spines are drawn on both panels; erase any top/right rules.

### figure8.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — the core of this figure is excellent (three spectrograms on one shared colour scale, no 3D, no shadows, faint gridlines subordinate to the data, unboxed annotation in (h)). Redundancies to erase: (i) the x-tick labels on (c) and (e) repeat (g) exactly on an identical, vertically aligned axis; (ii) the string "PSD (dB re 1 μV²/Hz)" appears three times (colourbar, (d) y-axis, (f) y-axis); (iii) panel (a)'s grey seizure patch is a filled block where a thin bracket or a labelled span rule under the trace would carry the same information with less ink over the waveform.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — two issues. (1) Panel (a) spans the full figure width while (c,e,g) span only the left column, so the time axes do not align; the reader cannot drop a vertical line from the seizure in (a) into the spectrograms, which is the single most important comparison in the figure. (2) The shared colourbar is parked in the middle gutter, so the eye must cross a non-data strip on every left→right hop (c→d, e→f, g→h). The a,b / c,d / e,f / g,h zigzag itself is natural.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Filled grey rectangle behind the ictal segment in (a) (replaceable by a span bracket or two thin vertical rules).
  - Duplicated x-tick labels on (c) and (e).
  - Triple repetition of the PSD unit string.
  - Nothing else: no borders beyond the axes, no 3D, no shadows, no decorative fills.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, with two local problems — the strip between the colourbar and panels (d)/(f) is wasted, and the top-right region beside (b) is underused while (b) itself is compressed. Panel (f) is mildly crowded by the four-entry legend sitting above the spectral peak.

AXES:             Issues — (1) time axes of (a) and (c,e,g) are not co-registered (see flow); (2) panel (b) is plotted on relative time 0–6 s although it is excerpted from t = 318 s, breaking the link to (a) and (f); relabel as 318–324 s; (3) (c) and (e) carry tick labels but no axis title while (g) carries the title — acceptable as a shared axis, but then the intermediate tick labels should go; (4) non-zero y-origins in (d), (f) and the colourbar are legitimate (dB ratio scale); (h) correctly starts at zero counts. Frequency axes start at ~0.5 Hz rather than 0 — worth a one-clause note in the caption.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — the caption gives ν for every estimator (2, 6, 8.7, 7.7), so the reader has the ingredients for a confidence interval but is shown none. Not blocking, but a single χ²-based 95% CI bar drawn in (d) and (f) would convert the ν values from prose into visual fact and directly justify the smoothing comparison.

PANEL LABELS:     Inconsistent — all eight are present and uniformly styled "(x)" at upper left, but (a) and (b) sit *inside* the axes while (c)–(h) sit *outside*, in the left margin. Pick one convention.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — wide-and-short spectrograms and time series, near-square spectra and histogram. Only (b) is narrower than ideal for a 6-s waveform excerpt; widening it (using the spare space at top right) would make the ictal rhythm more legible.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — the "median |difference| 0.9 dB" note in (h) is borderless, fill-free, sits in empty space, and is anchored by the zero reference line; this is the model case. The ▼(d)/▼(f) markers above (a) are compact and unboxed. Only gap: there is no ▼ marking the (b) excerpt in (a), so (b)'s provenance is prose-only.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — a four-entry detached legend in the upper region of (f). It appears borderless and does not overlap the plotted curves (peak ≈ 37 dB, legend occupies ≈ 42–75 dB), so it is not blocking. With four mutually overlapping smoothed spectra, inline endpoint labelling would collide at 30 Hz, so a key is defensible here; however it is currently costing the most crowded panel its headroom. Consider relocating it to the empty upper-right of (d), or to the figure's unused top-right gutter, and keeping the colours/line weights identical so the single key serves both (d) and (f).

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) and panels (c,e,g) do not share a common horizontal time scale or x-extent; the primary visual comparison (waveform → spectrogram) requires mental rescaling.
  - [ADVISORY] Shared colourbar placed in the central gutter, splitting the left and right columns and forcing the eye across non-data ink at every row.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant x-tick labels on (c) and (e) duplicate (g).
  - [ADVISORY] "PSD (dB re 1 μV²/Hz)" printed three times; the colourbar label also sits immediately beside (f)'s identical y-axis label, inviting misattribution of the colourbar to panel (f).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) uses relative time (0–6 s) instead of absolute time (318–324 s), and is not marked in (a).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel-label convention differs between (a,b) (inside axes) and (c)–(h) (outside axes).
  - [ADVISORY] Grey fill behind the seizure in (a) overlays the waveform; a bracket or paired rules would preserve the trace.
  - [ADVISORY] No confidence intervals shown despite ν being reported for all four estimators.
  - [ADVISORY] Four-entry legend occupies the free headroom of the busiest spectral panel, (f).

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-lay the figure so (a) occupies exactly the left-column width and shares one time axis with (c), (e), (g); stack (a), (c), (e), (g) as a single axis-linked column and extend the dashed instant markers as thin rules through all four.
  - Move the colourbar out of the gutter: either a slim horizontal bar directly beneath (g), or flush against the right edge of the spectrogram column, so left and right columns become visually independent.
  - Delete x-tick labels on (c) and (e); keep ticks only, with "Time (s)" once under (g).
  - Keep the unit string once on the colourbar and shorten (d)/(f) y-labels to "PSD (dB)"; remove the remaining duplicate.
  - Replot (b) on absolute time 318–324 s, widen it into the spare top-right space, and add a ▼(b) marker (or a short span rule) in (a).
  - Move all panel labels outside the axes at upper left, same bold weight and size.
  - Replace (a)'s grey block with a thin horizontal bracket beneath the trace labelled "seizure", or two light dashed verticals at onset and offset.
  - Add one χ²(ν) 95% CI bar per estimator (or a single bar annotated with the four ν values) in a blank corner of (d); this makes the ν=2 → 8.7 progression visible rather than merely stated.
  - Relocate the legend to the empty upper-right of (d) and note in the caption that it applies to both (d) and (f).
  - State in the caption that the spectrograms are drawn from ~0.5 to 30 Hz, so the omission of the 0 Hz bin is documented.

### figure9.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — the core design is economical (one shared colour bar for six spectrograms, inline series labels in (a) and (h), no 3-D effects, no shadows, no background fills), but there is recoverable non-data ink:
- x-tick labels `0 10 20 30 40` are repeated on all six spectrogram panels even though the panels are column-aligned and share an identical time axis. Keep them on the bottom row (f, g) only, as you already correctly did for the "Time (s)" axis title.
- The `jet` colour map itself is a data-ink problem: its non-monotonic luminance manufactures sharp false contours (particularly the cyan→yellow and yellow→red transitions), so ink appears where no gradient exists in the data. This is the single largest visual-fidelity issue in the figure.
- Low-frequency power is clearly clipped to saturated dark red across the bottom of every panel; that region carries no information at present.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — (a) across the top, (b–g) paired left/right by estimator with resolution increasing downward, (h) as a summary at the bottom. The colour bar on the far right requires one lateral jump but it is shared and correctly placed after the panels it serves. One missed opportunity: the five shaded spindles in (a) are not carried down into (b–g), so the reader must eyeball time coordinates between panels.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- None decorative. Gridlines in (h) are light grey and subordinate to the markers — correct weight.
- Borderline: the full bounding box on each spectrogram is justified (it delimits the image extent), so not flagged.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate overall, locally excessive — the band to the right of panel (a) and above the colour bar, and the region right of (f, g) below the colour bar, are empty. In (h), the left third of the axis (0.40–0.75 Hz) is empty except for the bottom row, and there is an unused strip below the "4 s" row. (h) could be ~25 % shorter and the colour bar lengthened or the whole block tightened.

**AXES:** Issues —
- (h) x-axis begins at ≈0.40 Hz rather than 0. Half-power width is a ratio-like positive quantity and the comparison of interest is between paired markers, so a non-zero origin is defensible, but it is not currently documented. State it in the caption or extend to 0.
- (a) y-axis is labelled EEG (µV) but the grey 11–16 Hz trace is arbitrarily offset, so the axis values are invalid for it. This is a silent axis violation.
- Frequency axes correctly start at 0; y-tick labels correctly suppressed on the right column.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — none required; the ν values serve as the variance statement.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, all upper-left, outside the axes, same size and parenthetical style.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — wide-and-short for the time series and spectrograms is correct for time-on-x data; (h) is a horizontal dot plot with adequate row spacing, though slightly taller than its data require.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable / overlapping — the white resolution rectangles are an elegant device (window length × achieved width, area ≈ ν/2) and are consistently placed at upper-left in all six panels. However:
- In (b) the white text "resolution of the panel" lies directly on the spectrogram and obscures data in the 20–24 Hz band from ~2–14 s. Since the same information is in the caption, consider moving this label into the panel title line or the margin, or shrinking it.
- In (h), `α = 1, L = 1: small-α regime` floats mid-row between the two markers and reads as if it annotates the connecting line rather than the row; anchor it left-aligned just right of the blue marker or place it above the row label.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Inline — clean, with crowding — no detached legend box anywhere; "Multitaper" (blue) and "Cosine taper, then parabola" (orange) are inline and colour-matched to their markers, and (a) labels its two traces inline in matching black/grey. Correct Tufte practice. Two crowding risks: in the "4 s, R = 1 Hz" row the labels `0.8 Hz / ν = 6.0` and `1.0 Hz / ν = 8.7` are only ~0.2 Hz apart and their text blocks nearly touch; and the two colour-key words sit immediately beneath that same row, so three label groups stack in one small region. At final column width this area will read as a single grey mass.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] `jet` colour map introduces perceptually false banding; six panels are compared on this scale, so the artefact is systematic.
- [ADVISORY] Dark-red saturation at low frequency indicates out-of-range values with no indication of clipping on the colour bar.
- [ADVISORY] White annotation text in (b) overlaps plotted spectrogram data.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant x-tick labels on panels (b–e).
- [ADVISORY] (h) x-axis non-zero origin undocumented.
- [ADVISORY] Grey trace in (a) is offset but shares a labelled µV axis.
- [ADVISORY] Label crowding in the "4 s" row of (h) plus adjacent colour key.
- [ADVISORY] Shaded spindle intervals in (a) are not linked to (b–g).
- [ADVISORY] Empty regions upper-right and lower-right of the colour bar; unused left third of (h).

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace `jet` with a perceptually uniform sequential map (viridis, magma, or a scientific map such as `batlow`); the spindle band at 11–16 Hz will remain legible and false contours disappear.
- Add triangular extend caps to the colour bar (or raise the upper limit) to declare the clipped low-frequency power.
- Drop x-tick labels from (b), (c), (d), (e); retain them on (f) and (g) only.
- Move "resolution of the panel" out of the image area — append it to the (b) title line, e.g. "Multitaper, L = 1: 1 s by 1.1 Hz, ν = 2.0 (white box: panel resolution)".
- In (a), either remove the y-axis tick for the offset trace region or annotate "offset for display; amplitude scale as above"; alternatively give the filtered trace its own short scale bar.
- Add small grey tick marks or narrow shaded strips at the top edge of (b–g) at the five spindle times so the reader can track the same events across resolutions.
- In (h): left-align the "α = 1, L = 1" note near the blue marker; stagger the `0.8 Hz` label above and `1.0 Hz` below their markers (or add short colour-matched leader lines); move the two colour-key words up into the top row beside their first markers so they are not stacked under the crowded 4 s row.
- Either state the non-zero origin justification in the caption ("x-axis truncated at 0.40 Hz; widths are strictly positive") or extend to 0.
- Reduce the height of (h) and tighten the right margin so the colour bar spans the (b)–(g) block, reclaiming the empty upper- and lower-right corners.
