## Color & Accessibility Auditor

---
PER-FIGURE:

**efigure1.png** (4×4 taper-bank grid)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — one hue per column/estimator (pink = Welch, gold = cosine+parabola, green = sliding sinc, blue = multitaper), with within-hue lightness ramps encoding taper index *i*. No decorative fill.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Borderline/Good — 4 distinct hues, but each is a genuine categorical level and hue is redundant with column position and panel title. Acceptable.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk (mild) — pink vs. gold and green vs. gold converge under deuteranopia/protanopia. Risk is mitigated because hue never carries a within-panel distinction (columns are spatially separated and titled). The lightness ramps within each hue are monotone and survive achromatopsia, though the lightest members (i=4) in (b), (c), (h) are close to the white background and will be very faint in grayscale.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good — reads as a hand-tuned but principled Okabe-Ito-like set. Recommend darkening the lightest ramp step (or switching each column to a 4-step sequential ramp from ColorBrewer Blues/Greens/Oranges/PuRd) so all four members retain contrast against white.

**efigure2.png** (AR(4) estimates and residuals)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — every colour is one estimator; grey = periodogram, black = truth.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Borderline — 4 hues plus three luminance levels of blue plus grey plus black = 7 visually distinct strokes in one axes. Panel (a) relies on a 7-entry legend.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk — (i) green ("Untapered, then box") vs. orange ("Cosine taper, then parabola") is the headline comparison of the panel and is the classic deuteranope collision; (ii) mid-blue (L=7) vs. dark blue (L=7 adaptive) differ mainly in luminance and overlap heavily in (b), where they are the two curves the reader must separate.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good base, needs disambiguation — keep the hues but add line-style redundancy (adaptive = dashed) and, as in (b), direct end-labels in (a) too, so the legend is not load-bearing.

**efigure3.png** (kernels at matched resolution)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — one colour per kernel; dotted black = ideal box reference.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Borderline — 5 series (blue, green, orange, grey, light blue) + black reference. Dashing of Bohman helps.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk (mild) — green vs. orange again; light blue (Bohman) vs. blue (multitaper) rely on luminance only, though the dash pattern rescues them. Direct end-labels in (b) are excellent and should be copied into (a), which currently depends on a 5-entry legend.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good — consistent with the rest of the paper.

**efigure4.png** (ν, leakage, side-lobe vs. resolution)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful, but overloaded — 9 series encoded by hue × line style × marker shape × marker fill (filled = "no flat top"). Fill state carries a fifth, independent meaning.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Excessive — 6 hues (blue, green, grey/dark grey, light blue, orange, pink) plus three dash patterns in a single legend. This is the least readable figure in the set.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk — green vs. orange; blue vs. light blue; grey vs. dark grey (Hann-then-box vs. Hann-then-Gaussian) is essentially a luminance-only distinction. Marker shapes (circle/square/triangle/diamond) do provide genuine achromatopsia-safe redundancy, which is the figure's saving grace.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work — consider grouping: all multitaper variants one hue with three dash/marker styles, all "taper-then-smooth" variants a second hue, Welch a third, and drop or grey out the Hann-then-Gaussian curve. Also label a few curves directly in (b) and (c) rather than forcing legend round-trips across panels.

**figure1.png** (three routes to one estimate)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful and unusually well-motivated — the two greens are deliberately chosen to signal "one estimator, two routes," and the caption states this.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — 1 accent (blue) + a two-step green family + black dotted guide.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes — blue vs. green survives all three simulations; the two greens are separated by large luminance difference and by line style (dashed vs. dotted) and are in any case separated vertically by ~12 decades in (b).
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good — exemplary restraint; direct in-plot labelling in (b).

**figure2.png** (motif: kernel + noise)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — grey = untapered periodogram, orange = cosine-tapered estimates, black = truth/kernels.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — one accent hue, everything else neutral. Best-in-set.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes — orange vs. grey vs. black is robust under all three deficiency types and in grayscale.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good.

**figure3.png** (choosing the resolution)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — orange = tapered record / taper accent; black = totals; grey = bias and noise components.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — one accent.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good. Minor: in (b) the two grey component curves ("Bias", "Noise") and the grey annotation leaders/dotted guides are the same grey; consider a slightly lighter grey for guides so data and annotation are separable.

**figure4.png** (why the multitaper kernel is a box)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — sequential blue lightness encodes taper index in (a)/(b) and L in (c); black = the L=N limit; dashed = box reference.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — single hue family, ordinal encoding matched to an ordinal variable. Textbook use.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes — monotone lightness ramp is safe under all deficiencies and in grayscale. The lightest step (L=1, h⁽⁷⁾) is faint against white; darken slightly.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good — consider an explicit sequential palette (Blues or viridis truncated) so the lightness steps are perceptually even.

**figure5.png** (three ways to build a bank)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — same column-hue scheme as efigure1; bar colours in (e–h) match the curve colours above them.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Borderline/Good — 4 hues, one per route, redundant with position and title.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk (mild) — same green/gold/pink convergence as efigure1; again mitigated by spatial separation. Within-panel lightness ramps are safe.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good.

**figure6.png** (the four estimators in practice)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — one hue per estimator, and the panel titles are set in the matching colour, which is effectively direct labelling. Kernels in (e–h) are deliberately black since they are not method-contrasted within a panel. Well designed.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — 4 hues across four separate panels, i.e. one colour per axes.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    Yes, effectively — because no two coloured curves ever share an axes, the green/orange and orange/pink collisions never have to be resolved by eye. The coloured titles are the only place hue matters, and the text itself is redundant.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Good.

**figure7.png** (EEG, three estimators)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Meaningful — blue/orange/pink map to the same three estimators as elsewhere; grey band = ±1σ.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Good — 3 hues.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk — panel (b) contains exactly two curves, **orange (cosine+parabola) vs. pink (Welch)**, and this pair is the panel's entire message. Under deuteranopia both shift toward a muted yellow-brown/olive and under achromatopsia they sit at similar mid-luminance; they are also heavily interleaved. This is the single most fragile categorical contrast in the manuscript.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work for (b) — keep the hues for consistency but add a style difference (Welch dashed) and/or move Welch to Okabe-Ito reddish-purple #CC79A7 at higher saturation while pushing the cosine/parabola curve to #E69F00; better still, direct-label the two traces at their right-hand ends as you do in efigure2(b).

**figure8.png** (seizure spectrograms)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Mixed — line colours in (d)/(f) are meaningful and consistent, but the spectrogram colormap is the problem: **jet/rainbow**.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Excessive in the image panels — jet sweeps the entire hue circle to encode a single ordered scalar (dB), so hue variety is maximal while information dimensionality is one.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk (serious) — jet is not monotone in luminance, so under achromatopsia the mapping is non-invertible (mid-scale yellow/cyan are both bright, dark blue and dark red are both dark); under deuteranopia the green–yellow–red half of the ramp collapses. It also creates false contour bands (the artificial cyan/yellow edges in (c)) that readers will interpret as structure, and the saturated red saturates visually at the top of the scale so the 22 dB ceiling is hard to read.
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work — replace with **viridis, magma or cividis** (cividis is explicitly optimised for deuteranopia). This will also remove the awkward clash between jet's blue/red and the paper's categorical blue and pink. Also note the jet blue in the spectrogram is the same hue family as "Multitaper" blue in (d)/(f), which invites a false semantic link.
    Minor: white dashed marker lines and white resolution boxes will lose contrast against viridis's dark low end — switch to black or a light-grey stroke with a dark halo.

**figure9.png** (sleep spindles at three resolutions)
    COLOR PURPOSE:      Mixed — panel (h) uses blue circles / orange squares consistent with the paper's method colours (good, and the axis labels are colour-matched). The six spectrograms again use **jet**.
    COLOR ECONOMY:      Excessive in (b–g) for the same reason as figure8.
    COLORBLIND SAFE:    At risk (serious) — same jet problems. Here the consequence is substantive: the argument is about whether spindle bursts are resolved in time vs. frequency, and jet's spurious luminance banding manufactures edges that make (b) look structured in a way that is partly an artefact of the colormap. Panel (h) is safe (blue vs. orange, plus distinct marker shapes).
    PALETTE QUALITY:    Needs work — viridis/magma/cividis. The single shared colorbar across all six panels is good practice and should be kept.

CROSS-FIGURE:
  - **Strong overall consistency for the line-plot set.** Blue = multitaper, orange/gold = cosine taper then parabola, green = untapered-then-box (and the "sliding sinc" route, correctly kept in the same green family per figure1's explicit rationale), pink = Welch, grey = raw periodogram, black = true PSD. This holds across efigure1–4 and figure1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8(d,f), 9(h). This is better than most manuscripts.
  - **Light blue is reused for two different groups.** In efigure2 light blue = "Multitaper, L=6"; in efigure3 and efigure4 light blue = "Bohman, then box". Same colour, different condition, in adjacent supplementary figures. This violates same-group-same-colour.
  - **Grey is overloaded.** Grey means "periodogram" (figure2a, efigure2, figure8d/f), "Hann, then box" (efigure3, efigure4), "bias/noise components" (figure3b), and also serves as annotation/guide-line colour throughout. Readers who have learned grey = periodogram will misread efigure3/4.
  - **Two Hann-related greys in efigure4** ("Hann, then box" vs. "Hann, then Gaussian") are separated by luminance only and read as the same series at print size.
  - **Palette-style mismatch between the line figures and the image figures.** The line figures use a muted, low-saturation, Okabe-Ito-like categorical palette; figure8 and figure9 use fully saturated jet. These do not look like they belong to the same paper, and jet's hues (saturated blue, green, orange, red) accidentally echo the paper's semantic categorical colours without carrying that meaning.
  - **Blue hue drifts slightly** between the sequential blue ramp (figure4, figure5d, efigure1d/h, figure9h) and the categorical "multitaper" blue (efigure2, efigure3, figure6b, figure7a). Cosmetic, but pin both to one hex value for the darkest ramp step / the categorical blue.
  - Direct end-of-line labelling is used in efigure2(b), efigure3(b), figure1(b), figure6 titles and figure9(h) but not in efigure2(a), efigure3(a), efigure4(b,c) or figure7. Inconsistent labelling strategy across the set.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] figure8 (c,e,g) and figure9 (b–g) use the jet/rainbow colormap for an ordered scalar (PSD in dB). It is not perceptually uniform, is non-invertible under achromatopsia, collapses in its green–red half under deuteranopia, and introduces false banding that is visible in the data and could be misread as structure — particularly damaging in figure9, where the claim concerns the appearance of time–frequency structure.
  - [BLOCKING] Same colour, different group: light blue denotes "Multitaper, L=6" in efigure2 but "Bohman, then box" in efigure3 and efigure4.
  - [BLOCKING] figure7(b): the panel's only categorical contrast is orange vs. pink with no redundant encoding and heavy curve overlap; this distinction is not reliably recoverable under deuteranopia/protanopia or in grayscale.
  - [ADVISORY] Recurrent green-vs-orange as a primary contrast (efigure2a, efigure3a/b, efigure4b/c) is a red-green-family collision. Acceptable where curves are end-labelled or spatially separated (efigure3b, figure6), risky where a legend is the only key.
  - [ADVISORY] efigure4 carries 9 series, 6 hues and 4 simultaneous encodings (hue, dash, marker shape, marker fill) in one legend; two of the hues (grey vs. dark grey; blue vs. light blue) are luminance-only distinctions.
  - [ADVISORY] Grey is used for at least four distinct meanings across the manuscript (periodogram, Hann-then-box, bias/noise curves, annotation/guides).
  - [ADVISORY] Lightest members of the within-hue ramps (i=4 in efigure1b/c/h, figure5; L=1 in figure4c; h⁽⁷⁾ in figure4a) have low contrast against white and will be lost in print or on a projector.
  - [ADVISORY] Palette-style discontinuity: muted categorical palette in the line figures vs. saturated rainbow in the image figures.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend-only keys in efigure2(a), efigure3(a), efigure4(b,c) where the same manuscript elsewhere demonstrates superior direct labelling.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Replace jet in figure8(c,e,g) and figure9(b–g) with **cividis** (first choice, deuteranopia-optimised and monotone in luminance) or viridis/magma. Keep the single shared colorbar per figure. Re-stroke the white dashed instant markers and the white "resolution of the panel" boxes in a colour that contrasts with the new dark low end (e.g. white with a thin black halo, or pure black).
  - Fix the light-blue collision: assign "Multitaper, L=6" to the multitaper blue family (e.g. the same blue as L=7 but dashed, or a distinctly lighter blue used *only* for multitaper variants), and reserve sky blue #56B4E9 exclusively for Bohman across efigure3 and efigure4. Add a one-line colour key to the supplementary text stating the global mapping.
  - Lock the palette to Okabe-Ito hexes and state them in a methods/plotting note: multitaper #0072B2, multitaper-L−1 #56B4E9 *or* dashed #0072B2, untapered-then-box #009E73, cosine-then-parabola #E69F00, Welch #CC79A7, Hann-then-box #555555, Bohman #56B4E9, periodogram #999999, truth/kernels #000000. Use one hex per group in every figure, including the darkest step of the sequential blue ramps.
  - In figure7(b), add line-style redundancy (Welch dashed) and direct end-of-line labels for both differences, matching the style already used in efigure2(b).
  - Add direct end-of-line labels to efigure2(a) and efigure3(a), and label at least the extreme curves in efigure4(b) and (c), so no panel depends on a ≥5-entry legend plus a red-green hue distinction.
  - Simplify efigure4: group by method family (one hue per family, dash/marker within family), drop or neutralise the Hann-then-Gaussian curve, and replace the grey/dark-grey pair with one grey plus a distinct dash pattern.
  - Darken the lightest step of every within-hue lightness ramp (target ≥40% of the range from white) so all ramp members remain visible in grayscale print: efigure1(b,c,h), figure4(a,c), figure5(b,c,d).
  - Reserve grey for the raw periodogram only; move "Hann, then box" to a dark neutral that is clearly distinct (e.g. #444444 with a distinct marker) and set annotation/guide lines in a lighter grey (#BBBBBB) than any data series.
  - Verify the final figure set through a deuteranopia/protanopia/grayscale simulator, with particular attention to efigure2(b), efigure4(b,c) and figure7(b).

## Typography & Annotation Inspector

---

## PER-FIGURE:

**efigure1.png** (16 panels, 2088 px ≈ 6.96 in at 300 dpi → double column)
- **FONT:** Consistent — one sans-serif family (DejaVu Sans / Helvetica-like) for all upright text; italic sans for variables (*k*, *h*, *i*, *ν*). Calligraphic 𝒦 in rows (m–p) is drawn from the math/script font and is the only glyph not from the text family; acceptable for math but should be checked to be the sans math set.
- **SIZE:** Borderline — tick labels ≈7.5–8 pt; axis titles ≈9 pt; panel labels ≈10 pt; **smallest = in-panel notes** ("shifts *n* = 0, 32, …, 192"; "centred at *k* = −128, 0, 128, 256"; "the bank itself, as in (d)") ≈6.5–7 pt. At any further reduction these drop below the 6 pt floor.
- **ABBREVIATIONS:** Undefined: *ν* (equivalent degrees of freedom — defined only by cross-reference to Fig. 5), dB reference level (caption says "relative to its peak" — OK), *R*, *α*, *Δ*, *N*, *L*, *f′* (primed frequency in (b) legend is never defined anywhere in caption).
- **AXIS LABELS:** Mostly complete; all quantities are dimensionless and the normalizations are shown. Issues: **"h_{k−n}/max h" renders as "maxh"** (missing thin space; "max" should be upright roman) in panel (c); "Weight c_i/Σc_i" should note it is normalized to unit sum (in caption only); 𝒦(*f*) (dB) should read "(dB re peak)".
- **STAT ANNOTATIONS:** Not present.
- **TITLE PLACEMENT:** Correct — column headers are panel descriptors, not a figure title; no bold title inside the artwork.

**efigure2.png** (2012 px → double column)
- **FONT:** Consistent sans-serif; italic math variables.
- **SIZE:** Adequate — ticks ≈8 pt, axis titles ≈9 pt, legend ≈8 pt, right-hand inline curve labels ≈8 pt. Panel labels (a)/(b) ≈10 pt.
- **ABBREVIATIONS:** Undefined in this caption: **PSD** (defined only in figure1's caption; supplements are read standalone), **AR(4)** (autoregressive, order 4), *ν*, *R*, *L*, *σ²*.
- **AXIS LABELS:** Complete — "PSD (dB re σ²Δ)", "Estimate − true PSD (dB)", "fΔ" (dimensionless, correctly normalized).

**efigure3.png** (2018 px → double column)

**efigure4.png** (2086 px → double column; 3 panels + legend block)

Grey-on-white at 6.5 pt is the weakest text in the whole set.

**figure7.png** (986 px → single column)
- **FONT:** Consistent.
- **SIZE:** Adequate — ticks ≈8 pt; legends ≈8 pt; **smallest = two-line grey-band annotation** in (b) ("Grey band: ±1.6 dB, the standard deviation of the multitaper estimate") ≈7 pt.
- **ABBREVIATIONS:** **EEG** not expanded; PSD used in the y-label without definition in this caption; *ν*, *R*, *L* defined.
- **AXIS LABELS:** Complete — "PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)", "Difference (dB)", "Frequency (Hz)". Panel (a) has no x-title (shared with (b)) — acceptable.
- **STAT ANNOTATIONS:** Not present; the ±1.6 dB band is defined in-figure and in caption (good).
- **TITLE PLACEMENT:** Correct.

**figure8.png** (1835 px → double column; 8 panels + colour bars)
- **FONT:** Consistent.
- **SIZE:** **Borderline** — ticks ≈7.5 pt; panel titles ≈8 pt; **smallest = in-panel note in (h)** ("median |difference| 0.9 dB", 3 lines) and the colour-bar tick labels ≈6.5–7 pt. Two-line y-axis titles in (d)/(f) ("PSD (dB re 1 µV²/Hz)") are the same size as elsewhere — good.
- **ABBREVIATIONS:** Undefined: **EEG**, **ELROND** (dataset acronym), **C4** (10–20 electrode position), **N2**-type staging terms are absent here; *ν*, *α*, *L* defined. "ictal / pre-ictal" are words, not abbreviations — fine.
- **AXIS LABELS:** **Incomplete:** spectrogram panels (c) and (e) carry x-tick numbers but no x-axis title/unit (only (g) has "Time (s)"). Because (c), (e), (g) are stacked and the interleaved right-hand panels break the visual column, the missing "Time (s)" is ambiguous. Colour bar is labelled with units — good. Panel (h) y-label "Pixels (thousands)" — state that "pixels" = time–frequency cells (caption does).
- **STAT ANNOTATIONS:** Not present.
- **TITLE PLACEMENT:** Correct — per-panel descriptors ("Periodogram: 2 s by 0.5 Hz, ν = 2.0") are informative panel titles, not a figure title; no bold title in the artwork.

**figure9.png** (2002 px → double column; 8 panels)
- **FONT:** Consistent.
- **SIZE:** **Borderline** — ticks ≈7.5–8 pt; panel titles ≈8 pt; **smallest = the in-panel white-box annotation** "resolution of the panel" in (b) ≈6.5 pt, and the in-panel inline labels in (h) ("Multitaper", "Cosine taper, then parabola", "α = 1, L = 1: small-α regime", and the per-marker "1.1 Hz / ν = 2.0" callouts) ≈7 pt.
- **ABBREVIATIONS:** Undefined: **EEG**, **C4-M1** (electrode/reference), **N2** (sleep stage; used in caption without expansion, "stage N2"), *ν*, *α*, *L*, *R*.

- **STAT ANNOTATIONS:** Not present.
- **TITLE PLACEMENT:** Correct.
- Additional: ranges are set with a **minus sign rather than an en dash** ("0.3−35 Hz", "11−16 Hz").

## CROSS-FIGURE:

- **Font family:** Uniform across all 13 figures — a single sans-serif text face with italic sans variables. Only the calligraphic 𝒦 (efigure1, efigure3, figure2, figure6) comes from a different (script/serif-derived) math face; it is used consistently, so this is a style rather than a consistency defect.
- **Effective point size depends on an unstated placement width.** Canvas widths range from 986 px (figure3, figure7) to 2092 px (figure4). If all were exported at the same dpi, point sizes match; if any figure is rescaled at typesetting (e.g. a 2088 px figure forced to single column, or a 986 px figure stretched to double column), equivalent elements will differ by up to 2×. No dpi/target width is declared anywhere.
- **Annotation tier is inconsistent in size.** Some figures put explanatory notes at ≈8 pt (efigure2, efigure3 inline labels), others at ≈6.5–7 pt (efigure1, efigure4c, figure3b, figure8h, figure9b). Three to four distinct type sizes are in circulation for the same semantic role.
- **Inset typography is the weakest link.** Insets in figure2 and figure6 use ≈6–6.5 pt tick numerals, ~1.5–2 pt smaller than main-axis ticks in the same figure.
- **Panel-label style is consistent** — lowercase parenthesised "(a)…(p)", ≈10 pt, outside the axes, upper left, in all 13 figures. Note this is lowercase, not the "A, B, C" convention some journals require; if uppercase is mandated, all 13 must change together.
- **Capitalization is consistently sentence case** for axis titles, legends and panel titles ("Half-power width ×NΔ", "Equivalent degrees of freedom ν", "Untapered, then box", "Cosine taper, then parabola") — good. Two deviations: "Max. |difference| (dB)" (figure1c) abbreviates where other figures spell out; "Eigen-taper h_k^(i)" (efigure1) hyphenated-capitalised differently from "Weight c_i/Σc_i".
- **Spelling register is mixed:** in-figure and caption text uses British forms ("centred" in efigure1/figure5, "colour" in captions) alongside American forms ("centered" in figure2's caption, "normalized", "-ize" endings throughout). Pick one.
- **Method names are consistent across figures** ("Multitaper, L = 7", "Untapered, then box", "Cosine taper, then parabola", "Welch, n Hann segments") — excellent; but captions alternate between "Multitaper" (in-figure) and "Thomson's estimate" (captions of figure4, figure5, figure6, figure7).
- **Unit style for decibels is consistent** ("dB re 1 µV²/Hz", "dB re σ²Δ") — good; but the relative-to-peak decibel axes (𝒦(*f*) (dB)) never state their reference in the label.
- **Abbreviation definition is figure-order dependent:** PSD is expanded only in figure1's caption; EEG is never expanded in any caption; ELROND, C4, C4-M1 and N2 are used without expansion. Supplementary figures (efigure1–4) reuse *ν*, *α*, *R* and AR(4) with no local definition.
- **Legend placement is mixed by design** (boxed in-axes legends in figures 1, 4, 7, 8; right-hand inline curve labels in efigure2, efigure3, figure1b, figure6; a legend in the vacant quadrant in efigure4). Acceptable, but the inline-label figures risk right-edge clipping and have no bounding box.

---

## ISSUES:

- **[BLOCKING]** No declared print width or dpi for any figure; canvas widths span 986–2092 px. Several figures cannot be certified ≥6 pt without this (efigure1 16-panel notes, figure2/figure6 inset ticks, efigure4c grey note, figure8h note, figure9b note all land at 6–7 pt at the most likely placement and fall below 6 pt under any reduction).
- **[BLOCKING]** Sub-6 pt risk in specific elements: inset tick labels in figure2 (e–h) and figure6 (e–h) ≈6–6.5 pt; "resolution of the panel" in figure9(b) ≈6.5 pt; the three-line note in figure8(h) ≈6.5 pt; the two-line grey note in efigure4(c) ≈6.5 pt in grey (lowest contrast text in the set).
- **[BLOCKING]** Typographic error: "h_{k−n}/max h" is set as **"maxh"** with no space and with "max" in italic in efigure1(c) and figure5(c); operator names must be upright roman with a thin space.
- **[BLOCKING]** Undefined abbreviations across the set: **EEG** (never expanded in any caption; figures 1, 7, 8, 9), **ELROND** (figure8), **C4**, **C4-M1**, **N2** (figures 8, 9), **AR(4)** (efigure2, figure3), **PSD** (defined only in figure1's caption but used in efigure2, figure2, figure6–9; supplementary figures must be self-contained), **f′** (efigure1(b) and figure5(b) legends — never defined).
- **[BLOCKING]** Missing axis units/normalization: figure3(a) y-axis "x_k" has no unit; efigure4(b) y-axis ("Kernel power beyond twice the half-power half-width") has no unit or "fraction of total" statement.
- **[BLOCKING]** Missing axis titles on panels that carry tick numbers: figure8(c) and (e) lack "Time (s)"; figure9(b)–(e) lack "Time (s)"; figure9(h) has no y-axis title.
- **[ADVISORY]** Label–caption mismatch: figure4(b) y-axis reads "|H^(i)(f)|²/NΔ²" while the caption says "|H^(i)(f)|²/Δ … in units of NΔ".
- **[ADVISORY]** Decibel axes relative to the kernel peak (efigure1 m–p, efigure3b, figure2 e–h, figure6 e–h) are labelled only "𝒦(f) (dB)" with the reference given in the caption.
- **[ADVISORY]** Cross-reference to "Table 2" placed *inside* the artwork of efigure4(a); artwork should not cite external numbered objects.
- **[ADVISORY]** Right-edge clipping risk for inline curve labels in efigure2(b), efigure3(b) and figure6; they extend to the canvas boundary.
- **[ADVISORY]** Mixed British/American spelling ("centred" in efigure1/figure5 art vs "centered" in figure2 caption; "colour" in captions vs "-ize" verbs).
- **[ADVISORY]** Inconsistent abb

## Technical Format Checker

### efigure1.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2088 x 1934 px — recommended **double column** (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **292 DPI** at that width (304 DPI if placed at 6.875 in; 597 DPI at single-column 88 mm, 642 DPI at 3.25 in)
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — figure type: line art (vector-style plots, no photographic content or gradients); 292 DPI at the required double-column width vs. 600 DPI preferred / 300 DPI minimum
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → TIFF (LZW), EPS, or PDF for final submission (PNG acceptable for review only)
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.43 MB (limit 10 MB); ample headroom for a 600-DPI or vector replacement
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick numerals, legend entries and math ($h_{k-n}$, $\mathcal{K}(f)$, $fN\Delta$) are pixel-based; subscript/superscript glyphs (e.g. $h_k^{(i)}$, $\nu$ values) will show visible stair-stepping at print size
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB) — journal requires RGB. If the production route converts to CMYK, the saturated teal/green (column 3) and the mid-blue (column 4) are the most at risk of dulling; the pale tints ($i=4$ traces, ~10–15 % saturation) may lose separation from white
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at the required 181 mm double-column width is 292 DPI — below the 300 DPI floor and well below the 600 DPI preference for line art. A 4×4 grid of 16 panels cannot be reduced to single-column width, so upscaling the placement is not an option.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and curves are rasterized in a line-art figure. Thin 1-px traces (the sinc side-lobes in (c), the dense side-lobe ripple in (p)) and the dotted $|f|=R/2$ guide lines will alias or drop out at print resolution.
  - [BLOCKING] Format: PNG is not an accepted final-submission format.
  - [ADVISORY] In-figure titles over panels (a)–(d) ("Welch, 7 Hann segments", "Cosine taper, then parabola", "Sliding sinc window", "Multitaper, $L=7$") conflict with the "no titles inside the figure" rule. They are functioning as column headers, which is usually tolerated, but the safest course is to move the four method names into the caption ("columns left to right: Welch…, cosine-taper…, sliding sinc…, multitaper…") or restyle them as small bold column labels rather than panel titles.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest type: the in-panel annotations ("shifts $n=0,32,\dots,192$", "centred at $k=-128,0,128,256$", "the bank itself, as in (d)") and the legend entries render at roughly 7–8 pt at 181 mm. That is inside spec but close to the 6 pt floor; any further reduction (e.g. if the figure is placed at less than full double-column width) puts them out of compliance.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical/estimator reporting is partially satisfied: the equivalent degrees of freedom $\nu$ = 13.4, 16.7, 17.2, 14.0 are printed in (i)–(l) but are not defined or repeated in the caption. Since noise levels are being compared across the four estimators, the caption must state how $\nu$ is computed (e.g. $\nu = 2\left(\sum_i c_i\right)^2/\sum_i c_i^2$) and the number of tapers/windows behind each value (7 Hann segments in (a)/(i); the number of non-negligible eigen-weights in (j) and (k); $L=7$ in (d)/(l)). Also state $N$ (=256) and the numerical value of $R$ used for the dotted lines.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption opens "Fig.~5 in full" and defers colours and columns to Fig. 5. Supplementary/extended figures should be self-contained; a reader of the e-figure alone cannot decode the colour mapping.
  - [ADVISORY] Legends are shown only in (b), (d) and (e); panels (f)–(h) reuse the same $i=1\ldots4$ lightness ramp without a key. The lightest tint ($i=4$) has low contrast against the white axes background and against the white bar gaps in (i)–(l).
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as vector PDF or EPS at a canvas width of exactly 181 mm (7.16 in) — e.g. `fig.set_size_inches(7.16, 6.63); fig.savefig('efigure1.pdf')` — which resolves the resolution, format and text-rendering blockers in one step. Keep fonts as Arial/Helvetica and embed them (Type 42/TrueType, not Type 3).
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for the final width: **4296 x 3978 px** minimum (181 mm at 600 DPI), saved as LZW-compressed TIFF, RGB. Do not upsample the existing 2088 px file — re-plot from source.
  - Increase minimum line width to ≥0.75 pt (≈0.26 mm) for all traces and to ≥0.6 pt for the dotted $|f|=R/2$ guides so they survive print reproduction.
  - Set all tick labels and axis labels to 8 pt and in-panel annotations/legend text to no less than 7 pt at the final 181 mm width; verify by printing at 100 %.
  - Remove the four in-panel titles (or demote them to compact column headers) and fold the method names into the caption; keep panel labels as the existing lowercase (a)–(p).
  - Expand the caption to be self-contained: define the colour-per-column mapping and the light-to-dark $i=1\ldots4$ ramp explicitly, give $N$ and $R$, and add the $\nu$ definition plus the taper/segment count underlying each $\nu$ and each weight ladder.
  - Add a visible edge (0.3 pt, matching hue at higher saturation) to the bars in (i)–(l) and darken the lightest tint used for $i=4$ to ≈25 % saturation so all four eigen-tapers remain distinguishable in print and for low-vision readers.
  - Confirm the file is tagged RGB (sRGB profile) and instruct production not to CMYK-convert; if CMYK proofing is unavoidable, pre-check the teal (column 3) and blue (column 4) for gamut clipping.

### efigure2.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2012 × 737 px (aspect 2.73:1) — recommended **double column** — **293 DPI** at 6.875 in (181 mm); 619 DPI if forced to single-column 3.25 in, but the two side-by-side panels would render each plot ~40 mm wide with ~3 pt text, so single-column is not viable. At double-column width the figure is 174 × 64 mm, well within the 230 mm height limit.
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (axis grids, curves, text; no photographic content or continuous-tone gradients). Actual 293 DPI at the recommended width vs. 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred for line art.
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG is accepted for review only; final submission requires TIFF, EPS, PDF, or PS. Because this is line art with fine, densely oscillating traces, re-export as **vector PDF or EPS** from the plotting source rather than up-sampling the raster.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.25 MB (limit 10 MB); large headroom for a 600 DPI or vector re-export.
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all labels are baked into the PNG bitmap; at 293 DPI the glyph stems show pixel stepping. BLOCKING for line-art quality.
COLOR MODE:     OK (RGB, as required). Potential shift only if the publisher converts to CMYK: the saturated mid-blue (Multitaper L=7), the teal-green (Untapered, then box), and the orange (Cosine taper) are all at risk, and the three-step blue ramp (light blue / blue / dark blue) is the most likely to lose separation.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution 293 DPI at double-column width — below the 300 DPI floor and far below the 600 DPI preference for line art.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and curves are rasterized; a raster line-art figure at <600 DPI will show aliased glyph edges and broken hairlines in the densely oscillating high-frequency regions of both panels.
  - [ADVISORY] Estimated in-figure type size is ~7 pt at 181 mm final width (tick labels marginally smaller than axis labels). This is above the 6 pt absolute floor but below the 8–10 pt preferred range.
  - [ADVISORY] The right-hand curve labels in panel (b) ("Multitaper, $L=7$, adaptive", "Cosine taper, then parabola") run to the extreme right edge of the canvas with no margin and risk being trimmed or colliding with the column edge.
  - [ADVISORY] Fonts appear to be a LaTeX serif/math family rather than a sans-serif (Arial/Helvetica). Times-like serif is permissible, but mixing math italics with the journal's preferred sans-serif labels should be checked for consistency across the figure set.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: the caption commendably gives the degrees of freedom $\nu$ for every estimator, satisfying the "state the estimator's degrees of freedom where noise levels are compared" requirement. However, panel (a) is stated to be a single realization, and the median offsets quoted in panel (b) ("18 dB", "10 dB", "4 dB") do not say what the median is taken over — frequency bins within $f\Delta\ge0.4$ of this one realization, or an ensemble of realizations. The number of realizations/windows must be stated explicitly.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) relies on colour alone to associate each trace with its right-hand text label; the light-blue $L=6$ band and the mid-blue $L=7$ band are adjacent in hue and overlap spatially, which will be hard to separate in greyscale or after CMYK conversion.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption opens with a standalone title-like sentence fragment ("Dropping the leakiest taper."). No title is embedded in the artwork itself, which is correct; confirm the house style permits a bold lead-in phrase in the caption.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting script as **vector PDF or EPS** sized to 181 mm width (e.g. `figsize=(7.16, 2.62)` in matplotlib), which resolves both the resolution and the rasterized-text blocking issues at once.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandatory, export TIFF at ≥600 DPI for 181 mm width, i.e. ≥4290 px wide (LZW compression; still far under 10 MB).
  - Set all text at the final size: 8 pt for tick labels and curve annotations, 9–10 pt for axis labels and panel letters. Do not rely on downscaling a larger canvas.
  - Add ~4–6 mm of right-hand padding (or shorten the labels, e.g. "MT, $L=7$, adaptive") so the panel-(b) annotations cannot be clipped at the column edge.
  - In the caption, state the number of realizations or frequency bins underlying the median dB offsets quoted for panel (b), and say whether the $f\Delta\ge0.4$ statistic is an ensemble or within-realization median.
  - Differentiate the three blue traces by line style or width (e.g. solid / dashed / heavier solid) in addition to hue so the $L=7$, $L=6$, and adaptive curves survive greyscale printing and CMYK conversion.
  - Soft-proof the green, orange, and three-step blue ramp in CMYK; if separation collapses, substitute a print-safe qualitative palette before final submission.

### efigure3.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2018 × 677 px — recommended **double column** — 293 DPI at 6.875 in (282 DPI if the full 181 mm / 7.16 in text width is used; 621 DPI if forced to 3.25 in single column)
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (two line-graph panels, no photographic content or gradients); 293 DPI at double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred for line art. Aspect ratio 2.98:1 is well suited to double column (final height ≈ 2.31 in / 59 mm, far under the 230 mm limit).
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG acceptable for review only → TIFF (LZW), EPS, or PDF for final submission; vector PDF/EPS strongly preferred for this figure type.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.2 MB (limit 10 MB); ample headroom for a 600-DPI raster or vector re-export.
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis numerals, axis labels ($\mathcal{K}(f)/N\Delta$, $fN\Delta$), legend entries and the right-hand curve labels are bitmap glyphs in the PNG; edges will show pixel stair-stepping in print. For a pure line-art figure this is a quality-blocking condition.
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB per PNG default; confirm in metadata) — IEEE requests RGB, so no conversion needed. Note that the saturated teal-green ("Untapered"), amber-orange ("Cosine taper") and the two blues are exactly the hues most prone to shift and to lose separation if any downstream CMYK conversion occurs; the dark blue vs. light blue pair is also the weakest contrast in grayscale reproduction.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at double-column width (293 DPI) falls below the 300 DPI floor and far below the 600 DPI line-art preference.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and curves are rasterized; fine features (the multitaper ripple in (b), the dotted ideal-box edges) will alias and the dotted black box may partially disappear at 293 DPI.
  - [BLOCKING] PNG is not an accepted final-submission format.
  - [ADVISORY] Estimated type size is ≈7 pt at 6.875 in final width (tick numerals slightly smaller) — above the 6 pt floor but below the 8–10 pt recommendation; legend text in (a) is the smallest element.
  - [ADVISORY] The right-hand in-plot curve labels in (b) sit in the outer margin and extend to the image edge; on reduction or with a tight bounding box they risk clipping.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption uses "recipe (b$'$)" in the same sentence as panel label (b), creating a label collision for the reader.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: degrees of freedom are given for every estimator ($\nu=14.0$, 16.7, 17.2, 8.9, 7.3), which satisfies the noise-level comparison requirement. Because the kernels are computed exactly there are no realizations or error bars — state this explicitly ("analytic kernels; no Monte-Carlo averaging, hence no error bars") so reviewers do not look for a realization count.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant identification scheme: (a) uses a legend box, (b) uses direct end-of-curve labels for the same five curves.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting source as vector PDF or EPS sized to 181 mm × ~61 mm, with all text kept as embedded (Type 1/TrueType) fonts — this resolves the resolution, text-rendering and format issues at once.
  - If a raster deliverable is required, render at ≥600 DPI for 181 mm width, i.e. ≥4276 px wide (≈1435 px tall), and save as LZW-compressed TIFF, RGB.
  - Set all fonts to a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at 8–9 pt for tick labels and 9–10 pt for axis labels, measured at the 181 mm final width; enlarge the legend text in (a) to at least 8 pt.
  - Keep the figure at double column; do not attempt single-column use — at 88 mm the 3:1 panel pair would compress each axis to ~40 mm and the text would fall below 6 pt.
  - Add ~4 mm of right-hand margin (or use a non-tight bounding box) so the (b) end-of-curve labels cannot be clipped.
  - Increase the dotted ideal-box line weight to ≥0.75 pt and use a coarser dash period so it survives reduction; likewise verify the Bohman dashed light-blue trace remains distinguishable from the solid dark-blue multitaper trace.
  - Consider dropping the legend in (a) and relying on the (b) end labels plus a shared color key, freeing space and removing duplication; if the legend is kept, ensure the dotted "Ideal box of width $R$" swatch is long enough to show at least three dots.
  - Rename the alternative recipe in the caption (e.g. "recipe R2" or "recipe b-prime") to avoid confusion with panel (b).
  - Confirm the file is tagged RGB (not CMYK or indexed) on export, and avoid any CMYK proofing step that would desaturate the teal/orange pair.

DIMENSIONS:     2086 x 1307 px (aspect 1.60:1) — recommended **double** column — 303 DPI at 6.875 in (642 DPI if placed at 3.25 in single column). At 181 mm the printed height is ≈113 mm, well under the 230 mm limit.
RESOLUTION:     Adequate (marginal) — line art with color; 303 DPI at double-column width meets the 300 DPI minimum but falls well short of the 600 DPI preferred for line art. Single-column placement (642 DPI) is not viable because the 3-panel + legend layout would render tick labels at ~3.4 pt.
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG → vector PDF/EPS (or 600+ DPI TIFF) for final submission. PNG is acceptable for review only.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.3 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to re-export at 600 DPI.
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all axis labels, tick labels, in-panel annotations and legend entries are bitmap text; pixel edges will be visible on math italics ($\nu$, $\alpha$, $N\Delta$) and on the thin dotted reference lines.
COLOR MODE:     OK if RGB (color_mode was not supplied in the metadata block — confirm). The palette contains saturated teal-blue, green, orange and magenta-pink, all of which are at risk of visible shift if the publisher converts to CMYK; the pink (Welch) and orange (cosine/parabola) are the most vulnerable.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text and line work in a line-art figure. Vector output is required to preserve legibility of the small annotations and the dotted 8/(NΔ) markers.
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution of 303 DPI at the recommended double-column width is below the 600 DPI preferred for line art; combined with rasterization this will visibly degrade thin curves and dotted lines.
  - [ADVISORY] Text size at final width: axis titles compute to ≈7 pt, tick labels ≈6.5 pt, and the in-panel notes ("8/(NΔ), the width in Table 2"; "filled markers: no flat top…") to ≈5.5–6 pt — at or below the 6 pt floor.
  - [ADVISORY] Three of the nine series are the same blue hue distinguished only by line style (solid/dashed/dotted) and marker (circle/square/triangle); at 303 DPI in a printed 181 mm figure these are hard to separate, especially in the crowded left half of panel (b).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel layout is 2x2 with the fourth quadrant used only for the legend, leaving a large area of whitespace around it; the three data panels are therefore smaller than necessary.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption ends with "and the legend is at the lower right", which describes layout rather than content.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is largely satisfied (the caption states the curves are exact computations with no sampling variability, and panel (a) is the equivalent degrees of freedom ν), but the caption does not state the number of Welch segments/windows implied by the 50 % overlap at N=256, which is the one quantity a reader needs to reproduce the pink curve.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as vector PDF or EPS with fonts embedded (or as a 600 DPI TIFF at exactly 181 mm width if a raster is required); do not upscale the existing PNG.
  - Set the figure canvas to 181 mm and specify fonts in points so that tick labels are ≥8 pt, axis titles 9–10 pt, and the in-panel annotations no smaller than 8 pt; use a sans-serif family (Arial/Helvetica) consistently, including for the math symbols.
  - Recover space by moving the legend to a single horizontal band spanning the full width below the panels (or into the unused upper-right of panel (b)/(c)) and re-tiling the three data panels, e.g. 1x3 or 2x2 with a larger panel (c).
  - Differentiate the three multitaper series by hue as well as line style (e.g. navy / mid-blue / cyan) or add short direct labels at the right-hand curve ends, and increase marker size slightly.
  - Confirm and declare RGB; if the journal will convert to CMYK, soft-proof the pink, orange and green and shift to CMYK-safe equivalents so the nine series remain distinguishable in print.
  - Add the number of Welch segments (and the segment length) for N=256 to the caption, and state explicitly that panel (b) and (c) values are deterministic kernel properties so no error bars apply.

### figure1.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1830 × 1052 px — recommended **double column** — 256 DPI at 181 mm (7.16 in); 266 DPI if the 6.875 in text-width value is used
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (multi-panel line plots, no photographic content); 256 DPI at double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum (600 DPI preferred). At single-column width (88 mm) the same file yields 523 DPI, but the 1.74:1 aspect ratio with three panels and a 3-line in-plot legend would render axis text near or below 5 pt — single column is not viable.
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG is accepted for review only; final submission requires TIFF (LZW), EPS, or PDF. Preferred path: regenerate as vector PDF/EPS directly from the plotting script.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.27 MB (limit 10 MB); a 600 DPI TIFF of this panel set will land ≈4–8 MB, still within limit
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, legend entries, and math symbols ($\lambda_i$, $N_s$, $1/N_s$) are pixel-bound in the PNG raster; edges will alias at print size
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB for PNG) — but note the palette is at high risk if the production house converts to CMYK: the thick saturated blue (≈#1f6fb4) and the two greens (≈#17a97f and ≈#0d4f3c) are exactly the hues that shift most. The mid-green/dark-green pair is already close in luminance; after CMYK compression they may become hard to separate in panel (b), where the only distinguishing cue is the text annotation.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution 256 DPI at the recommended double-column width is below the 300 DPI floor for color line art and far below the 600 DPI preference. Panel (b), which contains dense high-frequency oscillatory traces near the 1e-15 noise floor, will lose individual excursions to pixel merging.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. At 8–9 pt equivalent final size the stems of the italic math subscripts ($\lambda_i$, $N_s$) and the "−15" exponents in the panel (b) tick labels will show visible stair-stepping.
  - [BLOCKING] PNG is not an acceptable final-submission format for this figure class.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) and (c) traces rely on in-plot text annotations rather than a shared legend to identify curves; if the two greens compress under CMYK or in grayscale reprint, the reader must depend entirely on those annotations. Consider distinct dash patterns in (b) as a redundant cue (panel (a) already does this correctly).
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is partially satisfied: the caption correctly states $N=1024$, one epoch, and $\nu=17$ equivalent degrees of freedom for the multitaper estimate — this meets the "degrees of freedom where noise levels are compared" requirement for panel (b). However, panel (c) plots a *maximum* absolute difference without stating the population over which the maximum is taken (number of frequency bins) or whether each point is from the same single epoch. State this explicitly.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) compares two noise floors (rounding error ≈3e-13 dB vs. sliding-window bias ≈0.06 dB). Since these are deterministic differences, not error bars, consider a one-clause note in the caption that no averaging over realizations is involved, so a reader does not look for missing error bars.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption's parenthetical "(the two greens mark one estimator reached by two routes)" duplicates information already carried by the in-plot legend header "Three estimates that coincide." Minor redundancy; acceptable but trimmable.
  - [ADVISORY] Font appears to be a serif (Computer Modern/Times family). This is permitted, but the journal's stated preference is sans-serif (Arial/Helvetica); consider switching for consistency with the house style, keeping math italic.
  - [ADVISORY] Note that the brief provides two different single-column values (88 mm / 3.5 in in the specification, 3.25 in in the calculation instruction). DPI figures above are given for both; the conclusion (double column, sub-300 DPI) is unchanged either way.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as vector PDF or EPS at a canvas width of exactly 181 mm (7.16 in). This resolves the resolution, text-rendering, and format issues simultaneously and will keep the file well under 1 MB.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥4300 × 2470 px (600 DPI at 181 mm) and save as LZW-compressed TIFF, RGB. Do **not** upsample the existing 1830 px PNG — interpolation will not recover the lost detail in panel (b).
  - Set figure height to ≈100 mm at 181 mm width (current 1.74:1 ratio) — comfortably inside the 230 mm height cap, so no vertical reflow is needed.
  - Verify after export that the smallest text (panel (b) exponent tick labels) measures ≥6 pt at 181 mm; target 8 pt for tick labels and 9–10 pt for axis titles.
  - Keep the file in RGB and add a production note requesting no CMYK conversion, or pre-select a palette whose blue/green separation survives conversion (e.g., increase the luminance gap between the two greens).
  - Amend the caption for panel (c): state the number of frequency bins over which the maximum is taken and confirm that all five $N_s$ points come from the same single 1024-sample epoch, e.g. "...maximum taken over the 513 non-negative-frequency bins of the same epoch."
  - Confirm panel labels remain lowercase (a), (b), (c) after export — they are currently correct — and that no in-figure title is introduced during conversion.

### figure2.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2077 x 1097 px — recommended **double** column — 290 DPI at 181 mm (7.16 in); 593 DPI if forced to single column (88 mm)
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (multi-panel line plots, no photographic content); 290 DPI at the required double-column width, vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (acceptable for review only) → TIFF (LZW), EPS, or PDF for final submission; vector PDF/EPS strongly preferred for this figure type
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.21 MB (limit 10 MB); note that a 600-DPI TIFF re-render will grow to roughly 4–8 MB, still within limit
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis numerals, panel labels, and math glyphs are bitmap; pixel edges will be visible on the thin strokes of $\nu$, $\mathcal{K}(f)$, and the inset tick labels
COLOR MODE:     OK for submission (RGB required) — the single saturated orange (~#E0A33C) is a mid-chroma orange that will shift toward a duller ochre if the publisher converts to CMYK; grey/black/orange separation survives greyscale printing but grey (a) vs. black (true PSD) contrast is the weakest link

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at the double-column width is 290 DPI, below the 300 DPI floor for color line art and well below the 600 DPI preference. An 8-panel figure with dense periodogram traces (panels a, b) and fine kernel side-lobe structure (e, f) will alias badly at 290 DPI.
  - [BLOCKING] Text is rasterized. For line art, IEEE treats bitmap lettering as a quality defect; the inset tick labels ("0.39", "0.41", "−25", "−50") are the smallest elements and will be the first to break up.
  - [BLOCKING] Inset tick labels measure roughly 16–19 px, i.e. ≈4–4.7 pt at 181 mm final width — below the 6 pt absolute minimum. Main-axis tick numerals are ≈22–24 px ≈ 5.5–6 pt, at or just under the floor.
  - [BLOCKING] PNG is not an acceptable final-submission format.
  - [ADVISORY] Aspect ratio 1.89:1 gives a printed size of 181 × 96 mm — well within the 230 mm height limit and appropriate for a 4 × 2 panel grid. Do not attempt single-column placement; the 8 panels would render each subplot ~20 mm wide.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is largely compliant: "One realization ($N=1024$)" states the number of realizations and $\nu$ = 2.0, 2.0, 16.7, 98.4 gives the estimator degrees of freedom for each panel. However, no error bars or distributions are shown, so readers cannot judge realization-to-realization variability of the smoothed estimates in (c) and (d); the claim that a kernel "as wide as a peak is just right" rests on a single draw.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption's opening sentence ("Every estimator is the true spectrum blurred by a kernel, plus noise.") functions as a title/thesis rather than a description. IEEE style expects a descriptive caption; move this assertion into the body text.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption is ~300 words and contains substantial quantitative results (half-power widths, leakage percentages, side-lobe suppression in dB). Consider migrating the numeric comparisons to a small table or to the text, keeping the caption to panel identification.
  - [ADVISORY] Series identification relies on in-panel text annotations ("Periodogram, untapered", "True PSD") rather than a legend; this is acceptable and space-efficient, but the grey trace in (a) vs. the black true-PSD curve may be hard to separate in low-quality print. The grey is also the only panel using grey rather than orange, which is inconsistent with (b)–(d).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel labels (a)–(h) are correctly lowercase in parentheses and there is no title inside the figure — compliant.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting source as **vector PDF or EPS** at a canvas width of exactly 181 mm. This eliminates both the resolution and the rasterized-text blockers in one step and keeps the file well under 10 MB.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for 181 mm, i.e. ≥4280 px wide (≥2260 px tall), as LZW-compressed TIFF, RGB. Do not upsample the existing 2077 px PNG — regenerate from source.
  - Set fonts at final size: main-axis tick labels and inset tick labels to 8 pt minimum, axis titles 9–10 pt, panel labels 10 pt bold. Increase inset tick label size specifically; if space is tight, reduce the number of inset ticks (e.g. show only 0.39 and 0.41, or only −50 and 0) rather than shrinking the type.
  - Embed all fonts (Type 1 or TrueType subsets) in the PDF/EPS; confirm the math glyphs $\nu$, $\mathcal{K}$, $\Delta$, $\sigma^2$ are embedded and not substituted.
  - Keep RGB. If the publisher converts to CMYK, pre-check the orange by soft-proofing to US Web Coated; if it dulls, substitute a slightly more chromatic orange or differentiate the traces by line style (solid vs. dashed) so the comparison survives both CMYK and greyscale.
  - Darken or thin the grey periodogram in (a) to a mid-grey (~45–55% K) so it separates cleanly from the black true-PSD curve, and consider using the same orange in (a) as in (b)–(d) for visual consistency, distinguishing "untapered" by the in-panel label alone.
  - Add one sentence to the caption naming the tapered-periodogram smoothing window's degrees of freedom basis (e.g. "$\nu$ computed as $2\left(\sum_k w_k^2\right)^{-1}$ for the parabolic smoother") so the reported $\nu$ values are reproducible, as required when noise levels are compared across panels.
  - Optionally add a light shaded band or single error bar in (c) and (d) showing the $\pm$1 standard-deviation interval implied by $\nu$ = 16.7 and 98.4, and state in the caption that it derives from the chi-square approximation with those degrees of freedom on a single realization.
  - Trim the caption to panel descriptions plus the $\nu$ values and the inset span; relocate the leakage percentages and side-lobe suppression figures to the text or a companion table.

### figure3.png

---

**DIMENSIONS:** 990 × 1252 px — recommended **single column** (88 mm / 3.5 in) — **≈283 DPI** at that width
 - At the alternative 3.25 in reference width: ≈305 DPI
 - At double column (7.16 in): ≈138 DPI; rendered height would be 9.05 in ≈ 230 mm, i.e. exactly at the maximum page height — not viable
 - Aspect ratio (0.79 W:H, portrait) is well suited to a single-column, two-panel vertical stack; height at 88 mm = ~112 mm, comfortably within limits

**RESOLUTION:** **Below threshold** — figure type: **line art** (two-panel line plots, dashed curves, text annotations); actual ≈283 DPI at 88 mm (≈305 DPI at 3.25 in). IEEE requires ≥300 DPI minimum and prefers **600 DPI** for line art; this file is below the minimum at the journal's stated 88 mm single-column width and less than half the preferred value.

**FORMAT:** **Needs conversion** — PNG → TIFF (LZW) or, preferably, vector **PDF/EPS**. PNG is accepted for review only, not for final submission.

**FILE SIZE:** OK — 0.22 MB (limit 10 MB; ample headroom for a 600-DPI or vector re-export)

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all labels, tick numbers, italic math ($x_k$, $\nu$, $RN\Delta$) and annotations are baked into the PNG raster. Anti-aliased edges are visible on thin strokes and small glyphs; the hairline periodogram traces in (a) and the dotted vertical guides in (b) are the most vulnerable to further degradation.

**COLOR MODE:** **OK / Potential shift** — assumed RGB (correct for IEEE). The only chromatic element is the saturated orange tapered record in (a); saturated orange is one of the highest-risk hues in an RGB→CMYK conversion and can shift toward a muddier red-brown. Submit RGB and do not pre-convert. Note that the orange trace and the black record overlap heavily in (a); in grayscale reproduction the two become nearly indistinguishable except by lightness.

**ISSUES:**
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution ≈283 DPI at the journal's 88 mm single-column width is below the 300 DPI minimum and far below the 600 DPI preferred value for line art.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text and line work in a pure line-art figure. Dashed/dotted guides, hairline traces in (a), and small italic math will show pixel edges in print.
  - [BLOCKING] PNG is not an accepted final-submission format; conversion to TIFF/EPS/PDF is required.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest type is marginal. Estimated cap heights correspond to roughly 7–7.5 pt for tick labels at 88 mm, and the gray in-plot annotations ("peak width", "peak separation", "too noisy, ν = 2.0", "just right", "too smooth", "Bias", "Noise") appear a step smaller — likely 6–6.5 pt. Verify none falls below the 6 pt floor at final width.
  - [ADVISORY] Low-contrast gray annotation text and the light-gray horizontal gridlines in (b) may drop out or become illegible in print; the gray leader lines from the circled markers are ~1 px wide in the raster.
  - [ADVISORY] The three-item text row at the top of panel (a) ("Record / Tapered record / Taper (dashed)") sits above the axes and can be read as an in-figure title, which IEEE disallows. It is functionally a color key, but its placement is ambiguous.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is largely compliant — the caption states 300 independent realizations, states explicitly that no error bars are shown, and the upper axis reports equivalent degrees of freedom ν. However, the caption does not say how ν was computed for the parabolic smoother combined with the 25% cosine taper (the taper reduces the effective DOF relative to the smoother-only count), nor how many frequency bins enter the RMS average for the whole band versus the peak region (0.09 ≤ fΔ ≤ 0.16).
  - [ADVISORY] The three circled points are cross-referenced to panels (b), (c), (d) of another figure while this figure's own panels are also (a)/(b) — potentially confusing; consider phrasing as "Fig. X(b)–(d)".

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
  - Re-export from the plotting source as **vector PDF or EPS** sized to exactly 88 mm width with fonts embedded (Type 1/TrueType, no Type 3). This resolves the resolution, text-rendering and format issues at once.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandatory, re-render at 600 DPI for 88 mm → **2079 × 2629 px**, saved as LZW-compressed TIFF, RGB. Do not upsample the existing 990 px file.
  - Set all text to a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) and enforce a minimum of 7 pt at 88 mm final size; bring the gray annotations up to the same size as the tick labels.
  - Darken the gray annotation text and leader lines to ~40–50% black minimum, and set gridlines/leader lines to ≥0.5 pt stroke so they survive printing.
  - Convert the top row of panel (a) into an explicit in-axes legend (or move the labels beside the traces) so no text sits above the panel frame where it could read as a title.
  - Add to the caption: the number of frequency bins averaged in each RMS curve, and a one-clause statement of how the equivalent degrees of freedom ν on the upper axis were derived (smoother bandwidth × record length, corrected for the 25% cosine taper).
  - Keep the file in RGB; if the production office requests CMYK, check the orange trace after conversion and, if it shifts, substitute a slightly less saturated orange or add a dash-pattern/linewidth difference so (a) remains readable in grayscale.
  - Optional: increase the lightness separation between the orange tapered record and the black record (e.g., plot the untapered record in 60% gray) so the two traces remain distinguishable in monochrome reproduction.

### figure4.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2092 x 1307 px (aspect 1.60:1) — recommended **double column** — 292 DPI at 181 mm (7.16 in); 304 DPI at the 6.875 in working width; 598 DPI if forced to single column (88 mm)
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (multi-panel vector-style plots, no photographic content); 292 DPI at the recommended 181 mm width, versus the 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred for line art
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG acceptable for peer review only; PNG → TIFF (LZW) or, far better, native **PDF/EPS** vector export for final submission
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.25 MB (limit 10 MB); ample headroom to re-export at much higher resolution or as vector
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, math symbols ($h_k^{(i)}$, $|H^{(i)}(f)|^2/N\Delta^2$, $1-\lambda_i$) are bitmap glyphs; superscripts/subscripts and the sub/superscripted parentheses will show pixel edges at print size
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB PNG) — palette is a sequential blue ramp plus black/gray dashed-dotted lines; if the publisher converts to CMYK the darkest navy and the mid blue will compress toward each other, reducing the dark-to-light ordering that panels (a)–(c) rely on for meaning
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution 292 DPI at 181 mm is below the 300 DPI floor and well below the 600 DPI preferred for line art. Thin curves (the light-blue $L=1$, $h_k^{(7)}$ traces) and the dotted $\pm R/2$ and $L=2\alpha-1$ reference lines will alias or drop out in print.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Journal requires vector or ≥600 DPI text for graphs; nested super/subscript math at this pixel density will not survive downsampling to CMYK plates.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest type (tick labels and the three-column legend in panel c, the two-entry legend in panel d) measures roughly 7–7.5 pt at 181 mm. That is inside spec but close to the 6 pt floor; any reduction to single-column width would violate it.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) legend spans three columns inside the axes and sits directly above the curve family; at final size the "Box" dashed swatch is easy to confuse with the dotted $\pm R/2$ verticals carried over from panel (b). Consider distinguishing dash patterns or moving the legend outside the data region.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: the caption correctly states that every curve is computed exactly and nothing is simulated, so no error bars or realization counts are required. However, the caption compares taper counts ($L=1,3,5,7,9,N$) that directly set the estimator's variance, and the degrees of freedom are never stated. Add the $2L$ (or $2\sum\lambda_i$) equivalent degrees of freedom so the reader can connect the kernel shape in (c) to the noise level of the resulting estimate.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (d) y-axis spans $10^{-16}$ to above $10^{-1}$; the lowest points are at or near double-precision eigenvalue accuracy for the Slepian problem. State the solver/precision, or truncate the axis, so $1-\lambda_1\approx10^{-10}$ is not read as a resolved physical quantity.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) y-axis is clipped at $\pm0.20$ while the plotted extrema reach only ~$\pm0.12$; the wasted vertical range shrinks the usable curve amplitude. Panel (b) similarly runs to 0.6 with a maximum near 0.49.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the original plotting script as **PDF or EPS with fonts embedded** (Type 1/TrueType, not outlined-to-path if avoidable). This resolves both BLOCKING items at once and will still land far under 10 MB.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandatory, re-render at 181 mm × 113 mm at 600 DPI → **4276 × 2672 px**, saved as TIFF with LZW compression. Do not upscale the existing PNG; regenerate from source.
  - Set the figure canvas to exactly 181 mm wide in the plotting library and specify fonts in points (8–9 pt for tick labels, 9–10 pt for axis labels) so that on-screen and final print sizes match; verify no glyph falls below 6 pt.
  - Keep RGB for submission, but test a CMYK soft proof of the blue ramp; if the $i=1$ navy and $i=2$ blue converge, increase the lightness separation or add distinguishing line styles so the dark-to-light ordering described in the caption remains legible.
  - Tighten y-limits in (a) to about $\pm0.14$ and in (b) to 0.55 to recover vertical resolution in the curves.
  - Add to the caption a sentence of the form "With $L$ tapers the corresponding spectral estimate has approximately $2L$ degrees of freedom ($2L=14$ for the usual $L=2\alpha-1=7$)," and name the routine/precision used for the Slepian eigenvalues in (d).
  - Confirm the caption sits below the figure with no in-figure title; current panel labels (a)–(d) are already lowercase parenthetical and conform.

### figure5.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2088 x 979 px — recommended **double column** (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **292 DPI** at 7.16 in (304 DPI at the 6.875 in text-block width; 597 DPI if forced to 88 mm single column). Aspect ratio 2.13:1 → printed height ≈ 85 mm at double-column width, well within the 230 mm limit. Aspect ratio is appropriate for an 8-panel 4x2 grid; single-column use is not viable (panels would be ~20 mm wide).

RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — figure type: line art with color (curve plots + bar charts, no photographic content or gradients). Actual 292 DPI at 181 mm, i.e. below the 300 DPI minimum and far below the 600 DPI preferred for line art.

FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → vector PDF or EPS (or, if raster is unavoidable, 600+ DPI TIFF with LZW compression). PNG is acceptable for review only.

FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.21 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to re-export at much higher resolution.

TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick numbers, panel letters and in-panel annotations are baked into the PNG raster. At 292 DPI the mathematical glyphs (subscripts in $h_{k-n}$, superscript $(i)$, $\nu$, $\Delta$) will show pixel edges and thin-stroke breakup in print. BLOCKING for line art.

COLOR MODE:     OK for submission (RGB assumed; confirm no CMYK/ICC profile is embedded). Potential shift risk if the publisher converts: the saturated teal-green (panels c, g) and the mid-orange (b, f) are the most likely to darken/dull; the rose-pink (a, e) may lose saturation. Within-family light tints (the 3rd/4th lightest shades in b, c, d) are the most vulnerable to collapsing into one another after conversion or on grayscale print.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution is 292 DPI at the intended 181 mm double-column width — below the 300 DPI floor and less than half the 600 DPI preferred for line art.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and curves are rasterized rather than vector; thin traces (e.g. the overlapping sinc sidelobes in panel c) and small math glyphs will alias at print size.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (a)–(d) carry descriptive titles inside the figure ("Welch, 7 Hann segments", "Cosine taper, then parabola", "Sliding sinc window", "Multitaper, $L=7$"). IEEE TBME asks for no titles inside the figure; these should be relocated to the caption or reduced to short keyword labels.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest text (the in-panel annotations "shifts $n=0,32,\ldots,192$", "centred at $k=-128,0,128,256$", legend entries) is estimated at roughly 6.5–7.5 pt at final double-column size — close to the 6 pt hard floor. Verify after export; it must not fall below 6 pt.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: the estimator degrees of freedom $\nu$ are stated per panel (good), but the caption should also state explicitly that these are analytic/exact values with no averaging over realizations (currently implied by "Every curve and weight is computed exactly"), and give the number of data windows/segments behind each ladder (7, 12-ish, 12-ish, 7 as drawn) so the reader can tie $c_i$ counts to $\nu$. If $\nu$ values are compared across methods, note that they share the same $N=256$, $R=8/(N\Delta)$, $\alpha=4$ configuration.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (e)–(h) share a y-axis range but only (e) is labelled "Weight $c_i/\sum c_i$"; confirm the shared-axis convention is obvious in print, and that the x-axis extends to the same maximum $i$ in all four (the bars in (e) and (h) stop at $i=7$ while (f) and (g) run past $i=10$).
  - [ADVISORY] Colour is the only channel distinguishing bank members in (b)–(d); lightness ordering helps, but the two lightest tints are close. Grayscale reproduction may merge them.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as vector PDF or EPS at a physical canvas size of 181 x 85 mm (e.g. matplotlib `figsize=(7.13, 3.34)`, `savefig(..., format='pdf')`). This removes both blocking issues at once and will stay well under 10 MB.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for the 181 mm width, i.e. ≥4280 px wide (≥2010 px tall), and save as RGB TIFF with LZW compression.
  - Set all fonts to a sans-serif family (Arial/Helvetica) at 8 pt for axis labels and panel letters and no smaller than 7 pt for in-panel annotations and legends, specified at the final 181 mm size so no post-export scaling occurs.
  - Move the four column headings into the caption text (they already duplicate the caption's (a)–(d) descriptions), leaving only the lowercase (a)–(h) panel letters inside the figure.
  - Add to the caption: the number of tapers/segments contributing to each weight ladder, confirmation that curves are analytic (zero realizations, no error bars), and the definition already present for $\nu$ — plus an explicit note that $\nu$ is the estimator's equivalent degrees of freedom being compared across the four routes.
  - Increase lightness separation between the 3rd and 4th tints in (b)–(d), or add short direct labels on the curves, so the ordering survives grayscale and CMYK conversion.
  - Confirm the exported file has no CMYK or embedded output-intent profile; tag as sRGB.

DIMENSIONS:     2077 × 1097 px (aspect 1.89:1) — recommended **double column** — 290 DPI at 181 mm (7.16 in); 302 DPI at the 6.875 in text-block width; 593 DPI if forced to single column (3.5 in / 88 mm)
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (multi-panel line plots, insets, no continuous-tone content); 290 DPI at the recommended double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred for colored line art
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → vector PDF or EPS (preferred), or ≥600 DPI TIFF (LZW) if raster must be retained. PNG is acceptable for initial review only.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.18 MB (limit 10 MB); a re-export at 600 DPI or as vector will still be far under the limit
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis numerals, axis labels, panel letters and in-panel annotations are baked into the bitmap; pixel edges will be visible on the thin strokes of the italic math glyphs (𝒦(f), σ²Δ, ν) at print size
COLOR MODE:     Cannot verify — color_mode was not supplied in the metadata block. Confirm RGB. If the file is ever flattened to CMYK, the four series colors (teal ≈ #1BA778, mid-blue, saturated orange/amber, pink) are all in the at-risk set; the orange and teal will dull and the pink will shift toward magenta-grey, reducing separation from the black true-PSD curve.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution insufficient: 290 DPI at 181 mm double-column width fails the 300 DPI floor and is less than half the 600 DPI preferred for line art. The dense multitaper trace in (b) and the fine side-lobe ripple in (h) will alias or fill in as solid tone at print size.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Journal requires crisp vector text or ≥600 DPI raster; current 290 DPI text will show pixel stair-stepping, worst on the inset tick labels and italic symbols.
  - [BLOCKING] Format not accepted for final submission (PNG). Must be TIFF/EPS/PDF/PS.
  - [ADVISORY] Inset tick labels (0, −25, −50 and 0.39/0.4/0.41) measure roughly 20–23 px, i.e. ≈5–5.7 pt at 181 mm reproduction — below the 6 pt absolute minimum. Main-axis numerals compute to ≈7–8 pt, which is acceptable but at the low end.
  - [ADVISORY] Figure is 8 panels across 4 columns; at 181 mm each panel is only ~43 mm wide. Printed height is ~96 mm, well within the 230 mm limit, so there is headroom to use a 2×2 arrangement of (a–d) over (e–h) if panel width needs to grow.
  - [ADVISORY] Color mode unconfirmed in metadata; explicitly export RGB.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is mostly complete — the caption gives ν for each estimator (17.2, 14.0, 16.7, 17.6), states "one realization" for the displayed traces, and gives the 300-realization ensemble medians. However, the 22 dB and 14 dB leakage arrows and the "median for fΔ≥0.4" are single-realization quantities; state the number of frequency bins entering that median so the reader can gauge its stability. Also state explicitly that the per-panel ν values are the equivalent degrees of freedom of the respective smoothers, since these are the noise-level comparison being made across (a)–(d).
  - [ADVISORY] Caption contains no in-figure title and panel letters are lowercase parenthetical — compliant. The colored in-panel text ("Untapered, then box", etc.) functions as a legend rather than a title, which is acceptable, but note it relies on color to bind each label to its curve; a reader of a grayscale printout loses that mapping only mildly since panels are separate — acceptable as is.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting source (matplotlib: `savefig('figure6.pdf')` or `.eps`) as vector PDF/EPS with fonts embedded as Type 1/TrueType, not outlines-only, sized to exactly 181 mm width. This resolves the resolution, text-rendering and format blockers simultaneously.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at `dpi=600` for a 181 mm canvas (≈4276 × 2259 px) and save as LZW-compressed TIFF, RGB.
  - Set the figure size in the script rather than scaling after the fact: `figsize=(7.13, 3.76)` inches, then specify tick/label font sizes in points (main axes 8 pt, inset ticks ≥6.5 pt, panel letters 9–10 pt bold) so the on-page sizes are deterministic.
  - Enlarge the inset tick labels to ≥6.5 pt; if that crowds the insets, drop the intermediate −25 label or reduce the inset x-tick set to 0.39 and 0.41 only.
  - Confirm and lock RGB color mode on export; if the production stage converts to CMYK, pre-check the teal and orange series or substitute a CMYK-safe qualitative palette with luminance separation so the four estimators remain distinguishable.
  - Add to the caption the number of frequency bins used for the fΔ≥0.4 median that defines the 22 dB and 14 dB arrows, and label ν explicitly as the equivalent degrees of freedom of each estimator.
