### figure7.png

---
**DIMENSIONS:** 986 × 1226 px — recommended **single column** (88 mm / 3.5 in) — **≈282 DPI** at 88 mm (303 DPI if reduced to the 3.25 in text-block width; only ~138 DPI if run double-column at 181 mm)

Aspect ratio 0.80 (portrait, H/W = 1.24) is well suited to a stacked two-panel (a)/(b) spectrum + difference layout at single-column width; final printed height ≈ 109 mm, well under the 230 mm limit. Do **not** enlarge to double column — DPI would collapse to ~138.

**RESOLUTION:** Below threshold — figure type: **line art with color** (PSD traces, grid, text; no photographic or continuous-tone content). Actual ≈282 DPI at 88 mm vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred for line art.

**FORMAT:** Needs conversion — **PNG → EPS, PDF, or TIFF** for final submission (PNG is accepted for review only).

**FILE SIZE:** OK — 0.21 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to re-export at 600–1200 DPI.

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, legends and the in-panel grey-band annotation are bitmap glyphs; edges will soften/alias at print size and at reviewer zoom.

**COLOR MODE:** OK (RGB assumed per metadata requirement; RGB is the required mode). Potential shift only if a production step converts to CMYK: the saturated orange (cosine-taper trace) and the medium blue (multitaper trace) are the two most at-risk hues; the pink/mauve Welch trace may also desaturate. Blue vs. pink remain distinguishable, but blue/pink luminance is similar — a concern for grayscale reproduction.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Resolution is ~282 DPI at the recommended 88 mm width — below the 300 DPI floor and far below the 600 DPI preference for line art. Harmonic peaks and the thin ±1.6 dB grey band edges will show stair-stepping.
- [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. IEEE quality review commonly rejects bitmap-rendered axis/legend text at this pixel density.
- [BLOCKING] PNG is not an accepted final-submission format; must be supplied as vector (EPS/PDF) or high-resolution TIFF.
- [ADVISORY] Smallest text (tick labels and the two-line grey-band annotation in panel (b)) computes to roughly 7 pt at 88 mm — above the 6 pt floor but at the low end of the 8–10 pt target. Any further reduction in width would violate the minimum.
- [ADVISORY] Caption opens with a declarative claim ("The estimators used in practice agree to within their own noise."), which functions as a title. IEEE prefers a descriptive caption; move the interpretive claim into the body text or place it after the descriptive content.
- [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is largely compliant — ν is given for all three estimators (14, 16.7, 17.6), the number of frequencies compared is stated (1281), and the grey band is defined. However, the ±1.6 dB band is a *theoretical* χ²-based standard deviation (4.3√(2/ν)), not an empirical spread over realizations; the caption should say so explicitly so readers do not read it as an error bar from repeated windows. Also state that this is a single EEG epoch (n = 1 realization) rather than an ensemble, and give the epoch length / sampling rate that fix ν.
- [ADVISORY] Three-trace color set relies partly on hue (blue/orange/pink) with similar luminance for blue and pink; poor grayscale and CVD separability.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) legend text uses italic math *L* = 7 inside a rasterized label — if the figure is regenerated as vector, ensure math italics are preserved and match the caption notation.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Regenerate the figure from source (matplotlib/MATLAB) and export directly to **vector PDF or EPS** with fonts embedded — this simultaneously resolves the resolution, text-rendering, and format blockers at negligible file size.
- If a raster deliverable is mandated, export TIFF at **600 DPI minimum (1200 DPI preferred)** for a 88 mm width, i.e. ≥2080 px (ideally ≥4160 px) wide, LZW-compressed, RGB. At 600 DPI the file will still be well under 10 MB.
- Set figure width explicitly to 88 mm in the plotting script and specify fonts in points (labels 9–10 pt, ticks and legends 8 pt, annotation 8 pt) so that no text falls below 8 pt at final size; use a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) for consistency with IEEE preference, or keep Times consistently throughout if serif is intended.
- Differentiate the three traces by **line style as well as color** (e.g., solid multitaper, dashed cosine-taper/parabola, dash-dot Welch) and consider a CVD-safe palette; this preserves legibility in grayscale and under CMYK conversion.
- Revise the caption to lead with description, e.g.: "(a) PSD of the EEG epoch of Fig. X (dB re 1 µV²/Hz) estimated by three methods: Thomson multitaper, L = 7 (ν = 14); cosine-tapered periodogram smoothed by a parabola of half-power width R (ν = 16.7); Welch, nine Hann segments of 1.5 s stepped by 0.81 s (ν = 17.6). …" and move the "agree to within their own noise" claim to the end or to the text.
- Add to the caption: the single-epoch nature of the estimate (one realization, not an ensemble), the epoch duration and sampling rate, and an explicit statement that the grey ±1.6 dB band is the asymptotic χ²_ν standard deviation for ν = 14 rather than a measured dispersion.
- Confirm the exported file's color mode is RGB and avoid any CMYK conversion; if the publisher converts, soft-proof the orange and blue traces and darken/lighten one of blue/pink to open a luminance gap.

### figure8.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1835 x 1745 px — recommended **double** column — **267 DPI** at 6.875 in (564 DPI if forced to 3.25 in single column). Aspect ratio 1.05 (nearly square); at double-column width the figure prints 175 x 166 mm, well within the 230 mm height limit. An 8-panel layout containing three wide spectrograms cannot be legibly reduced to 88 mm, so double column is the only viable option.
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — mixed figure (halftone spectrograms + line-art traces/histogram); actual 267 DPI at double column vs. 300 DPI minimum for halftone and 600 DPI preferred for line art.
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG (review-acceptable only) → vector PDF or EPS preferred (spectrogram rasters embedded at ≥600 DPI); flattened 600-DPI TIFF/LZW is an acceptable fallback.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 1.59 MB (limit 10 MB); a 600-DPI TIFF re-export will grow substantially, so use LZW/ZIP compression to stay under 10 MB.
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick numbers, panel letters and in-panel annotations are pixel-bound PNG content; edges will alias at print size, and the smallest strings (legend entries in (f), "median |difference| 0.9 dB" in (h)) are the most vulnerable.
COLOR MODE:     Assumed RGB (metadata not supplied — confirm). **Potential shift** — the jet colormap's saturated deep blue (low PSD) and pure red/dark-red (ictal peaks) are exactly the hues that compress under RGB→CMYK; blue-to-cyan and red-to-dark-brown shifts would change the apparent contrast between (c), (e) and (g).
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] 267 DPI at the recommended double-column width is below the 300 DPI halftone minimum and far below the 600 DPI line-art preference.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and line work are rasterized rather than vector; in a line-art/graph figure this is a quality failure for IEEE production.
  - [BLOCKING] PNG is not an acceptable final-submission format for IEEE TBME (accepted for review only).
  - [ADVISORY] Color mode is not declared in the file metadata; embed an explicit sRGB profile and confirm RGB before upload.
  - [ADVISORY] The jet colormap is not perceptually uniform and is poor for color-vision-deficient readers; the yellow-green band creates a false "edge" in the 5–10 Hz region of (c), (e) and (g) that could be misread as a spectral feature.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (c), (e), (g), (d), (f), (h) each carry a text title inside the axes ("Periodogram: 2 s by 0.5 Hz, ν = 2.0", "Before the seizure, t = 247 s", "Panel (e) minus panel (g)", etc.). IEEE style asks that titles not appear inside the figure; these read as panel titles rather than data annotations.
  - [ADVISORY] The single shared colorbar sits vertically between the two columns and is not visually tied to (c), (e) and (g); a reader may not realize it applies to all three, and it is ambiguous whether it also applies to the line plots.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (c) and (e) have unlabeled x-axes (only (g) carries "Time (s)"); acceptable for a stacked shared axis but the three spectrograms are separated by full panel gaps, weakening the shared-axis reading.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is otherwise strong — window count (599), window length/step, degrees of freedom for every estimator (ν = 2, 6, 8.7, 7.7), and the pixel count behind the (h) histogram (144,359 = 599 × 241) are all stated. Consider also stating the bin width used in (h) and whether the median |difference| is over the same 144,359 pixels.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting source at double-column width (6.875 in / 181 mm) as vector PDF or EPS so all text, axes, traces and the histogram remain vector; let only the three spectrogram image layers and the colorbar be raster, rendered at ≥600 DPI (e.g. matplotlib `rasterized=True` on the `pcolormesh`/`imshow` artists plus `savefig(..., dpi=600)`).
  - If a raster-only deliverable is required, regenerate at ≥4125 px wide (600 DPI × 6.875 in) — not by upsampling the existing PNG — and save as LZW-compressed TIFF, RGB.
  - Verify final font sizes after export: axis labels ≈10 pt, tick labels ≈8 pt, and confirm the (f) legend and the (h) "median |difference| 0.9 dB" annotation are ≥6 pt at 181 mm.
  - Embed an sRGB profile and state RGB in the submission form; if the journal requests CMYK, soft-proof the jet ramp and check that the −13 dB blue and +22 dB dark red remain distinguishable.
  - Replace jet with a perceptually uniform, print-safe map (viridis, cividis, or a diverging map if a reference level is meaningful) and keep the identical −13 to 22 dB limits across (c), (e), (g).
  - Move the in-panel titles into the caption, or reduce them to short keyed labels (e.g. "ν = 2", "ν = 6", "ν = 8.7") beside the panel letters, with the full descriptions in the caption text.
  - Tie the colorbar to the spectrogram column: place it flush to the right edge of (c), (e), (g) spanning their combined height, or give each spectrogram its own short colorbar, and state explicitly in the caption that it applies to (c), (e) and (g) only.
  - Add "Time (s)" tick-label context or a light connecting frame so the shared x-axis of the three spectrograms is unambiguous; alternatively reduce vertical gaps between (c), (e) and (g).
  - In the caption, add the (h) bin width and confirm the number of realizations behind each ν value (number of tapers / Welch segments per window) so the noise-level comparison is fully reproducible.

### figure9.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2002 × 2091 px — recommended **double** column — 280 DPI at 181 mm (7.16 in); 291 DPI if the narrower 6.875 in text block is used. At single column (88 mm / 3.5 in) it would be 572 DPI, but an 8-panel figure with six spectrograms and a shared colourbar is unreadable at 88 mm, so single column is not viable. Aspect ratio 0.96 (W/H) is acceptable: at 181 mm width the printed height is ≈189 mm, within the 230 mm limit.

RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — mixed figure (halftone/continuous-tone spectrograms in b–g + line art in a, h and all axes/text); actual 280 DPI at the recommended double-column width. Fails the 300 DPI halftone minimum and is far below the 600 DPI preference for the line-art content.

FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → TIFF (LZW) or, preferably, vector PDF/EPS with the spectrogram images embedded as rasters. PNG is accepted for review only.

FILE SIZE:      OK — 1.79 MB (well under 10 MB). Note that a flat 600 DPI RGB TIFF at 181 mm would be ~50–60 MB uncompressed and likely >10 MB even with LZW; see fixes.

TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all labels, tick marks, axis rules, the (a)–(h) panel letters and the white resolution boxes are baked into the bitmap. Pixel-stepped edges are visible on thin axis lines and on the italic ν/α glyphs. For a figure whose line-art content carries the quantitative message, this is blocking.

COLOR MODE:     OK for submission (RGB), with **potential shift** risk if the publisher converts to CMYK: the jet-style colourmap in b–g is dominated by fully saturated blues (≈0,0,255) and reds/oranges that gamut-clip in CMYK, which will compress the −5 to +2.5 dB blue end and the 10–12.5 dB red end and can make the deep-red saturated (clipped) regions indistinguishable from the 12.5 dB ceiling. The blue (multitaper) and orange (smoothing) series colours in (h) are also in the at-risk range.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] 280 DPI at the required double-column width is below the 300 DPI halftone minimum and far below the 600 DPI line-art preference. The current pixel budget cannot satisfy either criterion at 181 mm.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and all line art are rasterized. Axis rules, tick labels and the in-panel white resolution rectangles will show pixel stepping in print; the thin grey guide lines in (h) are at risk of dropping out or aliasing.
  - [BLOCKING] PNG is not an accepted final-submission format; TIFF/EPS/PDF/PS required.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest text is marginal. At 280 DPI the axis tick labels measure ≈7.5–8 pt, but the in-panel annotation "resolution of the panel", the "11−16 Hz, offset" label and the 1.1 Hz / ν = 2.0 style callouts in (h) fall to roughly 6–6.5 pt at final size — at or just above the 6 pt floor with no margin if the figure is scaled down.
  - [ADVISORY] The descriptive strings above panels (b)–(g) ("Multitaper, L = 1: 1 s by 1.1 Hz, ν = 2.0", etc.) read as in-figure panel titles, which IEEE asks to be moved to the caption. They are arguably legends rather than titles, but a production editor may flag them.
  - [ADVISORY] Typeface appears to be a serif (Computer Modern / Latin Modern) family rather than the preferred sans-serif. Times-class serif is tolerated, but Arial/Helvetica is preferred and gives better legibility at 6–8 pt.
  - [ADVISORY] Jet-style colourmap is not perceptually uniform: it creates false banding in the 5–10 dB band and is poorly distinguishable for red–green colour-vision deficiency. The saturated dark-red floor below ~3 Hz is clipped in all six panels, hiding how much of the low-frequency power exceeds the 12.5 dB ceiling.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is only half-complete. The estimator degrees of freedom ν are given for every configuration (good, and required here because noise/variance levels are being compared), but the caption does not state the number of realizations behind each spectrogram column or the sample count in (h). A reader cannot tell that (h) shows single deterministic design quantities rather than sample means, and there are no error bars to justify.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption does not state the sampling rate, the number of subjects/recordings (this appears to be a single 40-s epoch from one subject), or how many windows each spectrogram contains, all of which are needed to reproduce the ν values.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting source at 181 mm (7.16 in) width as a **vector PDF or EPS**, with the six spectrograms embedded as raster images at 400–600 DPI and all axes, ticks, text and the white resolution boxes kept as vector objects. This resolves the resolution, text-rendering and format issues simultaneously and keeps the file a few MB.
  - If a flat raster is unavoidable, re-render at ≥ 4300 px wide (600 DPI at 181 mm) and save as LZW-compressed TIFF; verify the result is under 10 MB, and if it is not, drop the spectrogram rasters to 300–400 DPI while keeping 600 DPI for the overlaid line art (i.e. use the mixed-resolution vector route instead).
  - Raise the smallest annotation text so that nothing is below 7 pt at 181 mm final width; specifically enlarge "resolution of the panel", "11−16 Hz, offset", and the numeric callouts in (h). Set the base font to Arial or Helvetica.
  - Replace jet with a perceptually uniform, print-safe map (viridis, or a diverging map if a reference level is meaningful) and either extend the colourbar to the true maximum or add an explicit "≥ 12.5 dB clipped" note so the saturated low-frequency band is not misread.
  - Move the method descriptions above panels (b)–(g) into the caption, keyed by panel letter, leaving only the lowercase (a)–(h) labels inside the figure; keep the panel letters at a consistent size and position.
  - Add to the caption: sampling rate, that this is a single 40-s N2 epoch from one recording (n = 1), the number of windows per spectrogram (391 for 1 s, 381 for 2 s, 361 for 4 s at a 0.1 s step over 40 s — state the actual values), and an explicit statement that (h) plots design quantities (achieved half-power width and equivalent degrees of freedom ν) rather than sample estimates, hence the absence of error bars.
  - Confirm the RGB colour profile is embedded (sRGB) and ask production to keep the figure RGB; if CMYK conversion is mandatory, soft-proof panels (b)–(g) and the blue/orange markers in (h) for gamut clipping first.

## Caption & Legend Auditor

### efigure1.png

---
TITLE:              Missing — the legend opens with "Fig.~5 in full.", a cross-reference rather than a bold descriptive title.
PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Partial — rows (a–d), (e–h), (i–l), (m–p) are described, but the four *columns* (the four estimators) are never named in the caption; they are deferred to Fig. 5 and to in-figure titles. Individual panels are not addressable (e.g. what (f) or (k) shows individually).
ERROR BARS:         Not present — all curves/bars are deterministic; caption should state this explicitly ("no error bars; deterministic taper/kernel computations").
SAMPLE SIZE:        Missing — N (series length; apparently 256), the number of tapers/segments per column (7 Hann segments, L = 7, and the 12-ish bars shown in (j)/(k)), the Welch overlap (shifts n = 0, 32, …, 192 appear only in-figure), and the time–bandwidth product NW / R value are not given in the caption.
STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — the equivalent degrees of freedom ν are printed in (i)–(l) (13.4, 16.7, 17.2, 14.0) but ν is never defined in the caption, nor is the estimator used to compute it (e.g. ν = 2(Σc_i)²/Σc_i²). No noise-level comparison or test is claimed, so no test statistic is needed, but per journal policy the degrees of freedom must be stated in the legend text where estimators are compared.
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: ν; N; Δ; R; L; Q; 𝒦(f); c_i; i; k; n; f′; "max h"; dB reference level (stated as "relative to its peak" — OK); "Slepian sequences"; "Thomson's estimate"; "Hann"; "half-power width".
COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Partial — in-figure keys exist for i = 1–4 and f′NΔ = 0–3, and the dotted vertical lines are defined (|f| = R/2), but the column colour code (pink/orange/green/blue = which estimator) is only given by reference to Fig. 5.
SELF-CONTAINED:     No — a reader cannot identify the four estimators, N, R, or the meaning of ν, Q, 𝒦(f) from this figure plus legend alone.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] No bold, self-contained descriptive title; the legend begins as a cross-reference ("Fig. 5 in full"), so the figure cannot stand alone.
  - [BLOCKING] Column identity (the four estimators) and colour key are outsourced to Fig. 5; must be restated here.
  - [BLOCKING] ν (equivalent degrees of freedom) is displayed in (i)–(l) but undefined, and the estimator/formula for it is not given — required when noise levels/estimators are compared.
  - [BLOCKING] Key parameters absent: N (= 256?), sampling interval Δ, resolution bandwidth R, number of tapers/segments per column, Welch segment length and overlap.
  - [BLOCKING] No statement that the panels are deterministic computations with no realizations behind them (or, if any panel is Monte-Carlo, the number of realizations/windows).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel column titles are placed inside the figure ("Welch, 7 Hann segments", "Cosine taper, then parabola", "Sliding sinc window", "Multitaper, L = 7"); IEEE TBME asks for no titles inside the figure — move them to the caption or convert to neutral column labels.
  - [ADVISORY] The list "5.84, 8.03, 8.03 and 7.41/(NΔ)" implies the unit only on the last value; ambiguous. Also state which column each number belongs to.
  - [ADVISORY] Percentages of out-of-band power (0.05%, 0.13%, 1.39%, 0.22%) are given without defining the integration limits notation |f| ≤ R in words or the normalisation of the kernel.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (h) text "the bank itself, as in (d)" is an in-figure annotation that duplicates caption content; keep one location and explain the redundancy (eigen-tapers of Q equal the taper bank for the multitaper case).
  - [ADVISORY] Axis-label/annotation type size should be verified at final width (this is a 4×4 grid — at 181 mm the smallest in-figure text, e.g. the panel annotations, must remain ≥6 pt).
  - [ADVISORY] PNG at 2088×1934 px ≈ 293 DPI at 181 mm — below the 300 DPI minimum for colour line art; supply vector EPS/PDF (preferred, ≥600 DPI equivalent).
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Rewrite the opening as a bold standalone title, e.g. **"Construction, eigen-structure, weight ladder and spectral kernel of four multitaper-equivalent spectrum estimators."**
  - Name the columns explicitly in the caption: "Columns (left to right): Welch with 7 overlapping Hann segments (pink); cosine taper followed by parabolic weighting (orange); sliding sinc window (green); Thomson multitaper with L = 7 tapers (blue)."
  - Add parameters: "All panels use N = 256 samples, sampling interval Δ, and resolution bandwidth R = … /(NΔ); Welch segments are of length … with 50% overlap (shifts n = 0, 32, …, 192)."
  - Describe rows with panel letters tied to columns: "(a)–(d) taper bank as constructed; (e)–(h) first four eigen-tapers h_k^(i) of the quadratic-form matrix Q, where Q is …; (i)–(l) normalised eigen-weights c_i/Σc_i; (m)–(p) spectral kernel 𝒦(f) in dB relative to its peak, dotted lines at |f| = R/2."
  - Define ν and its estimator: "ν denotes the equivalent degrees of freedom of each estimator, ν = 2(Σ_i c_i)²/Σ_i c_i² (values 13.4, 16.7, 17.2 and 14.0 for the four columns)."
  - Add: "All curves are deterministic (analytically/numerically computed taper and kernel functions); no averaging over realizations is involved, hence no error bars."
  - Define every symbol used in axes/annotations: h_{k−n}, h_k^(i), max h, c_i, i, k, n, f′, 𝒦(f), Δ, R, L, and the dB reference.
  - Restate the summary statistics with explicit pairing and units: "Half-power kernel widths are 5.84, 8.03, 8.03 and 7.41 (in units of 1/(NΔ)) for Welch, cosine–parabola, sliding sinc and multitaper, respectively; the corresponding out-of-band power fractions (|f| > R) are 0.05%, 0.13%, 1.39% and 0.22%."
  - Move in-figure column titles into the caption and re-export the figure as vector PDF/EPS (or ≥600 DPI TIFF) at 181 mm width, confirming all text ≥6 pt.

### efigure2.png

---
TITLE:              Not bold — "Dropping the leakiest taper." is present as a lead phrase but is not set in bold, and it is a topical slogan rather than a descriptive title of what is shown
PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete — (a) and (b) both described, in order, lowercase labels match the figure
ERROR BARS:         Not present — single-realization traces; no error bars or shaded bands are plotted, so none require definition. (Equivalent noise information is instead carried by the stated ν values — good practice for this journal.)
SAMPLE SIZE:        Stated (partially) — "One realization" and N = 1024 samples are given; however the number of frequency bins (or realizations) underlying the median differences quoted for f∆ ≥ 0.4 in (b) is not stated
STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — no hypothesis test is performed or claimed (acceptable here), but: (i) the estimator degrees of freedom ν are given as bare numbers without ν ever being named as "equivalent degrees of freedom"; (ii) the medians quoted in (b) are reported without stating the population they are taken over (frequencies in 0.4 ≤ f∆ ≤ 0.5, count unspecified) ; (iii) no dispersion (e.g., interquartile range) accompanies the median bias values
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: PSD (power spectral density), AR(4) (fourth-order autoregressive), ν (equivalent degrees of freedom), L (number of Slepian/discrete prolate spheroidal tapers), R (smoothing half-power bandwidth — used in the header "R = 8/(N∆)" before being explained), ∆ (sampling interval), f∆ (normalized frequency), dB re σ²∆ (reference level convention is explained but the "re" notation is not), "box" and "parabola" (smoothing-kernel shapes), "adaptive" (adaptive weighting scheme, e.g. Thomson's)
COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Present — in-figure legend in (a), right-hand end labels in (b), and both restated in the caption; colors in the caption match the plot
SELF-CONTAINED:     No — a reader cannot interpret ν, L, R, ∆, the taper family used for the multitaper estimates (Slepian? time-bandwidth product NW?), the adaptive weighting rule, or the smoothing kernels without the main text
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Caption title is not bold and is not descriptive; rubric requires a bold, informative first sentence.
  - [BLOCKING] Degrees-of-freedom symbol ν is used repeatedly but never defined; this is precisely the quantity the journal requires to be stated when noise levels are compared, so it must be named explicitly.
  - [BLOCKING] Key symbols L, R, ∆, and the taper family/time-bandwidth product are undefined in the caption; the multitaper estimates cannot be reproduced or interpreted from figure + caption alone.
  - [BLOCKING] The quantitative claims in (b) ("18 dB above ... in the median", "10 dB", "lowers that to 4 dB") report a median without stating the number of frequency bins it summarizes or any measure of spread — required by the journal's statistical-reporting rule for distributions.
  - [ADVISORY] Effective resolution is ~281 DPI at 181 mm double-column width (2012 px), just below the 300 DPI minimum; re-export at ≥2140 px wide (preferably as vector EPS/PDF, since this is line art).
  - [ADVISORY] PNG is accepted for review only; supply TIFF/EPS/PDF for production.
  - [ADVISORY] The grey periodogram appears in (a) but is absent from (b); the caption says "each smoothed estimate minus the true PSD" without noting that the raw periodogram is deliberately omitted, which may read as an inconsistency.
  - [ADVISORY] "the smoothed periodogram" in the final sentence is ambiguous — three curves in the figure are smoothed periodograms (box, parabola, and arguably the multitaper). Name the orange cosine-taper/parabola estimate explicitly.
  - [ADVISORY] The adaptive estimate's frequency-dependent ν (14 → 6.5) is stated in prose only; consider plotting it as an inset or stating the frequency at which the transition occurs, since it underpins the final claim.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) has no in-figure legend box; the right-hand text labels crowd the plot edge and one label ("Cosine taper, then parabola") sits on the zero line. Check that all labels are ≥6 pt at 181 mm and do not overlap the axis frame.
  - [ADVISORY] The two panels use different y-ranges and the (b) labels extend beyond the axes; confirm nothing is clipped at final trim width.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Recast the opening as a bold descriptive title, e.g. **"Effect of dropping the leakiest Slepian taper on multitaper PSD bias for an AR(4) process."**
  - Add a definitions clause early: "∆, sampling interval; f∆, normalized frequency; L, number of Slepian (DPSS) tapers with time–bandwidth product NW = ...; ν, equivalent degrees of freedom of the estimator; R, half-power bandwidth of the smoothing kernel (R = 8/(N∆)); PSD, power spectral density; AR(4), fourth-order autoregressive process."
  - Spell out the adaptive scheme and its reference on first use, e.g. "adaptively weighted multitaper estimate (Thomson's adaptive weights), whose ν varies with frequency from 14 to 6.5."
  - Quantify the (b) statistics fully: "Median difference over the n = ... frequency bins with 0.4 ≤ f∆ ≤ 0.5 (interquartile range in parentheses): untapered-then-box +18 dB (...), multitaper L = 7 +10 dB (...), L = 6 +4 dB (...), adaptive ... dB (...), cosine-taper-then-parabola ... dB (...)."
  - State explicitly that all traces come from the same single realization (n = 1 realization; no averaging across realizations) so readers do not mistake the traces for means.
  - Add one clause noting that the raw periodogram is shown only in (a) and is omitted from (b) for clarity.
  - Replace "the smoothed periodogram" with "the cosine-tapered periodogram smoothed with the parabola (orange)".
  - Re-export as vector EPS/PDF (or ≥600 DPI TIFF for line art) at exactly 181 mm width, with all text in Arial/Helvetica ≥8 pt at final size; confirm RGB color mode and <10 MB.
  - Optionally move the (b) end-labels into a compact in-panel legend to avoid label/axis collisions after downscaling to 181 mm.

### efigure3.png

---
TITLE:              Missing — caption opens with a descriptive noun phrase ("Kernels at the same design resolution…") but there is no bold lead-in title sentence.
PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete — (a) linear scale near the main lobe, (b) dB relative to peak; both described in order and in lowercase, matching the in-figure labels.
ERROR BARS:         Not present — figure shows deterministic, exactly computed kernels; no error bars or distributions are plotted, so no definition is required.
SAMPLE SIZE:        Not applicable in the biological sense; the relevant analogue (N = 256 samples, L = 7 tapers) is given numerically but the symbols are not defined. Effectively "Partial".
STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — no statistical test is applicable, and the estimator degrees of freedom are commendably given for every curve (ν = 14.0, 16.7, 17.2, 8.9, 7.3). However, the symbol ν is never identified as "equivalent degrees of freedom," and the basis of the ν calculation (e.g., Welch/Bartlett-type ratio of moments of the kernel) is not stated, so a reader cannot verify or compare noise levels as the journal requires.
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: ν (equivalent degrees of freedom); 𝒦(f) (the smoothing/spectral kernel plotted on both axes); N (record length in samples); Δ (sampling interval); R (design resolution/bandwidth); L (number of Slepian tapers); "recipe (b′)" (external label); "box," "parabola," "skirt" used without definition; Bohman and Hann named without a statement that they are data tapers.
COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Present — in-figure legend in (a) plus direct curve labels in (b), and the caption redundantly restates colors and the dotted/dashed line styles. Consistent and adequate.
SELF-CONTAINED:     No — the reader must consult Table 2 for the resolution convention and the main text to learn what "recipe (b′)" is, what 𝒦(f) denotes, how ν is computed, and why the y-axis of (a) is normalized by NΔ.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] No bold title sentence at the head of the legend, as required by the journal's caption style.
  - [BLOCKING] The ordinate quantity 𝒦(f) is never defined in the caption. Since the entire figure is a plot of 𝒦(f), the figure is not interpretable from the legend alone.
  - [BLOCKING] ν appears five times as the key quantitative descriptor but is never defined; per the journal's requirement, the estimator's degrees of freedom must be explicitly named and its definition/source identified when noise levels are compared.
  - [BLOCKING] "recipe (b′)" is an unresolvable cross-reference to the main text and additionally collides with the panel label "(b)", creating a real risk of misreading. Panel labels and recipe labels must be typographically distinct.
  - [ADVISORY] N, Δ, R, and L are used without definition; the normalization 𝒦(f)/NΔ in (a) and the abscissa fNΔ are unexplained, so the numerical axis values carry no meaning for the reader.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption states half-power widths of 7.4/(NΔ) vs 8.0/(NΔ) but does not say how half-power width was measured, nor why the multitaper kernel is narrower despite the common design resolution R = 8/(NΔ).
  - [ADVISORY] The claim "a range of 51 dB" is a derived number the reader cannot check, because the −24 dB and −75 dB values are read off at a single frequency not marked in panel (b). Consider adding a thin vertical guide at |f| = 12/(NΔ).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) and (b) use different abscissa ranges (±10 vs ±24) with no statement that (b) is a wider view of the same kernels; add "note the different abscissa range."
  - [ADVISORY] Two blues are used (solid dark blue for multitaper, dashed light blue for Bohman). At 88 mm reproduction or in greyscale these may be confused; the caption's reliance on "blue" vs "light blue, dashed" is fragile.
  - [ADVISORY] Figure aspect ratio (2018 x 677 px) implies ~7.16 in double-column width at only ~282 DPI. Below the 300 DPI minimum for color line art; 600 DPI vector output (EPS/PDF) is preferred.
  - [ADVISORY] Confirm axis-label and tick font sizes are >= 6 pt (ideally 8-10 pt) after scaling to 181 mm; the in-figure legend text in (a) is the smallest element and should be checked.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add a bold title as the first sentence, e.g. "**Smoothing kernels matched at a common design resolution trade main-lobe shape against sidelobe suppression.**"
  - Define 𝒦(f) in the first clause after the title: "𝒦(f) is the effective spectral smoothing kernel of each estimator; 𝒦(f)/NΔ in (a) is normalized to unit area, and (b) plots 10 log10[𝒦(f)/𝒦(0)]."
  - Define ν explicitly and cite its formula/source: "ν denotes the equivalent degrees of freedom of the resulting spectral estimate, computed as [formula/reference]; larger ν implies lower estimator variance."
  - Define N, Δ, R, and L on first use: "N = 256 samples, Δ = sampling interval, R = 8/(NΔ) = design resolution (Table 2), L = number of Slepian tapers."
  - Rename "recipe (b′)" to avoid collision with panel (b) — e.g. "Recipe II" or "the cosine-taper/parabola recipe of Section X" — and, if the label must be retained, spell out what the recipe is in one clause so the caption stands alone.
  - State the measurement convention for half-power width, and add one clause explaining why the multitaper kernel is narrower (7.4 vs 8.0/(NΔ)) at the same nominal R.
  - Mark |f| = 12/(NΔ) with a light vertical dotted guide in (b) so the quoted −24 dB / −75 dB skirt values are verifiable from the figure.
  - Add "Note the different abscissa ranges in (a) and (b)" to the caption.
  - Increase contrast between the two blue curves (e.g. make Bohman purple or black-dashed) or annotate Bohman directly in (a) as well as (b).
  - Re-export as vector EPS/PDF, or raster at >= 600 DPI for the final 181 mm width; verify all text is >= 6 pt and sans-serif at final size, RGB color mode, < 10 MB.

### efigure4.png

---
TITLE:              Missing — the caption opens with a descriptive phrase ("Variance, leakage and side-lobe level against the measured resolution, for $N=256$.") but it is not set as a bold lead-in title.
PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete — (a), (b), (c) all described in order, and the in-figure labels are lowercase parenthesized as required.
ERROR BARS:         Not present — correctly so; the caption explicitly states "exact kernel and degrees-of-freedom computations, with no sampling variability," which satisfies the journal's requirement to account for variability even when none is shown.
SAMPLE SIZE:        Stated (partially) — record length $N=256$ is given, but the number of bandwidth-parameter values swept per method (i.e., the number of plotted points/range of $\alpha$, segment length, or Gaussian width) is not.
STATISTICAL DETAIL: Not applicable / Complete for this figure type — no inferential test is performed; the estimator degrees of freedom $\nu$ are reported explicitly in panel (a), satisfying the IEEE TBME requirement that degrees of freedom be stated where noise levels are compared. No p-values or test statistics are needed.
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: $\Delta$ (sampling interval), $\alpha$ (time–bandwidth product of the Slepian sequences), $L$ (number of tapers) is used in the in-figure legend but only implicitly defined in the caption text, $N$ (record length, implied but not spelled out), dB (acceptable as standard). "Half-power width" and "half-power half-width" are used without definition of the reference kernel.
COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Present — comprehensive in-figure legend at lower right, and the caption enumerates the nine methods and explains the filled-marker convention.
SELF-CONTAINED:     No — the caption depends on "Table 2" for the meaning of the dotted reference line, and the key symbols $\alpha$, $\Delta$, $L$ are not defined within the caption. A reader also cannot tell what quantity the half-power width is measured on (the spectral estimator's smoothing kernel) without the main text.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] No bold lead-in title as the first sentence of the legend. IEEE TBME requires a brief descriptive caption with no title inside the figure; the journal house style used here expects a bolded title phrase to open the legend.
  - [BLOCKING] Undefined symbols in a figure whose axes are entirely symbolic: $\Delta$ appears on all three x-axis labels ("Half-power width $\times N\Delta$") and is never defined; $\alpha$ appears in the in-figure legend ($L=2\alpha-1$, $L=2\alpha-2$) and is never defined. A reader cannot interpret the abscissa at all without these.
  - [BLOCKING] The dotted vertical reference line is explained only by cross-reference to "Table 2." The numerical value $8/(N\Delta)$ is given, but why this width is the benchmark is unrecoverable from the figure alone.
  - [ADVISORY] The number of parameter values swept per method (number of markers per curve) and the swept ranges are not stated, so the reader cannot tell why curves terminate at different abscissa values (e.g., Welch extends to ~24.5 while multitaper stops near 15).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) uses a logarithmic ordinate spanning ~10$^{-9}$ to 10$^{-2}$; the caption does not state that the scale is logarithmic or that "kernel power" is normalized to unit total kernel power.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) ordinate is "dB relative to its maximum" per the caption, but the axis label says only "(dB)". Acceptable, though the normalization reference should also appear on the axis or be flagged as normalized.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption's enumeration of methods ("untapered, Hann and Bohman periodograms followed by a box") does not use the exact wording of the in-figure legend entries ("Untapered, then box", "Hann, then box", "Bohman, then box"), forcing the reader to map between two vocabularies.
  - [ADVISORY] Two free-floating text annotations inside the axes ("8/($N\Delta$), the width in Table 2" and "filled markers: no flat top, so this is the skirt of the main lobe") duplicate caption content; verify they render at $\ge$6 pt at final width and are not mistaken for figure titles.
  - [ADVISORY] Layout: the legend occupies the empty fourth quadrant. At 181 mm double-column width this is efficient, but confirm all legend text and axis labels are 8–10 pt at final size; at 2086 px wide the current file is ~293 DPI at 181 mm, marginally below the 300 DPI minimum for color line art (600 DPI preferred). Resupply as vector EPS/PDF.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Add a bold lead-in title, e.g. **"Variance, spectral leakage and side-lobe level versus achieved resolution for nine spectral estimators."**
  - Define all symbols on first use in the caption: "$N=256$ samples at sampling interval $\Delta$; $\alpha$ is the time–bandwidth product and $L$ the number of tapers retained."
  - Make the reference line self-contained: "The dotted vertical line marks a half-power width of $8/(N\Delta)$, the common resolution at which the estimators are compared in Table 2."
  - State the sweep: "Each curve shows $k$ bandwidth settings ($\alpha=2,\dots$; segment lengths $\dots$); curves terminate where the corresponding parameter reaches its maximum admissible value for $N=256$."
  - State the normalizations explicitly: "(b) Fraction of total kernel power falling beyond twice the half-power half-width (logarithmic ordinate). (c) Peak kernel level beyond $1.5\times$ the half-power half-width, in dB relative to the kernel maximum."
  - Harmonize method names between caption and in-figure legend, using the legend strings verbatim.
  - Keep and slightly expand the existing no-variability statement, e.g. "All quantities are exact analytic/numerical evaluations of the estimator kernels and their equivalent degrees of freedom; no Monte Carlo realizations are involved, so no error bars are shown."
  - Resupply as vector EPS or PDF (or $\ge$600 DPI TIFF) at 181 mm width, RGB, sans-serif labels $\ge$8 pt, under 10 MB.

### figure1.png

---
TITLE:              Not bold — a descriptive opening sentence exists ("Three routes to one estimate of the power spectral density…") but it is a full sentence, not a short run-in title, and it is not set in bold.

PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete — (a), (b), (c) are described in order and match the lowercase in-figure labels.

ERROR BARS:         Not present — all traces and markers are single-realization quantities; no dispersion is plotted. Caption correctly identifies "one epoch," so no error-bar definition is required, but see ISSUES for the missing count of sliding windows.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Stated — one channel, one 8-s epoch, N = 1024 samples at 128 Hz. Missing: the number of sliding-window positions (realizations) averaged in the sinc-window estimate, and the number of N_s values probed in (c) (visibly 5).

STATISTICAL DETAIL: Not applicable (no hypothesis test) — however, estimator degrees of freedom are only partially reported: ν = 17 is given for the multitaper estimate, but the caption does not state that (or whether) the smoothed-periodogram and sliding-sinc estimates carry the same ν, even though panel (b) compares their numerical agreement. Under the journal's "state the estimator's degrees of freedom where noise levels are compared" rule this should be explicit for all three routes.

ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: EEG (electroencephalogram/-graphy); α (presumably the time–bandwidth product — symbol appears only as "α = 4" with no definition); Δ (sampling interval, used inside R = 8/(NΔ) but never named); "Max." (figure axis, trivial but expand in caption text); ν is used but its definition is outsourced to a section cross-reference. PSD, dB, N, R, N_s, λ_i are adequately introduced.

COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Present — in-figure legend in (a) plus inline colored labels in (b); caption redundantly maps thick blue / dashed green / dotted dark green and the dotted 1/N_s guide line in (c). Open-circle markers in (c) are not named in the caption (minor).

SELF-CONTAINED:     No — the definition of ν (and hence the meaning of "equivalent degrees of freedom") is deferred to Section~\ref{sec:periodogram}; α and Δ are undefined; EEG is unexpanded; and the axis of (c) is N_s/N while the caption describes it as "window length N_s," so a reader cannot map caption to axis without inference.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Caption title is not a short bold run-in title. IEEE TBME expects a brief descriptive caption; the rubric requires a bold, concise leading title. Current opening is a 60-word sentence carrying the entire experimental setup.
  - [BLOCKING] In-caption cross-reference ("as defined in Section~\ref{sec:periodogram}") breaks the self-containment requirement for the one quantity (ν) that the journal explicitly requires to be reported in the legend. Define ν in the caption itself.
  - [BLOCKING] Degrees of freedom are reported for only one of the three estimates whose noise levels/differences are being compared in (b) and (c). State ν for all three (or state explicitly that they are identical by construction).
  - [BLOCKING] Undefined symbols/abbreviations in the caption: α, Δ, EEG. A reader cannot reconstruct R = 8/(NΔ) = 1 Hz or verify α = 4 without them.
  - [BLOCKING] Raster resolution: 1830 px across a 181 mm (7.16 in) double-column width is ≈256 DPI, below the ≥300 DPI minimum (600 DPI preferred for line art). Regenerate as vector EPS/PDF or re-render at ≥600 DPI.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) abscissa is the ratio N_s/N (1, 2, 4, 8, 16), but the caption says "against the window length N_s." Fix the wording or the axis label so they agree.
  - [ADVISORY] Number of realizations behind the sliding-sinc average is not given. "Slid across the record one sample at a time" implies N − N_s + 1 positions, which is negative for N_s = 16N — the reader cannot tell whether the record is zero-padded, circularly extended, or longer than the 8 s epoch. This ambiguity also undermines panel (c).
  - [ADVISORY] "agrees to 3×10⁻¹³ dB" and "0.06 dB" do not state whether these are maximum or RMS differences over 0–40 Hz. Panel (c) axis says "Max. |difference|", so state "maximum over the displayed band."
  - [ADVISORY] "which is the rounding error of the arithmetic" should name the precision (e.g., IEEE 754 double, machine epsilon ≈ 2.2×10⁻¹⁶) to justify the claim.
  - [ADVISORY] Open-circle markers in (c) are not mentioned; state that markers are the computed values and the line is a visual connector (not a fit).
  - [ADVISORY] "de-identified scalp EEG … from the authors' archive" should carry an IRB/ethics-approval or exemption pointer, and the electrode/reference montage for the single channel is unstated.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (b) and (c) occupy the lower half with unequal widths; the large white gap left of (c) could be recovered, and confirm all fonts are ≥6 pt (8–10 pt preferred) at 181 mm final width — the (c) axis labels are the smallest text and should be checked.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Restructure as bold title + setup + panels, e.g.: "**Three routes to one PSD estimate coincide.** Power spectral density (PSD) of a single 8-s epoch (one channel, de-identified scalp electroencephalogram (EEG), 128 Hz sampling, sampling interval Δ = 7.81 ms, N = 1024 samples; IRB exemption #XXXX), design resolution R = 8/(NΔ) = 1 Hz, time–bandwidth product α = NΔR/2 = 4, in dB re 1 µV²/Hz."
  - Add to (a): "…dotted dark green: average of the periodograms of a sinc window of bandwidth R and length N_s = 16N slid one sample at a time across the (zero-padded / circularly extended — specify) record, M = <state> window positions."
  - Add to (b): "Maximum absolute differences from the multitaper estimate over 0–40 Hz: 3×10⁻¹³ dB for the smoothed periodogram (at the IEEE 754 double-precision rounding floor, machine epsilon ≈ 2.2×10⁻¹⁶) and 0.06 dB for the sliding-window average. Single realization; no averaging across epochs."
  - Add to (c): "Maximum absolute difference of the sliding-window average versus the window-length ratio N_s/N (5 values: 1, 2, 4, 8, 16); circles are computed values, the solid line connects them, and the dotted line is a 1/N_s reference slope (not a fit)."
  - Replace the section cross-reference with an in-caption definition: "All three estimates share ν = 17 equivalent degrees of freedom, ν = 2(Σλ_i)²/Σλ_i² for the multitaper form, so the comparison in (b) is between estimators of identical variance."
  - Re-export the figure as vector EPS/PDF (or ≥600 DPI TIFF) at exactly 181 mm width, RGB, <10 MB, and verify no in-figure titles and all type ≥6 pt.

### figure2.png

---
TITLE:              Not bold — a title-like opening sentence exists ("Every estimator is the true spectrum blurred by a kernel, plus noise.") but it is not set in bold, and it is an interpretive claim rather than a descriptive title
PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete — (a), (b), (c), (d) and (e–h) are all described in order
ERROR BARS:         Not present — single realization; no error bars or shaded intervals in the figure
SAMPLE SIZE:        Stated — one realization, N = 1024 samples; degrees of freedom ν = 2.0, 2.0, 16.7, 98.4 given for the four estimators
STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — no hypothesis test is performed (none required), so test statistic/p-values are N/A; however the journal's requirement to state "the number of realizations behind any distribution" is met only implicitly ("One realization"), and it is not stated whether panels (a–d) come from the *same* realization or independent ones. The definition/derivation basis of ν (equivalent degrees of freedom, Welch-type?) is not identified
ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: PSD (power spectral density, never spelled out); dB (acceptable as standard); AR is spelled out but the model **order and coefficients are not given**; symbols Δ (sampling interval), N (record length), f (frequency), R (half-power smoothing width), ν (equivalent degrees of freedom), 𝒦(f) (expected-value kernel) are used in axes/annotations without explicit definition in the caption (𝒦 and R are partially defined)
COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Present — black = true PSD, grey = untapered periodogram, orange = tapered/smoothed estimates; all stated in the caption and partially annotated in-figure
SELF-CONTAINED:     No — the AR process is not specified (order, coefficients, or a pointer to the equation/table defining it), Δ and N are not defined as symbols, "25% cosine taper" convention (total vs. per-end fraction) is ambiguous, and the parabolic smoothing window (Daniell-like? Bartlett–Priestley?) is not named

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] No bold figure title. IEEE TBME captions have no in-figure titles, so the legend must open with a short bold descriptive title; the current opening line is an unbolded thesis statement.
  - [BLOCKING] Abbreviation "PSD" (used in the y-axis label of (a–d) and throughout the caption) is never expanded. Expand on first use in the caption.
  - [BLOCKING] The generating process is not identified: "an autoregressive process" with no order or coefficients. A reader cannot reproduce or verify the true PSD (black curve) from the figure and caption alone.
  - [BLOCKING] Symbols appearing on the axes and in the panel annotations — Δ, N, f, ν, R, 𝒦(f) — are not all defined in the legend. At minimum define Δ (sampling interval), N (number of samples), ν (equivalent degrees of freedom of the estimator), R (half-power bandwidth of the smoothing parabola), and 𝒦(f) (expected-value/spectral window kernel).
  - [ADVISORY] It is not stated whether (a)–(d) are computed from one and the same realization (the figure strongly suggests they are). State this explicitly, since the visual comparison depends on it.
  - [ADVISORY] The convention for the "25% cosine taper" is ambiguous (25% of the record total, or 25% at each end). Specify.
  - [ADVISORY] The smoothing kernel is called "a parabola" — give its standard name/formula (e.g., Bartlett–Priestley/Epanechnikov window) so it is identifiable.
  - [ADVISORY] Roughly the last third of the caption (leakage percentages, skirt attenuation at 16/(NΔ) and 40/(NΔ), main-lobe widening, "is just right", "merges the peaks") is results/interpretation. TBME prefers descriptive captions; the numerical leakage table would be better as a small table or in the text.
  - [ADVISORY] The numbers 0.8, 1.2, 8.2, 47.8/(NΔ) and 1.3%, 0.02%, 0.12%, 65% are given as bare lists; make the panel mapping explicit ("(e–h), respectively") to avoid mis-pairing. Note also that the leakage sequence is non-monotonic (1.3%, 0.02%, 0.12%, 65%), which looks like a possible transcription error — verify.
  - [ADVISORY] "Where the side lobes are too close to draw, the curve is the envelope of their peaks" — good, but state in which panels the envelope (rather than the true kernel) is plotted, since this changes the apparent sidelobe structure in (e) vs. (f).
  - [ADVISORY] Resolution: 2077 px across a 181 mm double-column width ≈ 291 DPI, marginally below the 300 DPI minimum and well below the 600 DPI preferred for line art. Re-export at ≥600 DPI or supply vector EPS/PDF for final submission.
  - [ADVISORY] Inset axes tick labels appear near the lower end of the acceptable type-size range; verify ≥6 pt (ideally 8 pt) at 181 mm final width.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel labels are correctly lowercase (a)–(h); ensure the in-figure annotation text ("Periodogram, untapered", "True PSD", etc.) is not read as an in-panel title by the copy editor — these are acceptable as data labels but should be duplicated in the legend (they are).

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Recast the opening as a bold descriptive title, e.g.: "**Fig. 2. Bias–variance trade-off of tapering and spectral smoothing, and the corresponding expected-value kernels.**"
  - Add a specification sentence: "Data are a single realization (N = 1024 samples, sampling interval Δ) of a [order-p] autoregressive process with coefficients given in [Eq. (x)/Table y], whose true power spectral density (PSD, black) has two peaks of half-power width 7/(NΔ) separated by 30/(NΔ) on a background spanning 65 dB."
  - Add a symbol/definition sentence: "f is frequency, Δ the sampling interval, N the record length, ν the equivalent degrees of freedom of the estimator, R the half-power bandwidth of the parabolic (Bartlett–Priestley) smoothing window, and 𝒦(f) the expected-value kernel (spectral window) of the estimator."
  - State explicitly: "Panels (a)–(d) are all computed from the same realization; no averaging over realizations was performed, hence no error bars are shown. Variability is instead summarized by ν (a) 2.0, (b) 2.0, (c) 16.7, (d) 98.4."
  - Disambiguate the taper: "a split-cosine-bell taper applied to 12.5% of the record at each end (25% total)."
  - Tag the numerical lists to panels: "Half-power widths are (e) 0.8, (f) 1.2, (g) 8.2, and (h) 47.8, in units of 1/(NΔ); the fraction of kernel power outside |f| ≤ 8/(NΔ) (broadband leakage) is (e) 1.3%, (f) 0.02%, (g) 0.12%, (h) 65%." Re-check the (h) value against (g).
  - Note which panels show sidelobe envelopes: "In (e) and (f) the sidelobes are unresolvable at this scale, so the envelope of their peaks is drawn; insets show the unsmoothed main lobe over 0.02/Δ in (e)–(g) and 0.08/Δ in (h), centered at fΔ = 0.4."
  - Move the final three interpretive sentences (Fejér skirts, 22/39 dB attenuation, "just right", "merges the peaks") to the Results text, or compress to one sentence, e.g.: "Tapering suppresses broadband leakage; smoothing with R comparable to the peak width resolves both peaks (c, g), whereas R exceeding their separation merges them (d, h)."
  - Re-export at ≥600 DPI or as EPS/PDF (RGB, <10 MB), confirm all type is sans-serif and ≥6 pt at 181 mm, and remove none of the existing lowercase panel labels.

### figure3.png

---
TITLE:              Not bold — "Choosing the resolution." is present and concise as the first sentence, but it is not typeset bold in the LaTeX source (no `\textbf{}`).

PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete — (a) and (b) are both described, in order, with lowercase labels matching the in-figure labels.

ERROR BARS:         Not present — explicitly and correctly stated ("the curves are ensemble averages and carry no error bars"). This satisfies the definition requirement.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Stated — N = 1024 samples in (a); 300 independent realizations in (b). Advisory: state the number of realizations behind *each* curve (whole-band vs. peak-region) if they come from the same ensemble, and confirm the peak-region curve uses the same 300 realizations.

STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — no inferential test is performed (none is needed), and this should be said explicitly. Absent items: (i) the definition/estimator of the plotted RMS error (is RMS taken over frequency within each realization and then averaged over realizations, or is it computed from the ensemble-mean estimate?); (ii) whether the "Noise" and "Bias" gray curves are empirical (from the 300 realizations) or analytic/theoretical predictions; (iii) the relation used to convert R to the equivalent degrees of freedom ν on the upper axis (e.g. ν ≈ 2RNΔ·c for the parabolic kernel with the 25% cosine taper), which is required by the journal's rule on stating the estimator's degrees of freedom when noise levels are compared; (iv) no test statistic or p-values — correctly not applicable, but say so.

ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: PSD (power spectral density), AR(4) (fourth-order autoregressive — acceptable only if defined earlier in the text), dB (acceptable), and the symbols Δ (sampling interval), f (frequency), k (sample index), x_k (record value), R (half-power width of the smoothing parabola — described but not formally defined as a frequency width). RMS is spelled out — good.

COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Present (partially) — line styles/colors for (a) are fully described. In (b) the caption identifies "solid black" (whole band) and "dashed" (peak region) but does not map the marker shapes (filled circles vs. open squares) or the gray unlabeled curves; the gray Bias/Noise curves are labeled in-figure, which is acceptable, but the caption should mirror them. The large open circles used as annotation rings should also be identified as markers, not data.

SELF-CONTAINED:     No — the panel (a) record and the three circled operating points are defined only by cross-reference to another figure ("the record of Fig. motif", "panels (b), (c) and (d) of Fig. motif"). A reader of this figure alone cannot recover the process parameters, the sampling interval Δ, the frequency band used for the whole-band score, or what the circled points depict.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Figure title is not bold. Set the leading sentence in `\textbf{}` (or the journal's equivalent) as required.
  - [BLOCKING] The estimator's degrees of freedom are compared across the figure (upper ν axis, ν = 2.0 / 16.7 / 98.4 annotations) but the ν(R) relation and the taper/kernel assumptions behind it are never stated. IEEE TBME requires the degrees of freedom to be given where noise levels are compared.
  - [BLOCKING] The definition of the plotted RMS error statistic is ambiguous (order of RMS-over-frequency vs. average-over-realizations) — the quantity cannot be reproduced from the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] It is not stated whether the "Bias" and "Noise" gray curves are empirical estimates from the same 300 realizations or analytic predictions; if empirical, the caption must say so and state n again is 300.
  - [ADVISORY] Marker shapes (filled circle, open square) are not mapped to conditions in the caption; readers printing in grayscale rely on marker shape, not on "solid/dashed" alone.
  - [ADVISORY] The whole-band frequency range is not given, although the peak region (0.09 ≤ fΔ ≤ 0.16) is — the two scores cannot be compared without it.
  - [ADVISORY] PSD and AR(4) are used without expansion in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Δ is used in three axis labels/expressions (RNΔ, fΔ, 48/(NΔ)) but never defined as the sampling interval.
  - [ADVISORY] Cross-reference collision: this figure's own panel is "(b)", and the caption also refers to "panels (b), (c) and (d) of Fig. motif" — high risk of reader confusion. Rephrase.
  - [ADVISORY] Image is 990 px wide; at single-column 88 mm this is ≈283 DPI, below the 300 DPI minimum and well below the 600 DPI preferred for line art. Re-export as vector (EPS/PDF) or at ≥600 DPI.
  - [ADVISORY] In-figure annotation text ("too noisy", "just right", "too smooth", "peak width", "peak separation") is interpretive; verify it is ≥6 pt at final size and consider echoing the three labels in the caption so the annotation is traceable.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Bold the title and expand abbreviations on first use, e.g.: "**Choosing the resolution.** (a) The N = 1024-sample record of Fig. X from the fourth-order autoregressive [AR(4)] process (black), ... (b) Root-mean-square (RMS) error, in dB, of the cosine-tapered periodogram smoothed by a parabolic kernel of half-power width R, relative to the true power spectral density (PSD) ..."
  - Define the statistic precisely: "For each of n = 300 independent realizations the dB error was computed at every Fourier frequency; the RMS was taken over the scoring band and then averaged across the 300 realizations. Curves are ensemble averages; no error bars are shown because the realization-to-realization spread is <0.1 dB (max)."
  - State the dof mapping: "The upper axis shows the equivalent degrees of freedom ν of the smoothed estimate, ν = ... for the parabolic kernel applied to a 25% cosine-tapered periodogram; ν = 2.0 at R = 0 and ν = 131 at RNΔ = 64."
  - State the nature of the decomposition curves: "Gray curves show the noise (standard deviation of the estimate) and bias (kernel blur) components, computed analytically [or: estimated from the same 300 realizations]."

  - Define Δ once: "Δ is the sampling interval."
  - Add an explicit no-test statement: "No hypothesis test was performed; the figure reports a deterministic bias–variance trade-off, so no test statistic or p-values are given."
  - Resolve the cross-reference clash by naming the other figure's panels explicitly (e.g., "Fig. 1(b)–(d)") and re-export the artwork as vector or ≥600 DPI TIFF sized to 88 mm.

### figure7.png

---
TITLE:              Not bold — "The estimators used in practice agree to within their own noise." is a serviceable declarative title but is not set in bold as required.

PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete — (a) and (b) described in order, lowercase labels match the figure.

ERROR BARS:         Not present — no error bars; the grey ±1.6 dB uncertainty band is defined once (caption and in-figure annotation) and traced to ν, which satisfies the requirement.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Partial — the number of frequency bins (1281), number of tapers (L = 7), number of Welch segments (9), segment length (1.5 s) and step (0.81 s), and ν for all three estimators are given. Missing: the epoch itself (n = 1 epoch — state this explicitly), its duration and sampling rate, the subject/dataset and the electrode/derivation. Also missing: whether the 1281 bins are treated as independent (they are not — spacing ≪ half-power width), so the quoted medians/percentiles come from a correlated sample.

STATISTICAL DETAIL: Complete for the comparison made — no hypothesis test is performed, and none is needed; descriptive statistics (median absolute difference, 95th percentile) and the theoretical dB standard deviation with its ν are reported. Absent only: the provenance of the constant 4.3 in 4.3√(2/ν) (i.e. 10/ln 10), and a statement that no inferential test was applied.

ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: PSD (axis label, never expanded); EEG (expand at least once, even if defined earlier); "dB re 1 µV²/Hz" reference convention is fine; "Slepian" and "Hann" acceptable as named windows. Symbols undefined in caption: R (half-power width of the parabola), ν (degrees of freedom — state it means the chi-square equivalent degrees of freedom), L.

COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Present — in-figure legends in both panels plus redundant colour naming in the caption (blue/orange/pink); consistent between panels.

SELF-CONTAINED:     No — "recipe (b′)" is an unresolvable cross-reference to the main text; the data are identified only by "the EEG epoch of Fig. X"; R and ν are unexplained; and the caption's labels "(smoothed)" and "(averaged)" do not map onto the legend entries.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Title sentence is not bold. Wrap the first sentence in \textbf{}.
  - [BLOCKING] "recipe (b′)" cannot be understood from the figure and caption alone. Replace with a one-clause description of the smoother (e.g. "parabolic smoother of half-power width R applied to a cosine-tapered periodogram").
  - [BLOCKING] The figure's underlying data are not described: no epoch duration, sampling rate, channel, subject/dataset, or n = 1 epoch statement. A reader cannot reconstruct what is plotted without the other figure's caption.
  - [BLOCKING] Statistics in the caption use labels ("smoothed", "averaged") that do not correspond to the legend strings ("Cosine taper, then parabola", "Welch, 9 Hann segments"). Ambiguous attribution of the 0.6/0.9 dB and 4.2/5.9 dB values.
  - [ADVISORY] Symbols R, ν and L are used without definition in the caption; ν should be identified as the chi-square equivalent degrees of freedom of the estimator.
  - [ADVISORY] PSD and EEG are not expanded in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] The 1281 frequency bins are strongly correlated (bin spacing ≈ 0.031 Hz vs. half-power widths ≈ 1 Hz), so the median and 95th percentile are computed over ~40 independent resolution cells, not 1281. State this to avoid overstating the sample.
  - [ADVISORY] The constant 4.3 in 4.3√(2/ν) is unexplained; give it as 10/ln 10 ≈ 4.34 dB.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption states the grey band is ±1.6 dB (multitaper) while also quoting 1.5 dB for the other two; make clear the band is drawn for the multitaper reference only, consistent with the in-figure annotation.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) has tick marks but no x-axis tick labels; acceptable for a shared axis, but state in the caption that both panels share the frequency axis of (b).
  - [ADVISORY] The dotted zero line in (b) and the grey band are the only in-figure annotations not colour-keyed; confirm the dashed black line is described as the zero-difference reference.
  - [ADVISORY] Figure aspect (986 × 1226 px) is single-column; at 88 mm width this is ~285 DPI. Supply at ≥600 DPI line art (or vector EPS/PDF) for final submission; also check that the smallest in-figure text (the grey-band annotation) is ≥6 pt at 88 mm.
  - [ADVISORY] Fonts appear serif (Times-like); permitted, but confirm consistency with the rest of the figure set.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Revised caption: "\textbf{Estimators used in practice agree to within their own noise.} (a) Power spectral density (PSD) of a single X-s electroencephalogram (EEG) epoch (n = 1 epoch; channel XX, sampling rate XXX Hz; the same epoch as Fig. Y), in dB relative to 1 µV²/Hz, estimated three ways: Thomson's multitaper method with L = 7 Slepian tapers (chi-square equivalent degrees of freedom ν = 14; blue); a cosine-tapered periodogram smoothed by a parabolic kernel of half-power width R = X Hz (ν = 16.7; orange); and Welch's method with nine Hann segments of 1.5 s stepped by 0.81 s (ν = 17.6; pink). Half-power resolution widths are 0.91, 1.03 and 0.97 Hz, respectively. (b) Difference of each estimate from the multitaper estimate over the 1281 frequency bins plotted (0–40 Hz); the dashed line marks zero difference. Median absolute difference 0.6 dB for the parabola-smoothed estimate and 0.9 dB for Welch's estimate; 95th percentiles 4.2 and 5.9 dB. Both medians fall below the estimators' own dB standard deviation, (10/ln 10)√(2/ν) = 1.6, 1.5 and 1.5 dB for ν = 14, 16.7 and 17.6; the grey band shows ±1.6 dB, the standard deviation of the multitaper reference. No hypothesis test was applied; values are descriptive. Because the bin spacing (0.031 Hz) is far smaller than the ~1 Hz half-power widths, the 1281 bins correspond to roughly 40 independent resolution cells. The largest discrepancies occur in the valleys between harmonics, which are filled in by the side lobes of the seventh Slepian taper."
  - Bold the title sentence and delete any in-figure title text (none is present — good).
  - Align the caption's estimator names verbatim with the in-figure legend strings.
  - Add the epoch metadata (duration, sampling rate, channel, dataset/subject ID) so the panel is interpretable without the earlier figure.
  - Define PSD, EEG, ν, L and R in the caption.
  - Re-export as vector EPS/PDF or ≥600 DPI TIFF at 88 mm width, RGB, and verify all text ≥6 pt.

### figure8.png

---
TITLE:              Present but **not bold** — "Three estimators of a seizure, in one recording." is a serviceable title sentence, but the LaTeX source contains no `\textbf{}`/`\textit{}` emphasis.

PANEL DESCRIPTIONS: Complete — (a), (b), (c,e,g) as a group, (d,f) as a group, (h) all described, in order. Advisory: because (c,e,g) and (d,f) are collapsed into grouped sentences, the reader must map each estimator to the correct panel using the in-figure headers; consider one clause per panel letter.

ERROR BARS:         Not present — no error bars, CIs, or shaded uncertainty bands appear in any panel. The caption correctly substitutes the equivalent degrees of freedom (ν) for each estimator, which satisfies the journal's noise-level requirement.

SAMPLE SIZE:        Stated — 599 windows (2 s, 1-s step), 241 frequencies, 144,359 pixels in (h), one recording, one channel (C4 vs. common average). Missing: how many subjects/seizures exist in the source set and why this one recording/channel is representative (only the selection rule is given).

STATISTICAL DETAIL: Partial — no hypothesis test is performed or claimed, which is acceptable for a methods-comparison figure. Absent: (i) the spread of the (h) distribution beyond the median |difference| (e.g., IQR or 5th–95th percentile); (ii) the bin width of the histogram in (h); (iii) the sign convention stated in words ("(e) minus (g)" appears only in the in-panel header); (iv) acknowledgement that the 144,359 pixels are not independent (50 % window overlap and 2 Hz smoothing correlate neighbours), so the effective number of realizations is far smaller than the pixel count.

ABBREVIATIONS:      Undefined: PSD (appears on the (d), (f) and colour-bar axes, never expanded in the caption); EEG (not expanded at first use); ν (never stated to mean *equivalent degrees of freedom*); L (number of tapers); α (time–bandwidth product NW); "ELROND seizure-annotation set" (no citation or accession/version given); "dB re 1 µV²/Hz" reference convention is given in the caption but not tied to the axis label wording.

COLOR/SYMBOL KEY:   Partial — the in-figure legend in (f) maps the four line colours, and the colour bar is labelled with the units and range. Missing from the caption: the statement that the (f) legend also applies to (d) (which has no legend of its own); the meaning of the grey shading in (a); the meaning of the white dashed lines is given, and the black triangles are explained — good. The vertical black line at 0 dB in (h) is unexplained.

SELF-CONTAINED:     No — a reader can follow the comparison, but is left without: expansions of PSD/EEG/ν/L/α, a citation for the ELROND set, confirmation that (d) uses the (f) colour key, the histogram bin width, and notice that panel (a) traces are clipped at ±500 µV (the ictal excursion is visibly truncated, so the displayed peak amplitude is not the true amplitude).

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Title sentence is not bold; the journal/house style requires a bold lead-in title in the legend.
  - [BLOCKING] Undefined abbreviations and symbols carried in the axis labels and panel headers: PSD, EEG, ν, L, α. ν in particular is the quantity on which the whole figure's argument rests and must be defined as "equivalent degrees of freedom of the estimator."
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (d) has no in-figure legend and the caption does not state that the (f) legend applies to it; four unlabelled curves in (d) are therefore unidentifiable from the figure plus legend alone.
  - [BLOCKING] The distribution in (h) is reported with an n (144,359 pixels) that is not a count of independent realizations (50 % window overlap, 2 Hz half-power smoothing). Either state the effective number of independent windows/frequencies or explicitly say the pixel count is descriptive only.
  - [ADVISORY] "ELROND seizure-annotation set" has no citation, version, or availability statement; a reader cannot locate the record, and the name is not a recognisable public EEG corpus.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) is displayed "between ±500 µV" and the ictal segment is clearly clipped at the axis limits; the caption should say the trace is clipped for display, not merely "displayed between."
  - [ADVISORY] Histogram bin width and the meaning of the vertical line at 0 dB in (h) are unstated; the spread of the difference distribution (IQR or 5–95 %) is not reported alongside the median.
  - [ADVISORY] Each spectrogram and each spectrum panel carries an in-figure header line ("Periodogram: 2 s by 0.5 Hz, ν = 2.0", "Before the seizure, t = 247 s", "Panel (e) minus panel (g)"). IEEE style discourages titles inside the figure; these should ideally be moved into the caption or reduced to short annotations.
  - [ADVISORY] The "2 s by 0.5 Hz" / "2 s by 1.7 Hz" / "2 s by 2.0 Hz" notation in the panel headers is never explained; a reader cannot know these are time × half-power frequency resolutions.
  - [ADVISORY] The three spectrograms (c, e, g) have no x-axis label except on (g), and (c) and (e) omit the "Time (s)" label; acceptable for a stacked column but worth stating that the time axis is shared.
  - [ADVISORY] Resolution: 1835 px across a 181 mm double-column width is ≈257 DPI, below the ≥300 DPI minimum for colour line art (≥600 DPI preferred). Re-export at ≥2140 px (300 DPI) or supply vector EPS/PDF with the spectrograms as embedded 300+ DPI rasters.
  - [ADVISORY] Statement of subject/recording provenance is thin: n = 1 recording, 1 channel; add whether this is a representative example and, if so, how many records were screened.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Bold the title and sharpen it, e.g. **"Periodogram, multitaper, and smoothed-periodogram spectrograms of one focal seizure."**
  - Define symbols on first use: "ν, equivalent degrees of freedom; L, number of Slepian tapers; α = NW, time–bandwidth product; PSD, power spectral density; EEG, electroencephalography."
  - Add to (d): "Curves are coloured as in the legend of (f)." Add the colour map explicitly for robustness: "grey, periodogram; blue, multitaper (L = 3); orange, cosine taper then 2 Hz parabola; pink, Welch with 0.7-s Hann segments."
  - Rewrite (a) clause: "…channel C4 referenced to the common average; the trace is clipped for display at ±500 µV. Grey shading marks the electrographic seizure (approximately 303–366 s). Black triangles, and the white dashed lines in (c), (e), (g), mark the two instants analysed in (d) and (f)."
  - Rewrite (h) clause: "Histogram of the pixel-wise difference, (e) minus (g), across all 599 windows × 241 frequencies = 144,359 pixels (bin width 0.25 dB; vertical line at 0 dB). Median |difference| 0.9 dB, interquartile range X to Y dB. Pixels are not independent: windows overlap by 50 % and the parabolic smoother spans 2 Hz, so the pixel count is descriptive rather than an effective sample size."
  - Give the data provenance a citation and identifier: "…de-identified scalp EEG from the ELROND seizure-annotation set [ref], record ID xxx, sampled at 200 Hz."
  - Explain the resolution notation once: "Panel headers give the time × half-power frequency resolution and the equivalent degrees of freedom of each estimator."
  - State the channel-selection rule as a method, not an afterthought, and move it earlier: "The displayed channel is that with the largest ictal increase in 2–20 Hz power among all channels."
  - Move the in-panel header text into the caption where possible, and re-export the figure at ≥300 DPI (preferably vector PDF/EPS) at 181 mm width, confirming all text remains ≥6 pt at final size.
