Remedies: modest alpha (0.7–0.8) on the upper traces, slightly differing line weights (thickest for the trace most often hidden), and/or an explicit note of draw order in the caption. Colour accessibility: blue/orange/pink is mostly safe, but orange vs. pink can converge under deuteranopia and in greyscale — differentiate with dash patterns as a redundant channel.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** Flag for verification — No pairwise significance annotations are present, so no correction is formally required; however the figure invites an implicit 1281-fold comparison against the grey band. Two items for the authors: (i) The 1281 frequency bins are **not independent** — the smoothing/tapering bandwidth (≈0.9–1.0 Hz half-power width) spans many bins, so excursions outside the band are strongly serially correlated and the effective number of independent comparisons is roughly (40 Hz)/(≈1 Hz) ≈ 40, not 1281. Any statement about the *fraction* of frequencies outside the band (including the 95th percentiles of 4.2 and 5.9 dB) should be interpreted with that in mind, and the caption should say so. (ii) The grey band is ±1 SD (≈68% coverage for a Gaussian), not a 95% interval; readers habitually read shaded bands as confidence intervals, so many in-family excursions will look anomalous. Label the band explicitly as "±1 SD (≈68%)".

**TIME SERIES:** Not applicable — Frequency-domain data on an apparently uniform grid; no gaps or uneven intervals, and no misleading interpolation. (If the frequency grid differs between methods, see the COMPARISON note.)

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The reference band in (b) is the standard deviation of the *multitaper estimate alone* (±1.6 dB), but the plotted quantity is a *difference of two estimates*. The SD of a difference is larger than that of either component (for independent estimates, ≈√(1.6² + 1.5²) ≈ 2.2 dB; the true value depends on the — substantial — correlation between two estimators computed from the same data). As drawn, the band understates the null dispersion of the quantity plotted, which makes the "medians are smaller than the estimators' own noise" claim appear stronger than the figure justifies. Either widen the band to the SD of the difference, or relabel it unambiguously as a single-estimator noise scale and adjust the caption's inferential language.
- [BLOCKING] n = 1 epoch is not stated anywhere in the figure, while 1281 frequency bins are foregrounded in the caption. The headline claim ("the estimators used in practice agree to within their own noise") is a general statement supported here by one record; the figure must make the single-record basis visible.
- [ADVISORY] Fixed z-order in (a) means agreement and occlusion are visually indistinguishable.
- [ADVISORY] The summary statistics carrying the argument (median absolute difference 0.6 / 0.9 dB; 95th percentiles 4.2 / 5.9 dB) exist only in prose and cannot be read off the figure.
- [ADVISORY] Non-independence of adjacent frequency bins (smoothing bandwidth ≈ 1 Hz) is not indicated graphically, so excursion runs may be read as many independent failures.
- [ADVISORY] Abscissa truncated at 40 Hz without a stated Nyquist frequency; behaviour above 40 Hz is unassessable.
- [ADVISORY] Orange/pink pairing is weak under deuteranopia and in greyscale.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace or supplement the ±1.6 dB band with the expected SD of the *difference*, stating the assumed correlation between estimators; label coverage explicitly ("±1 SD, ≈68%").
- Add "single 12.15 s EEG epoch, one subject; 1281 frequency bins" as a panel annotation in (a), and report the sampling rate and Nyquist frequency.
- Add a horizontal bar (or a marginal histogram/ECDF strip on the right of panel b) showing the median absolute difference and 95th percentile for each estimator, so the quantitative claims are legible from the figure.
- Draw a short horizontal bar in (a) marking the half-power width (0.91/1.03/0.97 Hz) to make the resolution–variance trade-off visible and to signal the correlation length of adjacent bins.
- Apply alpha ≈ 0.75 and graded line weights to the overlaid traces; add redundant dash patterns to distinguish orange from pink; note draw order in the caption.
- Add an inset zoom over one harmonic peak and its adjacent valley to substantiate the claim that the largest differences arise in the valleys from seventh-Slepian side-lobe leakage.
- Confirm in the caption that all three estimates share an identical frequency grid (or describe any interpolation performed before differencing).
- Soften the title claim to reflect the single-record evidence base, e.g. "…agree to within their own noise for this epoch," or add a supplementary figure aggregating across epochs/subjects.

### figure8.png

---

**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a signal-processing/methods demonstration, not a group-comparison figure, so the bar-graph and pie-chart criteria do not apply. Time series (a, b), image-based spectrograms (c, e, g), overlaid line spectra (d, f), and a histogram (h) are all the correct choices for these data. Two refinements: (i) the spectrograms use a rainbow/jet-type colour map, which is perceptually non-uniform and creates spurious banding (the cyan–yellow transition reads as an edge where the underlying PSD changes smoothly) and is not deuteranopia-safe — a monotone-luminance map (viridis, cividis, turbo-with-caution) would preserve the visual comparison across (c), (e), (g) without inventing contours; (ii) panel (h) reports only the median |difference| — consider overlaying the signed median/quartiles so the visible near-symmetry of the distribution is quantified rather than inferred by eye.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible — The figure itself does not state that this is a single recording, a single channel, and a single subject; all of that lives in the caption. The derived n's (599 windows, 241 frequencies, 144,359 pixels) are likewise caption-only. Panel titles do carry the resolution parameters (2 s by 0.5/1.7/2.0 Hz, ν = 2.0/6.0/8.7), which is good practice and should be extended: annotate n = 599 windows on (c, e, g) and n = 144,359 pixels on (h). Because n(recordings) = 1, no distributional or "individual data point" requirement applies in the usual sense — but the single-record basis must be unmissable so readers do not read (h) as a population result.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — Two issues. (1) In (d) and (f) the four estimators are computed from the *same* data segment, i.e. this is an intrinsically paired comparison, and overlaying them on shared axes is exactly the right paired display — good. However, the four estimators have materially different degrees of freedom (ν = 2, 6, 8.7, 7.7) and different resolution bandwidths, so their curves are not comparable on equal footing without an indication of estimator variance. A χ²-based confidence ribbon (or a single ν-scaled error bar per curve in a corner) would let the reader judge whether the multitaper/parabola/Welch divergences exceed sampling noise. (2) Panels (d) and (f) use different y-ranges (−20 to 20 dB vs 0 to 75 dB) and different tick spacing, which defeats visual comparison of pre-ictal vs ictal spectra — the ictal peak's true magnitude relative to baseline cannot be read across panels. Either share one axis or add an explicit annotation of the offset.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present — Not the classic misleading-bar-chart case, but three truncations should be disclosed on the face of the figure. (1) Panel (a) is "displayed between ±500 µV" and the ictal burst is visibly clipped flat against both limits; panel (b) shows a transient cut off below −500 µV. The reader cannot tell how far the true amplitude exceeds the frame, which understates ictal amplitude while the shaded band simultaneously draws attention to it. Add "clipped" markers or a broken-axis indicator. (2) The shared colour bar is truncated at −13 to 22 dB, and the post-ictal suppression (≈ t = 370–400 s) and parts of the ictal band appear saturated at the dark-blue and dark-red ends respectively; saturated pixels are then silently included in the (h) difference distribution. State the fraction of pixels out of range, or use over/under colours. (3) Panel (h) y-axis begins at 0, which is correct; no issue there.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — Two forms. (1) Panel (c) is dominated by ν = 2 speckle at essentially one PSD sample per pixel; when 599 × 241 values are rasterised into the printed panel, any resampling/interpolation will alias this speckle into false texture. Confirm nearest-neighbour or exact 1:1 pixel rendering and state the interpolation setting. (2) In (f) the grey periodogram, the blue multitaper and the orange cosine-taper/parabola curves overlap heavily between 10 and 30 Hz, and the orange and pink traces are nearly indistinguishable in places; the visual claim that the three smoothed estimators agree is therefore partly an artefact of occlusion. Use partial transparency, differing line weights/dashes, or add a small residual sub-panel (smoothed minus multitaper) so agreement is shown rather than asserted by superposition.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** Flag for verification — No pairwise significance annotations appear anywhere, so there is no correction problem in the conventional sense. However, panel (h) aggregates 144,359 pixels that are strongly dependent: windows are 2 s stepped by 1 s (50 % overlap), the parabola smoother couples neighbouring frequency bins, and the multitaper estimate couples bins within its 1.7 Hz bandwidth. The effective number of independent comparisons is far smaller than 144,359. Because only a median absolute difference is reported and no p-value or confidence interval is attached, this is not fatal — but if any inferential claim is made from (h) in the text, the variance must be computed with an effective-n or block-bootstrap that respects the overlap. Please verify and state explicitly that 144,359 is a pixel count, not an independent-sample count.

**TIME SERIES:** Concern — (1) The three spectrograms appear to begin near t ≈ 1 s and end near t ≈ 599 s because of the window centring, and the first/last half-windows are absent; this edge behaviour is invisible and should be stated. (2) Panels (c) and (e) carry no x-axis label, relying on (g); acceptable if the axes are exactly aligned, but the tick ranges should be verified as identical to (a) so the shaded seizure in (a) maps correctly onto the dashed lines below. (3) Panel (a) shows an abrupt low-amplitude segment around t ≈ 365–400 s that reads in the spectrograms as near-total suppression with a narrow vertical stripe at ≈ 405 s. If any of this is a dropout, disconnection, or artefact-rejected interval rather than genuine post-ictal suppression, interpolating the spectrogram across it is misleading and the gap must be masked (white/hatched) rather than coloured. If it is genuine physiology, say so. (4) Panel (b) is plotted on a 0–6 s axis while the caption places it at t = 318 s; relabel as t − 318 s or show absolute time to prevent a reader from mis-registering it against (a).

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (a)/(b) amplitude clipping at ±500 µV is not marked on the figure; the ictal burst and at least one transient are flattened against the frame, understating ictal amplitude in the panel that establishes the phenomenon. Add explicit clipping indicators or a broken axis.
- [BLOCKING] The apparently suppressed interval at t ≈ 365–400 s is not identified as physiology vs. missing/rejected data. If it is a gap, the spectrograms interpolate across it and must instead mask it; this is the misleading-interpolation case.
- [BLOCKING] n = 1 recording, 1 channel, 1 subject is inferable only from the caption, while panel (h) presents a smooth distribution over 144,359 pixels that invites reading as a population-level result. The single-record basis must appear in the figure.
- [ADVISORY] Colour-bar truncation at −13 to 22 dB with visible saturation at both ends; saturated pixels feed into (h) without disclosure.
- [ADVISORY] Rainbow/jet colour map is perceptually non-uniform and not colourblind-safe; it manufactures apparent contours in smoothly varying PSD fields.
- [ADVISORY] Panels (d) and (f) use different y-ranges, preventing cross-panel comparison of pre-ictal and ictal spectra.
- [ADVISORY] No indication of estimator variance (χ² confidence ribbons or ν-scaled error bars) in (d)/(f), despite ν differing by more than fourfold across the four curves.
- [ADVISORY] 144,359 pixels are treated as a single population in (h) despite 50 % window overlap and frequency-domain smoothing; effective n is much smaller.
- [ADVISORY] Overlapping near-identical traces in (f) (orange vs pink) and dense occlusion above 10 Hz.
- [ADVISORY] Half-window edge truncation of the spectrogram time axis is undocumented; (c) and (e) lack x-axis labels.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) time axis is relative but captioned as absolute t = 318 s.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Mark every clipped sample in (a) and (b) with an out-of-range glyph (e.g. filled triangles at the frame) and report the true ictal peak amplitude in the caption, or add a broken-axis inset showing the unclipped burst.
- Determine whether t ≈ 365–400 s is genuine post-ictal suppression; if any portion is missing or artefact-rejected, render it as a white or hatched mask in (c), (e), (g), exclude those pixels from (h), and recompute the median absolute difference on the retained pixels.
- Print "single recording, single channel (C4 − common average), n = 1 subject" directly in the figure, and annotate "599 windows × 241 frequencies = 144,359 pixels (overlapping; not independent)" on (h).
- Replace jet with a monotone-luminance map (viridis or cividis); add over/under colours to the colour bar and state the percentage of pixels outside −13 to 22 dB.
- Put (d) and (f) on a common y-axis, or, if the dynamic range forbids it, add a shared reference gridline and annotate the offset between panels.
- Add χ²-based confidence ribbons (or one representative error bar per estimator) to (d) and (f) using each estimator's ν, so the reader can see which inter-estimator differences exceed sampling variability.
- Add a small residual sub-panel to (f) plotting each smoothed estimator minus the multitaper estimate, replacing the occluded superposition with an explicit agreement plot; differentiate the orange and pink traces by dash pattern.
- If (h) supports any inferential statement, recompute uncertainty with a block bootstrap over non-overlapping time blocks (or an effective-n correction for the 50 % window overlap and the 2 Hz smoother) and report the signed median with an interval alongside the median absolute difference.
- Confirm and state that the spectrograms are rendered at 1:1 pixel mapping with no interpolation; document the half-window edge truncation and add "Time (s)" labels or an explicit shared-axis note to (c) and (e).
- Relabel the (b) x-axis as t − 318 s, or use absolute time 318–324 s to match (a).

### figure9.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a methodological/illustrative figure, not a group-statistics figure. (a) is a raw time trace, (b–g) are spectrograms (the correct display for time–frequency estimates), and (h) is a paired dot-and-line plot, which is the right choice (a bar chart here would have been inappropriate). No bar graphs, no pie charts, no error bars. Two presentation concerns, however: (i) the rainbow/jet-like colormap in (b–g) is perceptually non-uniform and will create apparent contour boundaries and false edges that are artefacts of the colour scale rather than of the PSD — a perceptually uniform map (viridis/cividis/magma) is preferable, especially since the paper's whole point is a subtle resolution comparison; (ii) the colour scale is clearly saturated (solid dark red below ~3 Hz in every panel and hard blue at the top of b), so dynamic range is being clipped at both ends without the colorbar indicating it.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible — n is effectively 1 (a single 40 s epoch, one channel, one subject) and this is inferable only from the caption, not the figure. That is acceptable for a demonstration figure, but the figure/caption should state explicitly that this is a single representative epoch from one recording and that no across-subject generalisation is claimed. Panel (h) shows all six computed values individually (3 window lengths × 2 methods), so "individual points shown" is satisfied there; there is no distribution to hide because these are deterministic, analytically/numerically determined quantities, not sampled measurements.

**COMPARISON:** Valid — The multitaper-vs-smoothing comparison is intrinsically paired (same data, same window length, matched target resolution R), and panel (h) correctly renders this pairing with connecting lines per row, and (b–g) are matched left/right at each window length on a single shared colour scale. This is good practice. Minor concerns: (1) the comparison at 1 s is acknowledged as an unequal-resolution comparison (1.1 vs 2.1 Hz, ν = 2.0 vs 4.7) — because neither bandwidth nor degrees of freedom are matched there, the visual difference between (b) and (c) conflates method with resolution, and the caption should say so even more plainly than it does; (2) the white "resolution box" is a good device but is easy to overlook — consider annotating one panel with an explicit label pointing to it in every row, or repeating the ν value inside the box.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present — Panel (h) x-axis begins at ≈0.40 Hz rather than 0, which inflates the apparent relative gap between the blue and orange markers (e.g. 0.8 vs 1.0 Hz looks like a large separation but is a 25 % difference; 1.1 vs 2.1 Hz is genuinely large). Since the headline claim is "at α = 2 the widths are much closer," a truncated axis risks exaggerating the residual differences. Also note the spectrogram frequency axes stop at ~24 Hz although the trace is filtered to 35 Hz — legitimate cropping, but it should be stated so readers do not assume the full analysed band is displayed. Panel (a) y-axis: the grey 11–16 Hz trace is drawn with an arbitrary vertical offset and no separate amplitude reference, so its amplitude cannot be read against the µV axis.

**OVERPLOTTING:** None — Panel (h) has six well-separated markers. In (b–g) the windows are stepped by 0.1 s while being 1–4 s long, i.e. 90–97.5 % overlap, so adjacent columns are near-duplicates; this is not "overplotting" in the scatter sense but it does render the images visually smoother and more finely time-resolved than the underlying estimate actually is. The resolution box partially addresses this, but the caption should state that the horizontal smoothness is a consequence of window overlap, not of genuine temporal resolution.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No p-values, significance brackets, or inferential annotations appear anywhere in the figure; nothing requires correction.

**TIME SERIES:** OK — 40 s of continuous, evenly sampled data with no gaps, no missing timepoints, and no interpolation across discontinuities. The five shaded spindle intervals in (a) align with the time axis of (b–g), which share the same 0–40 s range, so cross-panel reading is valid. One item to verify: the spectrograms appear to span the full 0–40 s even though centred windows of 1–4 s cannot be fully supported at the edges; the edge/padding convention (zero-padding, reflection, truncated window, or shifted window centres) should be stated, as edge columns are not comparable to interior columns.

**ISSUES:**
- [ADVISORY] Colour scale is saturated at both ends (solid dark red below ~3 Hz throughout; near-uniform deep blue at high frequency in b), so PSD differences in the delta band — which dominates N2 sleep — are entirely invisible and the low-frequency comparison between methods cannot be evaluated by the reader.
- [ADVISORY] Jet/rainbow colormap introduces perceptually artefactual banding and is not colour-vision-deficiency safe; apparent "structure" in the spindle band may partly reflect the colormap's non-uniform lightness gradient.
- [ADVISORY] Truncated x-axis in panel (h) (starting ≈0.40 Hz) visually exaggerates the multitaper-vs-smoothing gap, particularly for the 2 s and 4 s rows where the paper's claim is that the two methods nearly agree.
- [ADVISORY] n = 1 epoch/subject is stated only in the caption text; the figure carries no indication that this is a single representative example rather than a group-level result.
- [ADVISORY] 90–97.5 % window overlap makes the spectrograms look more finely time-resolved than they are; readers may over-interpret apparent sharp onsets/offsets of spindles.
- [ADVISORY] Grey 11–16 Hz trace in (a) has an unlabelled arbitrary offset and no amplitude reference, so spindle amplitude in the filtered band cannot be read.
- [ADVISORY] Edge handling for the first/last half-window of each spectrogram is unspecified; edge columns may not be comparable to interior columns.
- [ADVISORY] The 1 s comparison (b vs c) is not resolution- or ν-matched, so method and bandwidth are confounded in that row; this is acknowledged in the caption but is easy for a reader to miss when scanning the images.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Replace the rainbow colormap with a perceptually uniform one (viridis, cividis, or magma) and, if colour-deficiency safety is a priority, verify with a simulator.
- Extend the colour limits or add "extend both" arrowheads/triangles to the colorbar to make clipping explicit; alternatively add a second colour scale (or a separate row) optimised for the 0–4 Hz band so the delta-band comparison is legible.
- Extend the panel (h) x-axis to 0 Hz, or, if zooming is required for legibility, add an explicit axis break symbol and state in the caption that the axis is truncated.
- Add "single representative 40 s N2 epoch; one subject, channel C4-M1 (n = 1)" as visible text in panel (a) rather than relying on the caption alone.
- State the window step (0.1 s) and resulting overlap fraction directly in the panel titles or caption, with a sentence noting that horizontal smoothness reflects overlap, not temporal resolution.
- Add a vertical scale bar (in µV) for the grey filtered trace in (a), or plot it in a separate small subpanel with its own labelled axis.
- Specify the edge-handling/padding convention, and consider greying or hatching the first and last half-window of each spectrogram to mark columns affected by edge effects.
- In the row where the comparison is not resolution-matched (1 s), add a short in-panel note (e.g. "not ν-matched") so the confound is visible without reading the caption.
- Optionally, add a supplementary panel repeating (h) for several epochs or subjects to show that the achieved-width/ν relationships are stable, since the current figure demonstrates them on one epoch only.

## Cross-Figure Consistency Orchestrator

# FINAL CROSS-FIGURE SYNTHESIS
## 13-figure set (figure1–9, efigure1–4) — consistency audit

---

## BLOCKING ISSUES (must resolve before submission)

**1. Same colour, different condition — light blue.** Light/sky blue denotes "Multitaper, *L* = 6" in efigure2 but "Bohman, then box" in efigure3 and efigure4. These are adjacent supplementary figures likely read in sequence, so a reader who learns the mapping in one will misread the next. Pick one owner for sky blue (recommend Bohman, #56B4E9) and move *L* = 6 into the multitaper blue family distinguished by dash pattern.
— affects: [efigure2, efigure3, efigure4]

**2. Same condition, two names — the "untapered, then box" / "sliding sinc" estimator.** The identical estimator (identical green, identical ν = 17.2, identical 8.0/(*N*Δ) half-power width) is labelled "Sliding sinc window" in efigure1 and figure5, and "Untapered, then box" in efigure2, efigure3 and figure6. The paper's own thesis is that these are one estimator, but the figure set never says so at the point of use, so the shared colour looks like a palette error rather than a claim. Relabel as "Sliding sinc window (= untapered, then box)" in efigure1/figure5, or vice versa.
— affects: [efigure1, figure5, efigure2, efigure3, figure6]

**3. Grey carries four incompatible meanings across the set.** Grey = raw periodogram (figure2a, efigure2a, figure8d/f), = "Hann, then box" (efigure3, efigure4), = bias/noise decomposition curves (figure3b), and = annotation/leader/guide ink (nearly every figure). A reader trained by figure2 and efigure2 that grey means "periodogram" will misread efigure3 and efigure4. Reserve grey exclusively for the periodogram, move Hann-then-box to a distinct dark neutral with a distinct marker, and set all annotation ink to a lighter grey than any data series.
— affects: [figure2, figure3, figure8, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4]

**4. The kernel-in-dB panels are not mutually comparable.** The same quantity 𝒦(*f*) in dB is plotted with three different vertical floors — −80 dB (efigure1 m–p), −100 dB (efigure3b), −150 dB (figure2 e–h, figure6 e–h) — and on two different abscissae (*f*Δ centred at 0.4 in figure2/figure6 vs *fN*Δ centred at 0 in efigure1/efigure3, spans ±25 and ±24). The manuscript's central argument is a cross-method comparison of skirt levels, which the reader must therefore perform across four figures with three scales. Standardise one dB floor and one abscissa convention for all kernel panels; if a wider floor is needed in one figure, mark it.
— affects: [figure2, figure6, efigure1, efigure3]

**5. Two incompatible axis-frame conventions.** figure4 draws a full four-sided box on all four panels and figure3(b) draws a full frame (right spine carries nothing), while figures 1, 2, 5, 6, 7 and efigures 1–4 use open left+bottom spines. Gridline use is equally split: none anywhere in figure5, none in rows 1–3 of efigure1 but present in row 4, present throughout figures 1–3, 6, 7 and efigures 2–4. Adopt one spine and one gridline policy for the whole set.
— affects: [figure3, figure4, figure5, efigure1] (and by contrast all others)

**6. Rainbow (jet) colormap in the two image figures breaks both perceptual validity and set cohesion.** figure8(c,e,g) and figure9(b–g) encode an ordered scalar with a non-monotone-luminance rainbow, while the other eleven figures use a muted, luminance-ordered categorical palette. The cross-figure consequence is twofold: (i) the two image figures do not look like they belong to the same manuscript, and (ii) jet's saturated blue and red accidentally reproduce the manuscript's semantic "multitaper blue" and "Welch pink" inside panels where those hues carry no meaning, inviting a false link with the line traces in figure8(d,f) and figure9(h). Replace with cividis or viridis in both figures and re-stroke the white markers/resolution boxes for contrast against the new dark end.
— affects: [figure8, figure9]

**7. The entire set is uniformly below the raster specification, and must be re-exported in one coordinated pass.** Every figure is PNG at 256–303 DPI at its required placement width, with rasterized text. This was flagged per figure, but the cross-figure dimension matters: fixing figures piecemeal will produce a set with mixed vector/raster text, mixed glyph rendering and mixed effective type sizes. Re-export all 13 from source as vector PDF/EPS at declared final widths (88 mm or 181 mm), with the spectrograms in figure8/figure9 as the only embedded rasters (≥600 DPI) inside otherwise-vector artwork.
— affects: [all 13]

**8. No declared placement width, and equivalent elements will not survive typesetting at equal size.** Canvas widths range from 986 px (figure7) and 990 px (figure3) to 2092 px (figure4). Because no target width or DPI is specified anywhere, the two portrait single-column figures and the eleven landscape double-column figures may be rescaled independently, making equivalent elements differ by up to 2×. Compounding this, the smallest-text tier is already inconsistent across the set: ~6–6.5 pt (figure2 and figure6 inset ticks; efigure4c grey note; figure8h note; figure9b "resolution of the panel") against ~8 pt for the same semantic role in efigure2, efigure3 and figure1. Fix the figure canvas in physical units, set font sizes in points, and enforce a single minimum (≥7 pt) for every annotation in every figure.
— affects: [all 13, acutely figure2, figure3, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9, efigure4]

**9. No single uncertainty convention across the set.** Uncertainty is conveyed three different ways and never defined: (i) ν (equivalent degrees of freedom) is printed in-panel in figures 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9 and efigures 1–4 and is the manuscript's de facto variance currency, yet no caption defines it; (ii) figure7 is the only figure with a graphical dispersion element, a grey ±1.6 dB band that is 1 SD of one estimator applied to a plotted *difference* of two estimators; (iii) figures 6 and 7 additionally quote ensemble medians over 300 realizations in prose only. A reader cannot compare "noise" across figures. Define ν once, identically, in every caption that prints it; and if any band is drawn, use one stated definition (e.g. χ²-based interval for the plotted quantity) applied consistently.
— affects: [figure2, figure3, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9, efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4]

**10. Numeric inconsistency for the same estimator across figures.** figure1 reports ν = 17 for the all-taper/box estimate, while figures 5, 6, efigure1, efigure2 and efigure3 report ν = 17.2 for the same estimator; the cosine-taper-then-parabola leakage is 0.13 % in efigure1 but 0.12 % in figure2 and figure6. These are almost certainly *N*-dependent (256 vs 1024) rather than errors, but as printed they read as discrepancies. Quote consistent precision and state the *N* alongside every such number.
— affects: [figure1, figure2, figure5, figure6, efigure1, efigure3]

**11. Panel-label anchoring is not uniform.** The style is otherwise exemplary — lowercase parenthesised (a)…(p), ~10 pt, non-bold, upper-left — but figure8 places (a) and (b) *inside* the axes while (c)–(h) sit outside, and figure3 places (b) to the left of the axes at mid-height of the secondary axis while (a) sits above the top-left corner. Anchor all labels at one identical offset from the top-left corner of the axes in all 13 figures, and decide once whether they are bold (journal house style may require it — currently none are).
— affects: [figure3, figure8] (and the set-wide bold/non-bold decision)

---

## ADVISORY ISSUES (strongly recommended)

**1. Legend convention alternates, sometimes within a single figure.** Boxed in-axes legends appear in figure1(a), figure4(a–d), figure7(a,b), figure8(f), figure5(b,d), efigure1(b,d,e), efigure2(a), efigure3(a) and efigure4 (own quadrant); direct end-of-line labelling appears in efigure2(b), efigure3(b), figure1(b), figure6 (coloured titles) and figure9(h). efigure2 and efigure3 switch convention between panel (a) and panel (b) of the same figure. The end-label solution is demonstrably better here — adopt it wherever curves separate, and reserve boxed legends for the genuinely crowded cases (figure4c, efigure4).
— affects: [efigure2, efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure4, figure5, figure7, figure8]

**2. Marker fill means three different things.** In efigure4(c) filled = "no flat top, so this is a main-lobe skirt"; in figure3(b) filled circles vs open squares distinguish two scoring bands; in figure9(h) filled circles vs filled squares distinguish two methods; in figure1(c) and figure4(d) open circles are the only marker style. Reserve fill state for one semantic role across the set, or drop it as an encoding channel outside efigure4.
— affects: [figure1, figure3, figure4, figure9, efigure4]

**3. Marker sizes and line weights drift.** Markers run roughly 4 pt (figure4d), 5 pt (figure3b, efigure4), 6 pt (figure1c) and 7 pt (figure9h); the deliberately thick blue in figure1(a) is justified but should be the only intentional weight departure. Set one marker size and one data line width (with a documented exception list) in a shared plotting style file.
— affects: [figure1, figure3, figure4, figure9, efigure4]

**4. Method naming is not stable between artwork and captions.** In-figure text says "Multitaper, *L* = 7" while captions of figures 4, 5, 6, 7 say "Thomson's estimate"; "Cosine taper, then parabola" is called "recipe (b′)" in efigure3 and figure7. Worse, "recipe (b′)" collides typographically with panel label (b) in both of those figures. Use the in-figure name verbatim in every caption and rename the recipe to avoid the panel-label clash.
— affects: [figure4, figure5, figure6, figure7, efigure3]

**5. Mixed British/American orthography inside the artwork.** "centred" in efigure1(c) and figure5(c) annotations vs "centered" in figure2's caption; "colour" in captions vs "-ize" verb endings throughout. Pick one register for both artwork and captions.
— affects: [figure2, figure5, efigure1] (and all captions)

**6. Abbreviation definitions are figure-order dependent, so supplements are not self-contained.** PSD is expanded only in figure1's caption yet used in figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9 and efigure2; EEG is never expanded anywhere; ELROND, C4, C4-M1, N2 and AR(4) are used without expansion; *f*′ appears in the legends of efigure1(b) and figure5(b) and is defined nowhere. Supplementary captions must repeat every definition they rely on.
— affects: [figure1, figure7, figure8, figure9, efigure1, efigure2, figure3, figure5]

**7. Kernel dB axes lack their reference in the label.** Four figures label the ordinate only "𝒦(*f*) (dB)" with "relative to its peak" stated in the caption, while the PSD figures correctly carry "dB re 1 µV²/Hz" or "dB re σ²Δ". Add "(dB re peak)" in all four for consistency with the set's own good practice.
— affects: [figure2, figure6, efigure1, efigure3]

**8. Coloured panel titles are used in only one figure.** figure6 sets its panel descriptors in the series colour (an effective direct-labelling device); figure2 partly does the same; all other figures use black titles. Either extend the device or drop it.
— affects: [figure2, figure6]

**9. Colorbar placement differs between the two image figures.** figure8 parks a tall shared colorbar in the central gutter between the left and right panel columns; figure9 places it flush right of the panel block. Use the same relative position in both.
— affects: [figure8, figure9]

**10. Visual weight is unevenly distributed across the set.** figure8 (1.59 MB) and figure9 (1.79 MB) are dramatically denser and more saturated than the eleven line figures; efigure4 reads as less finished than its siblings because a quarter of its canvas is an empty quadrant holding a nine-entry legend; figure3 and figure7 are the only portrait single-column figures among eleven landscape double-column ones. None of this is fatal, but replacing jet (blocking item 6) and re-tiling efigure4's panels into the empty quadrant would substantially improve cohesion.
— affects: [figure3, figure7, figure8, figure9, efigure4]

**11. Lightest steps of the within-hue sequential ramps fall below usable contrast.** The *i* = 4 / *L* = 1 / *h*⁽⁷⁾ traces in efigure1(b,c,h), figure4(a,c) and figure5(b,c,d) sit close to white and will be lost in print or on a projector. Darken the lightest step to ≥40 % of the ramp range in all of them together, so the ramps remain mutually comparable.
— affects: [figure4, figure5, efigure1]

**12. Blue hue drifts between the sequential ramps and the categorical "multitaper" blue.** Pin the darkest step of the ramps in figure4, figure5(d), efigure1(d,h) and figure9(h) to the same hex as the categorical multitaper blue in efigure2, efigure3, figure6(b) and figure7(a).
— affects: [figure4, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure9, efigure1, efigure2, efigure3]

**13. Ordering and citation — two items to verify, no reordering obviously required.** (i) The narrative order is sound: figure1 (identity, graphical abstract) → figure2 (kernel motif) → figure3 (resolution choice, citing figure2's panels) → figure4 (why the kernel is a box) → figure5 (banks, correctly citing efigure1 as Fig. S1) → figure6 (estimators in practice) → figure7 (EEG comparison) → figure8 (seizure) → figure9 (spindles). However, figure1 *asserts* the three-way identity that figure4 derives and figure5(c) restates; if figure1 is the graphical-abstract figure this is acceptable, but it should be explicitly signposted as a forward reference so a reviewer does not read Figure 1 as an unsupported claim. (ii) Only efigure1 has a visible citation hook (from figure5's caption). Confirm that efigure2, efigure3 and efigure4 are each cited at their first relevant point in the main text — efigure2 belongs with figure6(b)'s 14 dB leakage result, and efigure3/efigure4 both belong with Table 2, so their S2/S3/S4 order should follow the order in which figure6 and Table 2 appear.
— affects: [figure1, figure4, figure5, figure6, efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4]

**14. Cross-figure redundancy worth resolving while re-exporting.** figure3(a) replots the record already shown in figure2, and figure3(b)'s three circled points are explicitly figure2's panels (b)–(d); efigure3 partly duplicates figure6(e–h)'s kernels at the same *R*; efigure1(h) duplicates efigure1(d) by construction. Consolidating these would tighten the set and free space for the fixes above.
— affects: [figure2, figure3, figure6, efigure1, efigure3]

## OVERALL ASSESSMENT

**Publication readiness: Major revisions**

For a 13-figure set this is unusually disciplined work: the semantic colour mapping (blue = multitaper, orange = cosine-then-parabola, green = untapered/sinc, pink = Welch, black = truth), the panel-label style, the sentence-case capitalisation, the shared axis limits within rows, and the method naming in the artwork are all more consistent than in most submissions, and the direct end-of-line labelling in efigure2(b), efigure3(b) and figure6 is exemplary. The problems are almost entirely mechanical rather than analytical — nothing here requires new computation — but they touch every figure and cannot be fixed piecemeal without introducing fresh inconsistency.

The three things to fix first, in order: (1) re-export all 13 figures as vector artwork from source in one coordinated pass at declared physical widths, which simultaneously resolves the resolution, rasterized-text and type-size-drift blockers; (2) replace the jet colormap in figure8 and figure9 and resolve the three colour-semantics collisions (light blue, grey, and the sliding-sinc/untapered-box naming), which are the only issues that can actively cause a reader to misidentify a condition; (3) unify the kernel dB floors and abscissae across figure2, figure6, efigure1 and efigure3, and adopt one axis-frame/gridline policy set-wide, so the cross-figure comparisons the manuscript's argument depends on can actually be made by eye.
